L322: ## **Observations and Results: Changes from Control**
L323: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	A total of 23 animals, 15M and 8F, died prematurely. All deaths at 20 mg/kg BID were considered to be selumetinib-related. The 20 mg/kg BID dose was not tolerated in the males and resulted in early deaths of 14 animals in Weeks 8 to 16 (main, recovery, and TK), and the premature sacrifice of the remaining males in this group after 15 weeks of treatment. The cause of death in the majority of these males was due to changes in the lower urinary tract. Premature death occurred in 1M and 6F at 20 mg/kg BID in Weeks 11 to 25 due to marked inflammatory changes in the large intestine.
Clinical Signs	Unscheduled death or termination: ↓ activity/unresponsiveness, piloerection, hunched posture, abnormal breathing, and pallor. Scheduled termination: Females at the 20 mg/kg BID - fast breathing and thin appearance. No treatment-related adverse clinical observations at other dose levels.
Body Weight	↓ Mean body weight gain (77% of control) in males at 20 mg/kg BID (Weeks 0 to 15). ↓ Mean body weight gain (56% of control) in surviving females at 20 mg/kg BID. ↓ Mean body weight gain (81% of control) in females at 5 mg/kg BID. No treatment-related effects on body weight gains in males and females at 1 mg/kg BID or males at 5 mg/kg BID.
Ophthalmoscopy	No selumetinib-related changes
Hematology	Mild to minimal transient ↑ in total white blood cell counts (neutrophil, eosinophil, monocyte, and large unstained cells) at all dose levels. Transient ↓ in hematocrit, hemoglobin associated with ↓ in mean cell hemoglobin, and mean cell volume at 20 mg/kg BID. ↑ in circulating red blood cells and ↑ in reticulocytes at 20 mg/kg BID.
Clinical Chemistry	At 20 mg/kg BID for 15 or 26 weeks ↓ in plasma albumin and A/G ratio (males & females); ↓ in plasma glucose (females); ↑ in plasma phosphorus, sodium, calcium (males), and changes in plasma urea (↑ males, ↓ females). Minor ↓ in phosphorus and albumin levels in males only at 5 mg/kg BID.
L324: **FDA Comment on Clinical Chemistry:** At the 20 mg/kg dose level, the increases in phosphorous were 25% in males and 24% in females at Week 13 (mid-study). In surviving females at the 20 mg/kg dose, phosphorous remained high at Week 27.
L325: Urinalysis	Slight changes in the group mean pH value (↓), urinary sodium (↑), and chloride (↑) concentrations at 20 mg/kg BID.
L326: **FDA Comment on Urinalysis:** In males at the 20 mg/kg high dose level, urinary sodium and chloride were increased 23% and 47%, respectively, at the early group termination timepoint of Week 16.
L327: Gross Pathology	
In all but one of 14M at 20 mg/kg BID euthanized prematurely (Weeks 8 to 16) or found dead - obstruction of the lower urinary tract, which was considered to be the major factor contributing to the poor clinical condition or death of these animals; 1M had caecal ulceration.
In 1M and 6F at 20 mg/kg BID prematurely killed/found dead (Weeks 11 to 25) - changes in the large intestine were considered to be the major factor contributing to the poor clinical condition and death.
At scheduled necropsy (Week 27):
Thickening of caecum and colon, enlargement of spleen and enlargement of mesenteric lymph node at 20 mg/kg BID.
Enlargement of the mesenteric lymph node at 1 and 5 mg/kg BID.

Organ Weight	↑ spleen weights in females at 20 mg/kg BID.
Histopathology	
Unscheduled termination at 20 mg/kg BID:
Males (Weeks 8 to 16) - swelling, congestion, and/or hemorrhage of corpus cavernosum of the bulbocavernosus muscle, hemorrhagic, and inflammatory cell infiltration in the urethra, obstruction of the penile urethra, and marked distension of the urinary bladder; urethral obstruction resulted in backflow pressure leading to secondary changes in the ureters (distended), seminal vesicles (edematous), prostate (edematous), and kidney (enlarged, pelvic dilatation, ruptured); inflammatory changes in colon and duodenum of 3M.
1M and 6F (Weeks 11 to 25) - erosive and inflammatory changes in the large intestine including colon and caecum, with occasional perforation and/or involving the peritoneum; secondary changes to the intestinal inflammation in the liver, spleen, bone marrow, and lymph nodes; multifocal mineralization in the livers in 2M.
Males (terminated after 15 weeks) with no signs of ill-health prior to sacrifice - findings in lower urinary tract (congestion of the corpus cavernosum of the bulbocavernosus muscle, inflammation/necrosis in the preputial glands, and marked urethral luminal dilatation in one animal) with no evidence of urethral obstruction or bladder distension; changes in the GI tract (inflammatory changes in the colon, caecum, rectum, and duodenum, sometimes associated with epithelial hyperplasia and/or mucosal ulceration and degeneration of the underlying muscular propria); pituitary gland (congestion), bone marrow (↑ granulopoiesis); spleen (↑ presence of granulocytes) and mesenteric lymph nodes (↑ level of plasmacytosis).
Scheduled termination:
20 mg/kg BID females - erosive and inflammatory changes in the GI tract, with secondary reactive changes in the lymphoreticular system (↑ EMH in spleen;
L328: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 213756
## Koselugo (selumetinib)
L329: ↑ plasmocytosis in lymph nodes) and inflammatory cell infiltration of the portal area in the liver.
5 mg/kg BID - minimal to slight inflammation with hyperplasia in the colon, caecum, and rectum; and minimal multifocal mineralization in the liver in 1M.
1 mg/kg BID - minimal inflammation with epithelial hyperplasia in the colon in 1M and 2F.
Recovery	Full or partial recovery for most changes at the end of the 13-week recovery period. Minimal changes were seen in individual animals in the liver (multifocal mineralization), colon (inflammation), caecum (inflammation), spleen (↑ spleen weight, with no histological correlate). Only partial recovery in blood chemistry changes; plasma urea and phosphorous remained high in previously treated males and albumin and cholesterol levels were low in females, with A/G ratios were reduced in both sexes.
Mating Performance and Fertility	No selumetinib-related effects on male fertility at any dose level.

L333: **FDA Assessment:** FDA confirmed the Applicant's presentation of the mouse toxicology data and in general agrees with the Applicant's assessment; however, in addition to the GI tract, the lower urinary tract was a major target organ in male mice. Levels of neutrophils increased over time, possibly correlating with increased gastric lesions, and were up to 7.5 times the levels of control animals in high dose females at the end of the 26-week dosing period. As the Cmax and AUC values are important for comparisons to clinical data and the Applicant did not include the toxicokinetic values in the PK section, that data is included here. Following repeated administration of AZD6244 to male and female mice, exposure (Cmax and AUC) increased with increasing doses in Weeks 1 and 16/26 (Table 8), with larger increases in exposure to AZD6244 in females than males.

L342: ## **Study Title/Number: 3-month Oral Toxicity Study in the Rat (528088)**
L343: ## **Key Study Findings:**
L344: - Skin was considered to be the primary target organ for toxicity in rats in this study. Skin scabs and lesions in rats were associated with clinical observations and dose-dependent increases in incidence and severity of microscopic ulcerations in males \ge5 mg/kg/day and females at 2.5 mg/kg/day.
- In males dosed with 10 mg/kg BID selumetinib hyd-sulfate, physis dysplasia was observed in the femoro-tibial joint. Increased hematopoiesis in the femoral bone marrow in female rats at 12.5 mg/kg/day was not associated with peripheral hematology changes and was most likely a response to the skin lesions.
L345: - Soft tissue mineralization was observed in a number of tissues and was associated with changes in plasma inorganic phosphate and albumin.

L381: ## **Study Title/Study Number: 26-week Oral Toxicity Study in the Cynomolgus Monkey (VKS/0210)**
L382: ## **Key Study Findings:**
L383: - Administration of selumetinib to monkeys for up to 26 weeks was associated with loose/liquid feces, seen at 1.5 mg/kg BID in females and 4 mg/kg BID in males. Diarrhea was occasionally associated with signs of dehydration and/or body weight loss.
- Selumetinib-related GI disturbances were not associated with any macroscopic or microscopic changes.
- The no-observed-adverse-effect-level (NOAEL) in males was considered to be 1.5 mg/kg BID, however, a NOAEL was not established in females due to the liquid feces and dehydration observed in one female on Week 18. The dose of 0.5 mg/kg BID was considered to be the NOEL.

L422: ## General Toxicology; Additional Studies
L423: ## 1-month Oral Toxicity Study in the Mouse Followed by a Recovery Period (0355AM)
L424: In the 1-month study, dosing of selumetinib hyd-sulfate to mice at a dose of 103 mg/kg BID (total daily dose of 206 mg/kg/day) was not tolerated and resulted in unscheduled termination of several males. As a result, the high dose (HD) was reduced to 69 mg/kg BID (total dose of 137 mg/kg/day) on Day 12 and all remaining animals at this dose survived to the scheduled termination. The cause of death in these males, dosed at 103 mg/kg BID, was associated with erosive and inflammatory findings in the GI tract. In surviving animals, dosing of selumetinib hyd-sulfate was associated with adverse clinical signs (\ge34 mg/kg BID) and serum chemistry (increases in plasma calcium and inorganic phosphate, and a decrease in albumin) changes (69 mg/kg BID). Microscopic evaluation showed dose dependent mineralization in various tissues (cornea, kidney, liver, myocardium, and skeletal muscle) at \ge11 mg/kg BID and erosive and inflammatory changes in the GI tract at 69 mg/kg BID. Additional microscopic findings in the 103/69 mg/kg BID group included bone marrow hypocellularity, increased EMH in spleen, and inflammatory cell infiltration of portal area in the liver. At the end of the 4-week recovery period, similar histopathological findings were still present but at a reduced incidence and/or severity compared to main test animals.
L425: ## Maximum Tolerated Dose and 28-day Oral Toxicity Study in the Cynomolgus Monkey Followed by a 28 Day Recovery Period (VKS/0153)
L426: This study consisted of an initial maximum tolerated dose (MTD) phase followed by a 1-month repeat dose phase. In the MTD phase, selumetinib hyd-sulfate was administered BID (12 hours apart) by oral gavage (0.5 mL/kg per occasion) to 2 male and 2 female cynomolgus monkeys at the following dose levels: 0.92 mg/kg BID (Days 1 to 7), 3 mg/kg BID (Days 9 to14), 7.5 mg/kg BID (Days 15 to 21) and 15 mg/kg BID (Days 22 to 35), which were equivalent to total doses of 2, 6, 15, or 30 mg/kg/day, respectively.
L427: Administration of selumetinib hyd-sulfate in MTD study at 15 mg/kg BID (30 mg/kg/day total) was not tolerated and resulted in abdominal discomfort, persistent loose/liquid/red stained feces, red anal discharge, dehydration and bodyweight loss, hematological changes in all animals and early termination of 1 male for humane reasons.
L428: For the 28-day repeat dose phase of the study, groups of 2 male and 2 female cynomolgus monkeys received selumetinib hyd-sulfate dose levels of 0, 1.5, 5 or 10 mg/kg BID (3, 10, and 20 mg/kg/day total dose). Recovery was assessed in a separate group at 5 mg/kg BID. The clinical signs observed after 14 days of dosing at 10 mg/kg BID indicated that this dose could not be tolerated for the remaining duration. Dosing in the HD group was stopped for 1 day and the dose level was reduced to 7.5 mg/kg BID (15 mg/kg/day total dose) from Day 16 to termination. Histopathological evaluation of tissues was only conducted in animals dosed for 1 month with 10/7.5 mg/kg BID selumetinib and concurrent controls.
L429: The predominant clinical findings seen at doses of \ge 5 mg/kg BID were loose feces, which progressed into liquid feces as treatment continued and at the HD level, was associated with signs of dehydration (i.e., slightly sunken eyes). Following reduction of the HD level from 10 to 7.5 mg/kg BID from Day 16, persistent loose/liquid feces, piloerection, ungroomed appearance was seen in some animals necessitating a brief cessation of treatment for some animals. Body weight and food consumption were unaffected, despite the severity of the diarrhea. Minimal changes in clinical pathology parameters were noted at \ge 5 mg/kg BID. There were no histopathological changes associated with dosing selumetinib at 10/7.5 mg/kg BID for 28 days. There was evidence of recovery from the gastrointestinal (liquid feces) and clinical pathology findings following a 28-day recovery period in animals.
L430: Dosing at 1.5 mg/kg BID did not produce any significant test article-related changes and was therefore considered to represent a NOAEL following oral administration for 1 month.
L431: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L432: The main target organs of toxicity in the repeat dose studies with selumetinib were skin, bone, and GI tract. Soft tissue mineralization was noted in a number of organs in rodents and was associated with increases in the plasma total phosphate and/or calcium and a decrease in albumin. With the exception of the soft tissue mineralization, all findings, in the repeat dose studies in mice and monkeys, showed evidence of reversibility following a recovery period.
L433: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L434: FDA agrees with the Applicant's overall assessment. See FDA's comments after each chronic study for details. Findings in the one-month studies were similar to those in the chronic studies with no additional target organs identified.

L454: ## **In Vivo Clastogenicity Assay in Rodent (Micronucleus Assay)**
L455: ## **Data:**
L456: ## **Study Title/Number: Micronucleus Test in Bone Marrow Cells of Mice (0416QM)**
L457: ## **Key Study Findings:**
L458: - Following oral administration for 2 days, selumetinib induced micronuclei in the bone marrow of CD-1 mice at doses \ge 121 mg/kg/day with a confirmed NOEL of 24 mg/kg/day.
L459: GLP compliance: Yes
L460: Test system: Male CD-1 mice; bone marrow micronuclei; 2 oral doses (24 hours apart) of 0, 24, 121 or 242 mg/kg/day.
L461: In this study, selumetinib hyd-sulfate was investigated for its potential to induce micronucleated polychromatic erythrocytes (PCEs) in mouse bone marrow. Groups of 7 male CD-1 mice received 2 oral doses (10 mL/kg) of 24 hours apart of 0, 24, 121 or 242 mg/kg/day selumetinib hyd-sulfate. A separate group of 3 mice was given a single oral dose of the positive control, cyclophosphamide (65 mg/kg). The animals were killed 24 hours after the last dose and femoral bone marrow samples collected. For each animal, the number of the micronucleated PCEs was counted in 2000 immature erythrocytes. As an estimate of bone marrow toxicity, the ratio of immature erythrocytes to mature erythrocytes was determined in 1000 cells.
L462: There was a significant increase in micronucleated PCEs in the bone marrow of mice at selumetinib doses of 121 or 242 mg/kg/day. A dose of 24 mg/kg/day did not induce an increase in micronuclei formation and was considered to be the NOEL. There was no evidence of bone marrow toxicity.
L463: ## **Other Genetic Toxicity Studies (For API only; does not refer to impurities)**
L464: ## **Data:**
L465: ## **Micronucleus Test in Bone Marrow Cells of Mice (0156QM)**
L466: In a GLP study, selumetinib free base given to groups of mice as single oral doses of 0, 500, 1000, and 2000 mg/kg produced a significant increase in micronuclei at 2000 mg/kg at 24 hours post dose. In a second study, selumetinib free base given to groups of mice as single oral doses of 0, 50, 160, 500, 1000, and 2000 mg/kg produced a biologically significant increase in micronucleated immature erythrocytes (MIE) at dose levels of 1000 mg/kg and above at 24 hours post dose. The results obtained following dosing of 500 mg/kg (1 positive and 1 negative for the induction of MIE) were equivocal. The NOEL for the induction of micronuclei in the bone marrow of mice is 160 mg/kg selumetinib free base.
L467: ## **Centromeric Labelling of Slides from the Mouse Micronucleus Test After a Single Oral Dose (0120KY)**
L468: In this non-GLP study, unstained slides from the mouse in vivo micronucleus study (0156QM) were fluorescently labelled with a mouse pan-centromeric DNA probe and examined for the presence or absence of a centromere signal. The presence of a centromere signal within a micronucleus indicates the presence of a whole chromosome, suggesting that the micronucleus had arisen via an aneugenic mechanism. The absence of a centromere signal within the micronucleus indicates a chromosome fragment, resulting from breakage.
L469: In mice given 500 mg/kg of selumetinib, all of the induced micronuclei were centromere positive. At doses of 1000 and 2000 mg/kg, 59% and 61% respectively, of the micronuclei were centromere positive. This suggests that the majority of the micronuclei formed are a consequence of chromosome loss, consistent with an aneugenic mode of action.
L470: It was concluded that the majority of the micronuclei induced in the bone marrow of mice given selumetinib contain centromeric signals and, therefore, that selumetinib has an aneugenic mode of action. The reduction in proportion of micronuclei containing centromeric material at the highest selumetinib doses is consistent with the hypothesis that aneugens induce other forms of chromosome aberrations at cytotoxic dose levels.
L471: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L472: Selumetinib showed no evidence of genotoxicity in vitro in bacterial or mammalian cells, but induced micronuclei in the bone marrow of mice in vivo, primarily as a consequence of aneugenicity.
L473: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L474: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment; however, the aneugenic findings in the in vivo studies all occurred at doses that were higher than the clinical dose. In the study using the hyd-sulfate (current clinical formulation) form of selumetinib the Applicant did not include a toxicokinetic assessment. There were no findings in the study using the hyd-sulfate formulation of selumetinib at the dose of 24 mg/kg. In the 26-week general toxicology study in mice, the high dose of 20 mg/kg resulted in a Cmax of 29.6 µg/mL in males, a concentration approximately 38 times the human Cmax at the 25 mg/m2 twice daily. In the genetic toxicology studies using the free-base formulation, Cmax plateaued at doses greater than the low dose of 50 mg/kg. Aneugenic findings occurred at a dose resulting in Cmax values that were 30-40x higher than the human dose, FDA did not consider selumetinib positive for genotoxicity for the purposes of recommendations on contraception.

L548: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L549: Selumetinib showed no evidence of carcinogenicity in 6-month CByB6F1/Tg rasH2 transgenic mouse and 2-year Han Wistar rat studies.
L550: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L551: After an independent review of the carcinogenicity studies including a statistical analysis of the findings, the results of the 6-month study in transgenic mice and the 2-year study in rats were presented to the Executive Carcinogenicity Assessment Committee. FDA's review of these data focused on the neoplastic findings. Exposures (AUC) in the carcinogenicity studies at the highest dose levels were at least 15 times higher than the clinical exposure at the 25 mg/m2 twice daily dose even at the lowest tested doses in both species. The Committee agreed that the Applicant's studies were adequately conducted and showed no evidence of selumetinib-mediated carcinogenic potential.

L590: ## The Applicant's Position:
L591: Selumetinib had no effect on fertility in male or female mice. Although mating performance and fertility were not affected, the number of live fetuses was slightly reduced; no effects were apparent on any parameter after 3 weeks treatment withdrawal.
L592: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L593: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. The reduction in live fetuses in female mice treated with selumetinib was a reduction of a single fetus at each dose level other than control. There was also the loss of a single pup in the recovery cohort of dams treated 21 days prior to mating at the 37.5 mg/kg dose level. As the losses were of low incidence and not dose-dependent, the data does not suggest a selumetinib-mediated decrease in female fertility.
L594: The Applicant conducted the male fertility assessment as a part of the 26-week general toxicology study in mice. While there were pre- or post-implantation losses in naïve female mice mated to male mice treated with selumetinib for 10 weeks, these losses did not exceed losses that occurred in females mated to control males and there no clear effects on fertility. Repeated treatment of male mice with AZD6244 at doses up to 5 mg/kg BID (~1.4 times the clinical exposure at 25 mg/m2 BID) for 26 weeks had no adverse effects on progressive motility or sperm numbers. The Applicant could not perform sperm analysis on males at the 20 mg/kg dose level due to the premature sacrifice of these animals by Week 16.
L595: ## Embryo-Fetal Development
L596: ## Data:
L597: ## **Study Title/Number: Dose Range Finding Embryofetal Development Study in the Mouse (0430RM)**
L598: A dose-range finding embryofetal development (EFD) study in mated female CD-1 mice (10/group) was conducted to establish dose levels for a pivotal EFD study. Selumetinib was given to mice by oral gavage at 0, 2.5, 12.5, 37.5, or 75 mg/kg BID (total doses of 0, 5, 25, 75, or 150 mg/kg/day) during the period of organogenesis (Days 6 to 16 of post coitum). Selumetinib dosing up to 75 mg/kg BID was well tolerated with no evidence of maternal toxicity but caused embryo lethality, reductions in mean fetal weights, fetal malformations including open eye,
L599: incomplete closure of the abdominal wall and/or cleft palate at \ge 12.5 mg/kg BID. The NOEL for embryo-fetal survival and development in this study was 2.5 mg/kg BID.
L600: ## **Study Title/Number: Embryofetal Development Study in the Mouse (0480TM)**
L601: ## **Key Study Findings:**
L602: - Administration of selumetinib to female mice during the period of major embryonic organogenesis resulted in open eye in all fetuses and an increased incidence of cleft palate of dams dosed with 37.5 mg/kg BID selumetinib.
- Open eye was observed in a number of fetuses from the 2.5 mg/kg BID and was considered to be associated with erroneous administration of 37.5 mg/kg BID on Day 16 post-coitum in the respective animals.
- The NOAEL for maternal toxicity and fetal abnormalities and variants was considered to be 2.5 mg/kg BID (unbound AUC(0-12) of 113.8 ng.h/mL; Study 0430RM). The animal-to-human AUC ratio for EFD findings for the recommended human dose of 25 mg/m2 is &gt;3.5. To provide the most conservative safety margins, the unbound AUC value was used in the calculation.
L603: Conducting laboratory and location: AstraZeneca R&amp;D, England
L604: GLP compliance: Yes
L605: ## **Methods**
L606: Dose and frequency of dosing: 0, 2.5 and 37.5 mg/kg BID (0, 5, and 75 mg/kg/day total dose level); given BID approximately 12 hours apart
L607: Route of administration: Oral (gavage)
L608: Formulation/vehicle: 30% w/v aqueous Captisol
L609: Species/strain: Alpk: APfCD-1 mouse
L610: Number/sex/group: 22 mated females/group
L611: Satellite groups: No-TK evaluation conducted in the DRF study (0430RM)
L612: Study design: Time mated female mice were dosed from GD 6 to GD 16, encompassing the period of major embryonic organogenesis
L613: Deviation from study protocol affecting interpretation of results: During the study an administration error occurred and 9 females in the 2.5 mg/kg BID group received the 37.5 mg/kg formulation at their first daily dose administration occasion. Three of these females had fetuses with open eyes within their litter. Each of the affected dams received the increased dose on Day 16 post-coitum which corresponds with period
L614: eyelid closure in mice (Day 15.5-16.5 post-coitum). None of the 12 correctly dosed females or the 5 incorrectly dosed females which were outside the window of sensitivity had fetuses with open eyelids.

L616: Parameters	Major Findings
Mortality	None
Clinical Signs	No treatment-related adverse effects recorded.
Body Weight	↓ Maternal body weight gain (4% - 6%) at 37.5 mg/kg BID from Day 11 to scheduled termination on GD 18.
Slight ↓ in maternal food consumption at 37.5 mg/kg BID from GD 12 to GD 18.
↓ Gravid uterus weight at 37.5 mg/kg BID.
Necropsy Findings
Cesarean Section Data	2.5 mg/kg BID: None
37.5 mg/kg BID: ↓ number of live fetuses; ↑ in post implantation loss, both in terms of early and late intrauterine deaths.
Necropsy Findings
Fetal/Offspring	2.5 mg/kg BID: No effect on litter or fetal weights; uni- or bilateral open eye in a total of 10 fetuses from 3 dams. Each of the affected dams received the high dose (37.5 mg/kg by error) on GD 16 which corresponds with period eyelid closure in mice (Day 15.5 to Day 16.5 post-coitum).
37.5 mg/kg BID: ↓ Mean litter weights and mean fetal weight.
Bilateral open eye in all fetuses; Cleft palate; ↑ incidence of folded retina and changes to the consistency of the lens in the eyes in a number of fetuses.
L617: BID Twice daily; GD Gestation Day.
L618: ↓: indicates reduction in parameters compared to control.
L619: ↑: indicates increase in parameters compared to control.
L620: ## The Applicant's Position:
L621: Given the demonstrated potential for embryo-fetal toxicity and fetal abnormalities with selumetinib in mice at clinically relevant plasma exposure, and the known fetal developmental abnormalities seen in nonclinical studies with other approved MEK inhibitors, a second EFD study in a non-rodent species was considered not to provide additional information which could enhance the risk assessment in patients. Based on the nonclinical data, administration of selumetinib can cause embryofetal toxicity and therefore appropriate measures should be implemented to avoid the risk to the developing fetus, in conjunction with applicable risk-benefit considerations.
L622: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L623: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment that the embryo-fetal development studies in rats were sufficient to demonstrate risks to a developing fetus. In general, while FDA does calculate free Cmax values for comparisons to in vitro IC50 data in pharmacology studies, FDA does not
L624: calculate unbound vs. bound Cmax and AUC values for exposure comparison to humans for drugs with similar levels of protein binding in humans vs. animals, resulting in slight discrepancies in exposure margins between the Applicant and the FDA. The dose-range finding study for selumetinib in pregnant mice included two additional dose levels compared to the GLP-compliant study: 12.5 and 75 mg/kg twice daily. At the 12.5 mg/kg twice daily dose level the selmetinib exposure in dams was approximately 33 times the human exposure at the 25 mg/m2 twice daily dose. Findings at the 12.5 mg/kg dose level included increased post-implantation loss and cleft palate; open eyes occurred in fetuses from all litters at this dose level. While the Applicant attributes the open eye incidence at the dose of 2.5 mg/kg in Study #0480TM to dosing error (the dams having been administered a higher dose), in the pre- and postnatal development study (Study 20082140), there were findings of prematurely open eyes at all dose levels, including the low dose of 0.5 mg/kg (~0.6 times the human exposure at the 25 mg/m2 twice daily clinical dose), suggesting that defects in eyelids may occur at even lower doses.
L625: ## Prenatal and Postnatal Development
L626: ## Data:
L627: ## **Study Title/Number: Perinatal and Postnatal Development Study in the Mouse (20082138)**
L628: A dose-range finding pre and postnatal development (PPND) study was conducted in time mated female CD-1 mice (8/group) to establish dose levels for a pivotal PPND study. Selumetinib was given to mice by oral gavage at 0, 5, 12.5, 25, 40, or 75 mg/kg BID (total doses of 0, 10, 25, 50, 80, or 150 mg/kg/day) on GD 6 to Lactation Day 15. Another group were dosed at 75 mg/kg BID (150 mg/kg/day) from GD 15 to Lactation Day 15. Surviving F0 generation female mice and their litters were killed on Lactation Day 15. Administration of selumetinib at \ge 40 mg/kg BID was associated with clinical signs (including piloerection, hunched posture and tremors) animals which exhibited clinical signs did not deliver a litter. Lower maternal body weight gain was observed at \ge 5 mg/kg BID. Embryofetal death including total litter loss occurred resulting in low gestation index at 25 and 40 mg/kg BID (dosed from GD 6) and 75 mg/kg BID (dosed from GD 16), no live offspring were born in the 75 mg/kg BID (dosed from GD6). Decreased offspring viability was noted at \ge 25 mg/kg BID. Decreases in pup weight were noted at \ge 12.5 mg/kg BID. Selumetinib-related adverse clinical signs noted in the F1 generation pups during the postpartum period were generally associated with the decreased viability at \ge 12.5 mg/kg BID. Other changes observed in the offspring attributed to selumetinib included lenticular opacities, open eyes, cleft palate or inward rotation of the hindlimbs at \ge 12.5 mg/kg BID.
L629: ## **Study Title/Number: Perinatal and Postnatal Development Study in the Mouse (20082140)**
L630: ## **Key Study Findings:**
L631: - There were no selumetinib related effects on maternal reproductive function, and the reproductive NOAEL is considered to be 7.5 mg/kg BID (total dose of 15 mg/kg/day).
- A developmental NOAEL could not be established based on the low incidence of open eyes noted at the lowest selumetinib dose of 0.5 mg/kg BID (1 mg/kg/day).
- Malformations occurred at a mean maternal plasma concentration (unbound C_{max}) 0.4-fold the clinical exposure at the recommended clinical dose of 25 mg/m^2. To provide the most conservative safety margin, the mean unbound C_{max} was used in this calculation.

L649: Study design:
L650: F0 generation mice were dosed on GD 6 through Lactation Day 20 (mice that delivered a litter) or GD 22 (mice that did not deliver a litter). F1 generation pups were not directly given the test item or the control item formulations but may have been exposed in utero exposure or via maternal milk during the lactation period. On postnatal Day (PND) 4, F1 litters were reduced to 8 pups/litter. On PND 21, F1 pups were weaned and the F0 generation females were killed. On at least PND 90, mice were paired within each dose group (1:1 male to female, non-siblings). Surviving F1 generation male mice were killed after the completion of the cohabitation period (PND 113 to 123) and F1 generation female mice were killed on GD 13. L652: ## Observations and Results
L653: Generation	Major Findings
F0 Dams	
One F0 generation female in the 0.5 mg/kg BID dose group was euthanized on Lactation Day 9 due to adverse clinical signs (hunched posture, body weight loss). The cause of death could not be established but was considered unrelated to selumetinib as it occurred in the low dose group. In addition, one F0 generation female in the 2 mg/kg BID was euthanized on Lactation Day 12 due to a suspected gavage error.
There were no adverse maternal clinical signs or effects on maternal body weights in F0 generation females.
No noteworthy findings were reported on natural delivery parameters or litter observations. There were no selumetinib-related maternal macroscopic findings at any dose level.

F1 Generation	
Decreases in preweaning pup weight were noted at 7.5 mg/kg BID from PND 7 (up to 92% of controls). Body weights remained lower in the F1 generation animals during the post-weaning period. At termination, decreases in the absolute weight of the paired epididymides and absolute and relative weights of the paired testes noted at 7.5 mg/kg BID and were considered to be secondary to the reduced terminal body weights.
A selumetinib-related increase in prematurely open eyes was observed at all dose levels (2, 4, and 13 pups from 1, 2, and 6 litters in 0.5, 2, and 7.5 mg/kg BID dose groups, respectively).
A low incidence of cleft palate was observed at 2 and 7.5 mg/kg BID (1 and 2 pups each from a single litter/dose level, respectively). Similar findings (open eyes) were noted during the first day of the reflex and physical development evaluations (PND 13). At the 7.5 mg/kg BID dose level, there was a decrease in the number of pups that met the criterion for pupil constriction on PND 21.
L654: BID Twice daily; PND Postnatal day.
L655: ## The Applicant's Position:
L656: The peri- and postnatal development toxicity studies demonstrated the potential for selumetinib to adversely affect embryofetal survival and development if administered to patients that are pregnant or breast feeding. Therefore, appropriate measures should be in place to avoid risk to developing fetus or breast-fed children.
L657: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L658: FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. FDA has included Table 13 and Table 14 showing the concentration of AZD6244 in dams (F0 generation) and pups (F1 generation). Prematurely open eyes were present even in pups from dams treated with 0.5 mg/kg AZD6244 where exposure after 2 hours was about 0.6x clinical exposure. The inclusion of pharmacokinetic data in pups also shows that there was transfer of selumetinib in milk.

L665: ## 5.5.5. Other Toxicology Studies
L666: ## Data:
L667: ## Genotoxicity Studies with Metabolite:
L668: - N-desmethyl selumetinib, a pharmacologically active but minor metabolite, was not mutagenic in the presence or absence of metabolic activation system (\pmrat S9).
L669: ## Bacterial Reverse Mutation Test (0750BV)
L670: N-desmethyl selumetinib showed no evidence of mutagenic potential in *Salmonella typhimurium* strains TA98, TA100, TA1535, and TA1537 and *Escherichia coli* strain WP2 uvrA, when tested at concentrations up to 5000 \mu\text{g} per plate, in the presence or absence of rat liver derived metabolic activation system (\pmrat S9).
L671: ## Mouse Lymphoma Assay (0751MV)
L672: In a mouse lymphoma L5178Y tk locus assay, N-desmethyl selumetinib showed no evidence of mutagenic potential, in the presence or absence of metabolic activation (\pmrat S9), when tested up to concentrations determined by toxicity and/or solubility in tissue culture medium (maximum concentration tested was 167 \mug/mL).
L673: ## Phototoxicity
L674: - Selumetinib did not demonstrate a risk for phototoxicity at the highest recommended concentration of 100 \mug/mL.
L675: Selumetinib absorbs light in the ultraviolet (UV) range and showed enhanced cytotoxicity in the presence of UV light, in an in vitro 3T3 neutral red uptake phototoxicity test at 316 and 1000 \mug/mL concentrations which are 3- and 10-fold above the current International Conference on Harmonization (ICH) S10 2013 recommended maximum concentration of 100 \mug/mL. The positive phototoxicity result was seen at concentrations significantly greater (&gt;8000x) than the free clinical C_{max} achieved at the maximum intended clinical dose of 25 mg/m2 BID. The concentrations for the in vitro phototoxicity evaluation with selumetinib were selected based on the OECD 2004 432 evaluation criteria which was subsequently reduced from 1000 to 100 \mug/mL due to a high percentage of positive results. Additionally, selumetinib demonstrated low binding affinity for melanin in rat whole body autoradiograph. Therefore, selumetinib is not considered to demonstrate a risk for phototoxicity based on the available nonclinical information.
L676: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L677: While selumetinib did demonstrate a clear phototoxic potential at concentrations \ge 316 \mug/mL, FDA agrees that considering the updated advice under ICH S10 (2015) regarding maximum concentrations for testing (100 \mug/mL) and that the IC_{50} for neutral red uptake in the presence of selumetinib in the presence of irradiation was &gt; 100 \mug/mL, that selumetinib did not demonstrate clear phototoxic potential at clinically relevant concentrations.
L678: ## Applicant's Position:
L679: Overall, the nonclinical toxicology profile of selumetinib is considered to be acceptable for the proposed indication.
L680: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L681: FDA agrees that no additional nonclinical studies are warranted to support the use of selumetinib in the proposed patient population.

L685: ## 6 Clinical Pharmacology
L686: ## 6.1. Executive Summary
L687: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L688: Selumetinib is an inhibitor of mitogen-activated protein kinase/extracellular signal-regulated kinases 1 and 2 (MEK1/2) for the treatment of pediatric patients with neurofibromatosis type 1 (NF1) who have symptomatic, inoperable plexiform neurofibromas (PN). The proposed dosing regimen is 25 mg/m2 orally twice daily (BID) on an empty stomach (fasted 2 hours before and 1 hour after each dose).
L689: The Clinical Pharmacology section of the NDA is supported by single and multiple dose pharmacokinetics (PK) characterization, population PK and exposure-response analyses, absolute and relative bioavailability (BA), mass balance, food effect, hepatic impairment, renal impairment, race/ethnicity, QT, drug-drug interaction in vivo studies, in vitro studies, and physiologically-based PK (PBPK) modeling.
L690: The proposed dosing regimen of 25 mg/m2 BID is acceptable from a clinical pharmacology perspective. The key review questions focused on the appropriateness of the proposed dosing regimen in patients with body surface area (BSA) 0.55 - 0.69 m2, dose recommendations for patients with moderate (b) (4) hepatic impairment and those requiring coadministration of strong and moderate CYP3A4 modulators, and administration with regards to food to potentially alleviate gastrointestinal toxicities.
L691: ## Recommendations
L692: This NDA is approvable from a clinical pharmacology perspective, provided the Applicant and the FDA reach an agreement regarding the labeling language. The key review issues with specific recommendations/comments are summarized below.
L693: Review Issues	Recommendations and Comments
Pivotal evidence of effectiveness	An open-label, single arm trial (SPRINT Phase 2 Stratum 1) of selumetinib in pediatric patients with NF1 who have symptomatic inoperable PN provided primary evidence of effectiveness.
General Dosing instructions	The recommended dosage of selumetinib is 25 mg/m2 orally twice daily without food; minimum BSA of 0.55 m2 and capped at 50 mg when BSA is ≥1.9 m2.
L694: Doses should be rounded to nearest achievable dose with 10 and 25 mg strength capsules.

Avoid concomitant use with strong or moderate CYP3A4 inducers.
The recommended dosage for use in patients with severe hepatic impairment (Child Pugh C) has not been established. Reduce dose to 20 mg/m2 in patients with moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh B). No dose adjustment is recommended for patients with mild hepatic impairment.
No dose adjustment is recommended for patients with mild to severe renal impairment and patients with ESRD.
L695: No dose adjustment is recommended based on race (White, Asian, Black) or sex.

Labeling	The labeling reflects dosing recommendations for hepatic impairment, drug-drug interaction (DDI), and patients with BSA 0.55-0.69 m2. The review team also had content and formatting change recommendations according to the guidance of clinical pharmacology section of labeling for human prescription drug and biological products - content and format (published December 2016).
Bridge between the to-be-marketed and clinical trial formulations	Free base suspension, hydra-sulfate capsule, and granule formulations were evaluated in the clinical development program of selumetinib. In this NDA submission, the to-be-marketed formulation, i.e., hydra-sulfate capsule, was used for relevant clinical pharmacology studies and for clinical studies in the pediatric patient population. Relative bioavailability studies provided adequate PK bridging between the formulations and the to-be-marketed formulation.
L696: The following postmarketing requirement (PMR) is issued to the Applicant to study the PK and safety of selumetinib administered with food in pediatric patients:
L697: - Submit the final report and datasets from a pharmacokinetic trial in pediatric patients to confirm the effect of a low-fat meal on selumetinib exposure with the marketed capsule formulation and evaluate whether administration of selumetinib with food may alleviate gastrointestinal toxicities. Confirm appropriate dosing recommendation of selumetinib with a low-fat meal that maintains efficacy with acceptable safety.
L698: ## 6.2. Summary of Clinical Pharmacology Assessment
L699: ## 6.2.1. Pharmacology and Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L700: ## **Data:**
L701: The clinical pharmacology of selumetinib has been well characterized in adult subjects, adult oncology patients and pediatric patients.
L702: The PK of selumetinib in pediatric patients (aged 3 to 18 years old at initiation/enrollment) with NF1 and inoperable PN have been characterized following single and multiple dosing based on body surface area (BSA). There has been an extensive clinical pharmacology program (mass balance, food effect, hepatic and renal impairment, drug-drug interaction [DDI] studies with CYP3A4 strong inhibitor and inducer and CYP2C19 inhibitor, and ethnicity) as well as population PK analysis to characterize the PK of selumetinib in adults and pediatric patients which support
L703: the proposed posology in pediatric patients and appropriate dosing recommendations. Furthermore, PK data from healthy subjects after single dosing, and in adult oncology patients with advanced solid malignancy after both single and multiple doses of selumetinib and the outcomes of the exposure efficacy and exposure safety analysis conducted in NF1 pediatric patients was used to support the recommended dose of 25 mg/m2 selumetinib, administered orally BID on a continuous schedule.
L704: Pharmacological activity is primarily due to selumetinib and although 2 metabolites of selumetinib were identified to have pharmacological activity, the N desmethyl metabolite is ~3 to 5 fold more potent than parent (circulates at 5% to 7% of parent) and may contribute to some activity while the amide metabolite is ~50-fold less potent in vitro (circulates at ~2% of parent) and is unlikely to do so.
L705: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L706: The clinical pharmacology studies support the recommended dose of 25 mg/m2 selumetinib, administered orally BID on a continuous schedule for the treatment of pediatric patients with NF1 and inoperable PN. The PK of selumetinib is similar in adult subjects, adult patients and pediatric patients allowing for the use of results from adult populations for dose guidance in pediatric populations.
L707: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L708: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's characterization of selumetinib PK in pediatric patients. The PK of selumetinib appears to be similar in adult and pediatric patients.
L709: The FDA recommends a lower age cutoff i.e., 2 years of age and older for pediatric patients with NF1 and inoperable PN. Based on in vitro studies, selumetinib is primarily metabolized by CYP enzymes (56%, mainly by CYP3A4) with a lesser contribution by UGT enzymes (29%, UGT1A1 and UGT1A3), all of which have greater than 80% adult enzyme activity/maturation by 2 years of age.
L710: ## 6.2.2. General Dosing and Therapeutic Individualization
L711: ## 6.2.2.1. General Dosing
L712: ## **Data:**
L713: The Phase I portion of the SPRINT study initially evaluated 2 doses of selumetinib, 20 and 30 mg/m2 in pediatric patients with NF1 and inoperable PN to identify a suitable dose for Phase II. Initially, the lower dose of 20 mg/m2 was considered appropriate for long term treatment, however after systematic review of emerging evidence from another study (Banerjee et al 2017; Pediatric Brain Tumor Consortium study which evaluated 25, 33 and 43 mg/m2 BID; 25 mg/m2 was selected as the recommended Phase II dose in that study as
L714: 33 and 43 mg/m2 were not tolerated), an intermediate dose of 25 mg/m2 was introduced into the SPRINT Phase I study which was determined to be safe and tolerable with potential for better efficacy and hence, was selected as the Phase II dose. This 25 mg/m2 was defined as the MTD in both the SPRINT and Pediatric Brain Tumor Consortium pediatric studies.
L715: The results of the exposure-response analysis for both efficacy and safety did not indicate a clear relationship between selumetinib exposure and response within the exposure range investigated in the SPRINT study. Using the primary NCI central analysis, no exposure-efficacy relationship was observed, although a slight trend towards a higher exposure leading to higher efficacy in the independent central reviewer analysis was noted. The median concentrations of responding patients were marginally higher than non-responders, although there was significant overlap in individual concentrations between patients in both groups. This suggests that higher exposures might be more beneficial, however an increase in dose to 30 mg/m2 is not appropriate based on the long-term safety data from the SPRINT Phase I study (2/6 patients had DLT at 30 mg/m2 dose; numerically more adverse events (AEs) at this dose group compared to 20 and 25 mg/m2 dose). Importantly, the 25 mg/m2 dose ensures that patients will receive a clinically active dose regardless of inter-patient variability and allows prescribers to reduce the dose should this be necessary.
L716: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L717: Selumetinib dose of 25 mg/m2 BID was identified as the recommended dose based on a review of all available safety, tolerability, pharmacokinetic, and efficacy data in the Phase I component of the SPRINT study. The exposure-response analyses confirmed a lack of relationship between selumetinib exposure and various efficacy or safety endpoints in the SPRINT study, indicating that the 25 mg/m2 BID regimen produced robust and consistent therapeutic effects across the exposure range observed.
L718: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L719: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's proposed dosing regimen of selumetinib 25 mg/m2 BID, which is supported by safety, and efficacy findings in the indicated patient population, and PK-PD relationships in pediatric and adult patients:
L720: - In SPRINT Phase I study, 25 mg/m2 BID was determined to be the MTD and selected as the RP2D based on the following:ORR was similar across 20 to 30 mg/m2 BID doses with best ORR observed at 25 mg/m2 BID, i.e., 66.7% (8/12) at 20 mg/m2, 83.3% (5/6) at 25 mg/m2, 50.0% (3/6) at 30 mg/m2.
- Tolerability was similar between 20 and 25 mg/m2 BID doses based on DLT rates: 2/12 at 20 mg/m2, 1/6 at 25 mg/m2, 2/6 at 30 mg/m2.
- In SPRINT Phase II trial, a favorable efficacy/safety profile for selumetinib 25 mg/m2 BID was demonstrated. Exposure-response assessment demonstrated a positive trend for AUCss-efficacy (ORR) association and no clear ER relationship for safety (i.e., all grades
L721: TEAEs, Diarrhea, Nausea, vomiting) in pediatric patients. The exposure-response assessment should be interpreted with caution because of the limitation of the study design (e.g., a single dose level pivotal trial with a small number of subjects and a high dose interruption rate).
L722: (3) Study in adult patients demonstrated a direct relationship between inhibition of ERK phosphorylation (substrate of MEK) and selumetinib exposure, suggesting that higher exposure contributes to greater target engagement.
L723: ## 6.2.2.2. Therapeutic Individualization
L724: ## **Data:**
L725: BSA was identified as a covariate impacting selumetinib CL and volume of distribution. BSA had more than 20% impact on CL and hence AUC (at the extremes of the observed BSA distribution). This supports BSA-based dosing in pediatrics. The posology of BSA based dosing is shown in Table 15.
L726: The age range in the population PK analysis was wide and included individuals from the age 3 to 79 years old. The population PK analysis indicated that age has small but not a clinically relevant impact on selumetinib exposure. Age and BSA are correlated in pediatric patients and within the age range of 3 to 18 years, due to BSA based dosing, the difference in selumetinib exposure is small and unlikely to be of clinical relevance.

L732: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L733: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment regarding the proposed dosing regimen for patients with BSA ≥ 0.70 m2 and that for age, gender, renal impairment, and mild to severe hepatic impairment. L735: The detailed dosing schema by BSA is based on the availability of the capsule strengths, 10 mg and 25 mg. In the Applicant proposed dosing schema of selumetinib 25 mg/m2 BID (Table 2), the proposed dose for BSA 0.55-0.69 m2 is
L736: The mean simulated AUC0-12h with the proposed as compared to those of the other BSA subgroups.
L737: **Figure 4 Applicant's Simulated steady state AUC0-12h across BSA bands based on Applicant's proposed dosing schema of selumetinib 25 mg/m2 BID. Source: Population PK report.**
L738: Based on the findings above, the FDA recommends a **daily dose of 30 mg for BSA 0.55-0.69 m2, i.e., 20 mg morning dose and 10 mg evening dose**. This recommended dosing regimen is supported by the following rationale:
L739: - The 20 mg/10 mg dose could achieve a daily dose of 30 mg with a corresponding daily dose level of 43.5-54.6 mg/m2, which is similar to that of other BSA groups.
- Selumetinib exhibits dose proportionality at the dose range tested. The 20 mg/10 mg dose is expected to achieve similar AUC as compared to that of other BSA groups.
- In the SPRINT Phase I study, 2 patients with BSA 0.67 m2 received selumetinib 20 mg BID with acceptable tolerability. The safety of recommended morning dose of 20 mg is therefore supported.

L747: ## 2. Dose adjustment for patients with hepatic impairment
L748: Based on the dedicated hepatic impairment study, the FDA agrees with the Applicant for a dose reduction to 20 mg/m2 BID for patients with moderate hepatic impairment, and the recommended dosage cannot be established for patients with severe hepatic impairment. See Section 6.3.2 below for details.
L749: ## 6.2.2.3. Outstanding Issues
L750: A comprehensive clinical pharmacology package has been conducted which has characterized the PK of selumetinib and provided appropriate dosing guidance for intrinsic/extrinsic factors, special populations and DDI.
L751: ## The Applicant's Position:
L752: Not applicable.
L753: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L754: The FDA assessed that there is an outstanding issue of taking selumetinib on an empty stomach (fasted 2 hours before and 1 hour after each dose) due to the following reasons:
L755: - It poses difficulty for pediatric patients to take selumetinib twice daily on an empty stomach (total of 6-hour fasting time window) and may lead to compliance issues;
- The food effect studies showed a greater magnitude of exposure change when selumetinib was administered with a low-fat meal as compared to a high-fat meal, which is unusual;
- The incidence of gastrointestinal toxicities and dose interruptions due to gastrointestinal toxicities were high in pediatric patients;
- The safety profile with regards gastrointestinal toxicities is not similar in adult patients and cannot be extrapolated to pediatric patients.
L756: The Applicant is issued a PMR to conduct a trial to evaluate whether administration of selumetinib (as the to-be-marketed capsule formulation) with food may alleviate gastrointestinal toxicities in adolescent patients and also to confirm an appropriate dosing recommendation of selumetinib with a low-fat meal.
L757: ## 6.3. Comprehensive Clinical Pharmacology Review
L758: ## 6.3.1. General Pharmacology and Pharmacokinetic Characteristics
L759: ## **Data:**
L760: The PK of selumetinib are comparable between healthy adult subjects, adult patients with advanced solid malignancies and pediatric patients with NF1 and inoperable PN aged 3 to 18 years old at the time of enrollment at the investigated dose range. The observations and conclusions from the clinical pharmacology studies performed in healthy adult subjects and adult patients are considered of direct relevance for pediatric patients. An overview of the ADME properties, clinical PK, and DDI potential of selumetinib is provided below.
L761: **Absorption:** Selumetinib is a Biopharmaceutics Classification System Class IV (low solubility, low permeability) compound; however, extensive in vitro and in vivo assessments have been carried out and confirms that exposure from selumetinib capsules are not dissolution or permeability limited. Across healthy subjects and adult patients with cancer, absorption was rapid with C_{max} achieved at a median t_{max} of 1 to 1.5 hours after dosing following single oral doses of 25 to 75 mg dose of selumetinib. In pediatric patients after a single dose (SPRINT study), median t_{max} following oral dosing was 1 hour across the dose range (20 to 30 mg/m^2) demonstrating similarity in the rate of absorption in pediatric, adult patients, and healthy subjects.
L762: Absolute oral bioavailability of a single dose of selumetinib in healthy adult subjects was 62% (90% confidence interval [CI]: 60.1%, 64.1%).
L763: **Distribution:** The geometric mean volume of distribution (V_{ss}) determined following selumetinib intravenous administration (Study 80) was 59.8 L (range 41.7 to 87.5 L). Apparent volume of distribution (V_{ss}/F) across the dose range studied in adult patients (25 to 100 mg) was similar to that observed in healthy adult subjects (89 to 126 L and 116 to 170 L, respectively), indicating selumetinib is moderately distributed into tissues in adults. In pediatric patients, similar to that observed in adult patients and healthy adult subjects, selumetinib appeared to be moderately distributed with geometric mean apparent volume of distribution (Vz/F) values of 78 to 171 L in the dose range of 20 to 30 mg/m^2.
L764: Selumetinib was shown to be highly bound to human plasma proteins with a mean percentage binding of 98.4% in plasma from male human subjects.
L765: **Metabolism:** The major metabolic pathways for selumetinib involve N-demethylation, glucuronidation and oxidation. In healthy adult subjects, selumetinib was the major circulating drug-related component and accounted for ~40% of the plasma radioactivity. In addition, other circulating metabolites in plasma were identified as a glucuronide of the imadazoindazole
L766: metabolite (22%), a selumetinib glucuronide (7%), and N-desmethyl carboxylic acid (3.6%). The amide metabolite (AZ12791138) was quantified in human plasma to be less than 2% and is 50-fold less potent than parent and is therefore unlikely to significantly contribute to biological activity. The N-desmethyl metabolite of selumetinib (AZ12442942) is approximately 3- to 5-fold more potent in vitro than selumetinib and was quantified as ~3% of dose in human plasma (AUC ratio metabolite to parent is~7% across clinical studies).
L767: **Excretion:** The recovery of radioactivity in excreta in healthy adult subjects was collected for a total of 9 days. Over this period the total radioactivity recovered was 93% with approximately 59% excreted in feces (~19% as unchanged selumetinib) and approximately 33% recovered in urine (&lt;1% as unchanged selumetinib).
L768: The major component present in human urine was the glucuronide of imidazoindazole metabolite (M2) and accounted for ~10% of the radiochemical dose. Other metabolites present at &gt;3% of the dose in the urine include the 2 selumetinib glucuronides M7 and M4 at 6.0% and 3.8%, respectively, and the N-desmethyl glucuronide (M3) at 3.4%. Unchanged selumetinib was a minor component of urine, accounting for ≤1% of the dose in all subjects.
L769: The major component in feces was unchanged selumetinib and accounted for a mean of ~19% of the dose in the feces. Seven metabolites accounted for between 1% and 10% of the dose in feces: the predominant metabolites being the primary amide metabolite (M14 – 8.7%), the N-desmethyl amide (M12 – 6.5%) and the N-desmethyl metabolite (M8 – 6.1%). Remaining metabolites were &lt;3% of the dose.
L770: **Clinical Pharmacokinetics:** Selumetinib was rapidly absorbed after single and repeated oral BID dosing with a median t_{max} of 1 hour across the 20 to 30 mg/m^2 groups in pediatric patients. C_{max} and AUC increased in an approximately dose proportional manner in pediatric patients, at 20 to 30 mg/m^2, and in adult patients between 25 to 100 mg doses. Selumetinib had an apparent oral CL of 8.8 to 15.0 L/hour, mean apparent volume of distribution of 78 to 171 L, and the terminal half-life ranged between 6.2 to 9.4 hours in pediatric patients across the 20 to 30 mg/m^2 dose range. Minimal accumulation was observed on chronic BID dosing at steady state (accumulation ratio ~1.1). The observed within-subject variability (coefficient of variation [CV%]) for selumetinib AUC and C_{max} was 17% and 34%, respectively. The observed intersubject variability (CV%) for selumetinib AUC and C_{max} was 24% and 26%, respectively.
L771: Pharmacological activity is primarily due to selumetinib. Two metabolites of selumetinib were identified to have pharmacological activity of which N-desmethyl metabolite is ~3- to 5-fold more potent than selumetinib and may contribute to safety and efficacy while the amide metabolite is ~50-fold less potent in vitro and is unlikely to contribute to safety and efficacy. The apparent elimination half-life of N-desmethyl metabolite is similar to that of parent and is
L772: unlikely to accumulate. Based on 5% to 7% of the circulating levels, the N-desmethyl metabolite may contribute 21% to 35% of the activity of selumetinib.
L773: **Food effect:** In adult patients and healthy adult subjects, consumption of a high fat meal reduces the rate of absorption (\sim50% decrease in C_{max}; t_{max} delayed by 2 hours) and the extent of absorption (AUC decreases by 16% to 19%). In another study, consumption of a low-fat meal reduces the rate of absorption (65% decrease in C_{max}; t_{max} delayed by 2.5 hours) and the extent of absorption (AUC decreases by 38%) in adult healthy subjects. In the SPRINT study, pediatric patients were administered selumetinib in the fasted state (fasting for 2 hours before and 1 hour after dosing).
L774: **Gastric acid modifying agents:** Selumetinib capsule 25 mg dissolution is pH independent in which similar rates and extent of release is observed across the physiological pH range. Therefore, it is expected that drugs which modify the gastric pH (H2-receptor antagonists and proton pump inhibitors [PPIs]) present a low risk of impacting dissolution of selumetinib capsules and consequently affecting in vivo exposure. Hence, selumetinib can be co-administered with gastric pH modifying agents without any dose modifications.
L775: **Renal impairment:** Minor changes in selumetinib PK (28% and 16% reduction in AUC and C_{max} in subjects with ESRD, respectively) were observed compared to subjects with normal renal function. Selumetinib can be dosed to patients with mild, moderate, severe renal impairment or ESRD patients without dose adjustment. Dialysis has limited effect on systemic selumetinib so is unlikely to be beneficial to treat selumetinib overdose.
L776: **Hepatic impairment:** Selumetinib exposure was slightly lower (14%) in the mild hepatic impairment group compared to the normal group. For subjects with either moderate or severe hepatic impairment group, total exposure (AUC) was higher compared to the normal group, by 59% and 57%, respectively. The increases in unbound AUC were higher, 41% for moderate impairment and 317% for severe impairment. In pediatric patients, no dose adjustment is required in mild hepatically impaired subjects. For patients with moderate hepatic impairment, a starting dose of 20 mg/m^2 is recommended. Selumetinib is not recommended for use in patients with severe hepatic impairment.
L777: **Ethnicity:** Dose normalized AUC and C_{max} in healthy adult subjects of Asian ethnicity were slightly higher compared to Western subjects. However, these apparent differences were lower when AUC and C_{max} were corrected for body weight. Therefore, it is anticipated that the observed PK ethnic differences of selumetinib are likely not to be of clinical relevance, particularly as pediatric patients will follow a BSA adjusted dosing regimen of selumetinib.
L778: **Victim DDI:** Selumetinib is a substrate of P-glycoprotein (P-gp) and breast cancer resistance protein (BCRP) but clinically relevant interactions are unlikely. Selumetinib is metabolized
L779: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 213756
## Koselugo (selumetinib)
L780: primarily by CYP3A4 and to a minor extent by several other CYPs and UGTs including CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP1A2, CYP2E1, UGT1A1, and UGT1A3. In clinical studies, selumetinib exposure increased by ~50% when co-administered with the strong CYP3A4 inhibitor itraconazole or the strong CYP2C19 and moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor fluconazole. It is recommended to avoid co-administration of selumetinib and strong CYP3A4/CYP2C19 inhibitors. However, if coadministration is necessary and unavoidable, caution and monitoring may be required when co-administering selumetinib with strong CYP3A4 or CYP2C19 inhibitors. The exposure to selumetinib in healthy adult subjects was reduced by 51% when co-administered with the strong CYP3A4 inducer, rifampicin. Administration of selumetinib with strong CYP3A4 inducers should be avoided if possible.
L781: Based on physiologically-based PK (PBPK) analysis, moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors can increase selumetinib exposure by 30% to 40% and hence, cautious use and monitoring is recommended. Moderate CYP3A4 inducer can decrease selumetinib exposure by 40% and should be avoided, if possible. Weak CYP3A4 inhibitors and inducers are unlikely to impact selumetinib exposure.
L782: **Perpetrator DDI:** Based on in vitro data, selumetinib has low potential to cause clinically significant drug interactions via inhibition of CYP1A2, CYP2A6, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, CYP3A4, and CYP2E1 or induction of CYP1A2, 2B6, and 3A4 enzymes or transporters (BCRP, P-gp, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT2, OAT1, MATE1, and MATE2K). A clinically relevant effect on the PK of concomitantly administered substrates of OAT3 cannot be excluded.
L783: **QTc:** Based on a clinical study, selumetinib does not prolong the QTc interval in healthy adult subjects and does not have any adverse effect on cardiac repolarization.
L784: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L785: The current clinical pharmacology package provides characterization of the key PK properties of selumetinib and when combined with in vitro drug metabolism and PK profiling data provides sufficient data in support of the selected dose and adequate labelling for special populations and DDI. From a clinical PK perspective, these data support a BID 25 mg/m2 dose for these pediatric patients with NF1 and inoperable PN.
L786: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L787: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's summary of the general pharmacology and pharmacokinetics of selumetinib; however, the FDA has different dosing recommendations for DDI. See Section 6.3.2 below for details. The bioanalytical methods are summarized in the OCP Appendices.
L788: ## 6.3.2. Clinical Pharmacology Questions
L789: ## 6.3.2.1 Does the clinical pharmacology program provide supportive evidence of effectiveness?
L790: ## **Data:**
L791: Yes. The results of the exposure-response analysis for both efficacy and safety did not indicate a clear relationship between selumetinib exposure and response within the exposure range investigated in the SPRINT study using the BSA based dosing regimen. At the recommended daily dose of 25 mg/m2 BID, a positive benefit/risk profile that maximizes clinical activity in patients, while simultaneously minimizing the incidence and severity of adverse reactions as well as dose modifications was noted. The starting dose of 25 mg/m2 BID also allows for a potential for dose reduction to 20 mg/m2 in case of tolerability issues which has also shown clinical activity. Importantly, the 25 mg/m2 ensures that patients will receive a clinically active dose regardless of inter-patient variability and allows prescribers to reduce the dose should this be necessary. Appropriate dose adjustment guidance is provided in the proposed labelling.
L792: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L793: The proposed dosing regimen of 25 mg/m2 BID based on BSA is suitable for pediatric patients with NF1 who have symptomatic, inoperable PN.
L794: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L795: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's exposure-response analyses to provide supportive evidence of effectiveness.
L796: ## 6.3.2.2 Is the proposed dosing regimen appropriate for the general patient population for which the indication is being sought?
L797: ## **Data:**
L798: Yes. BSA was identified as a covariate impacting selumetinib clearance and volume of distribution. The proposed BSA based dosing in pediatric patients is appropriate as this is shown to be effective and generally well tolerated with no difference between gender. Simulation of 1000 patients at each band with their corresponding median and 75% prediction interval at the 25 mg/m2 dose indicates that the BSA based dosing is predicted to provide similar exposure across bands and ensures that all pediatric patients will be dosed in the range of the recommended dose of 25 mg/m2.
L799: Adverse events (AEs) were transient and was effectively managed with concomitant medications or dose modifications as needed. The number of patients with dose modifications (interruptions or reductions) was low (&lt;25%) indicating that the dose of 25 mg/m2 BID is generally well tolerated and appropriate for this patient population.
L800: ## The Applicant's Position:
L801: The BSA based dosing regimen of 25 mg/m2 BID is appropriate for pediatric patients with NF1 and inoperable PN.
L802: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L803: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's proposed dosing regimen of selumetinib 25 mg/m2 BID for the proposed indication in pediatric patients. The FDA recommends dosing regimen of 20 mg morning dose and 10 mg evening dose for patients with BSA 0.55-0.69 m2. See Section 6.2.2.2.
L804: ## 6.3.2.3 Is an alternative dosing regimen or management strategy required for subpopulations based on intrinsic patient factors?
L805: ## Data:
L806: **Hepatic impairment:** Selumetinib exposure was slightly lower (14%) in the mild hepatic impairment group compared to the normal group. For subjects with either moderate or severe hepatic impairment group, total exposure (AUC) was higher compared to the normal group, by 59% and 57%, respectively. The increases in unbound AUC were higher, 41% for moderate impairment and 317% for severe impairment. In pediatric patients, no dose adjustment is required in mild hepatically impaired subjects. For patients with moderate hepatic impairment, starting dose of 20 mg/m2 is recommended. Selumetinib is not recommended for use in patients with severe hepatic impairment.
L807: **Renal impairment:** Minor changes in selumetinib PK (28% and 16% reduction in AUC and Cmax in subjects with ESRD, respectively) were observed compared to subjects with normal renal function. Selumetinib can be dosed to patients with mild, moderate, severe renal impairment or ESRD patients without dose adjustment. Dialysis has limited effect on systemic selumetinib so is unlikely to be beneficial to treat selumetinib overdose.
L808: **Ethnicity:** Dose normalized AUC and Cmax in healthy adult subjects of Asian ethnicity were slightly higher compared to Western subjects. However, these apparent differences were lower when AUC and Cmax were corrected for body weight. Therefore, it is anticipated that the observed PK ethnic differences of selumetinib are likely not to be of clinical relevance, particularly as pediatric patients will follow a BSA adjusted dosing regimen of selumetinib.
L809: ## The Applicant's Position:
L810: Based on the assessment of intrinsic factors, dose adjustment is required for moderate hepatic impairment (Child Pugh B) and is not recommended for patients with severe hepatic impairment (Child Pugh C). For renal impairment and other demographics (age, gender, and ethnicity), no dose adjustment is required.
L811: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L812: Given that the N-desmethyl metabolite of selumetinib has 3-5 fold greater potency in vitro than selumetinib, FDA considered six molar-normalized exposure matrices in addition to selumetinib AUC and C_{max}: total moiety AUC, total moiety C_{max}, potency-adjusted total moiety AUC, potency-adjusted total moiety C_{max}, potency-adjusted unbound moiety AUC (adjusted free total moiety), and potency-adjusted unbound moiety C_{max} (adjusted free total moiety). Results were similar across all exposure matrices; therefore, dose adjustments for intrinsic patient factors are based on selumetinib exposures.
L813: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that selumetinib is not recommended for use in patients with severe hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh C). Based on results from the hepatic impairment study that showed a 3.17-fold (90% CI: 2.24, 4.50) increase in unbound AUC in subjects with severe hepatic impairment as compared to subjects with normal hepatic function, a dose reduction to 10 mg/m2 BID based on exposure matching would be recommended. However, considering the limited available dose strengths of selumetinib 10 and 25 mg capsules, a recommended dosing nomogram cannot be established. Furthermore, the potential for pediatric patients with NF1 to have severe hepatic impairment is low. Therefore, the FDA agrees that no dose recommendation could be made for patients with severe hepatic impairment at this stage. Should any alternative dosage form be available in the future, the FDA may re-evaluate the feasibility of dosing recommendations for patients with severe hepatic impairment.
L814: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's dosing recommendation to reduce the dose of selumetinib to 20 mg/m2 BID in patients with moderate hepatic impairment (Child-Pugh B) based on results from the hepatic impairment study that showed a 41% increase in unbound AUC in subjects with moderate hepatic impairment as compared to subjects with normal hepatic function. See DDI section below (Table 17) for the dosing regimen of 20 mg/m2 BID for different BSA subgroups.
L815: The FDA agrees with the Applicant that no dose adjustment is needed for mild hepatic impairment, renal impairment, sex, and race/ethnicity based on the data provided.
L816: ## **6.3.2.4 Are there clinically relevant food-drug or DDIs, and what is the appropriate management strategy?**
L817: ## **Data:**
L818: The clinically relevant food-drug and DDIs with concomitant medications are described in further detail below with their respective recommendation for management.
L819: **Food effect:** In adult patients and healthy adult subjects, consumption of a high fat meal reduces the rate of absorption (~50% decrease in C_{max}; t_{max} delayed by 2 hours) and the extent of absorption (AUC decreases by 16% to 19%). In another study, consumption of a low-fat meal
L820: ## Koselugo (selumetinib)
L821: reduces the rate of absorption (65% decrease in C_{max}; t_{max} delayed by 2.5 hours) and the extent of absorption (AUC decreases by 38%) in adult healthy subjects. In the SPRINT study, pediatric patients were administered selumetinib in the fasted state (fasting for 2 hours before and 1 hour after dosing).
L822: **Gastric pH:** Selumetinib capsule 25 mg dissolution is pH independent in which similar rates and extent of release is observed across the physiological pH range. Therefore, it is expected that drugs which modify the gastric pH (H2-receptor antagonists and PPIs) present a low risk of impacting dissolution of selumetinib capsules and consequently affecting in vivo exposure. Hence, selumetinib can be co-administered with gastric pH modifying agents without any dose modifications.
L823: **Victim DDI:** Selumetinib is a substrate of P-gp and BCRP but clinically relevant interactions are unlikely. Selumetinib is metabolized primarily by CYP3A4 and to a minor extent by several other CYPs and UGTs including CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP1A2, CYP2E1, UGT1A1, and UGT1A3. In clinical studies, selumetinib exposure increased by ~50% when co-administered with the strong CYP3A4 inhibitor itraconazole or the strong CYP2C19 and moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor fluconazole. It is recommended to avoid co-administration of selumetinib and strong CYP3A4/CYP2C19 inhibitors. However, if coadministration is necessary and unavoidable, caution and monitoring may be required when co-administering selumetinib with strong CYP3A4 or CYP2C19 inhibitors. The exposure to selumetinib in healthy adult subjects was reduced by 51% when co-administered with the strong CYP3A4 inducer, rifampicin. Administration of selumetinib with strong CYP3A4 inducers should be avoided if possible.
L824: Based on PBPK analysis, moderate CYP3A4 inhibitors can increase selumetinib exposure by 30% to 40% and hence, cautious use and monitoring is recommended. Moderate CYP3A4 inducer can decrease selumetinib exposure by 40% and should be avoided, if possible. Weak CYP3A4 inhibitors and inducers is unlikely to impact selumetinib exposure.
L825: **Perpetrator DDI:** Based on in vitro data, selumetinib has low potential to cause clinically significant drug interactions via inhibition of CYP1A2, CYP2A6, CYP2B6, CYP2C8, CYP2C9, CYP2C19, CYP2D6, CYP3A4, and CYP2E1 or induction of CYP1A2, 2B6 and 3A4 enzymes or transporters (BCRP, P-gp, OATP1B1, OATP1B3, OCT2, OAT1, MATE1, and MATE2K). A clinically relevant effect on the PK of concomitantly administered substrates of OAT3 cannot be excluded.
L826: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L827: Based on in vitro and clinical studies, appropriate management strategy for food, gastric pH, and DDI is proposed.
L828: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L829: Given that the N-desmethyl metabolite of selumetinib has 3-5 fold greater potency in vitro than selumetinib, FDA considered six molar-normalized exposure matrices in addition to selumetinib AUC and C_{max}: total moiety AUC, total moiety C_{max}, potency-adjusted total moiety AUC, potency-adjusted total moiety C_{max}, potency-adjusted unbound moiety AUC (adjusted free total moiety), and potency-adjusted unbound moiety C_{max} (adjusted free total moiety). Results were similar across all exposure matrices; therefore, dose adjustments for extrinsic patient factors are based on selumetinib exposures.
L830: The FDA has concerns with the Applicant's recommendation with regards to food. The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's dosing recommendations with regards to DDI.
L831: ## (1) Dosing recommendation for selumetinib with regards to food
L832: In Phase 1 and Phase 2 of the SPRINT study, selumetinib was taken in the fasted state. The FDA agrees with the Applicant's proposal to take selumetinib on an empty stomach based on currently available data that showed a 38% decrease in AUC (90% CI: 30%, 45%) when taking selumetinib with a low-fat meal as compared to fasted state, and exposure-response analyses that suggests decreased exposure may lead to decreased efficacy and target engagement. Although a high-fat meal showed minimal effect on selumetinib AUC, it is not feasible to recommend taking selumetinib with a high-fat meal. Given limitations of currently available data, the FDA recommends collecting additional PK and safety data via a PMR study to determine an appropriate dosing regimen of selumetinib with food that may alleviate gastrointestinal toxicities in pediatric patients. See Outstanding Issues.
L833: ## (2) Dosing recommendation with regards to DDI
L834: In the PBPK modelling, it was predicted that selumetinib AUC increased by ~40% when co-administered with a moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor, erythromycin, and reduced by ~40% when co-administered with a moderate CYP3A4 inducer, efavirenz. See Section 19.4.
L835: Strong or Moderate CYP3A4 Inhibitors or Fluconazole: The FDA disagrees with the Applicant's recommendation to avoid coadministration of strong CYP3A4/CYP2C19 inhibitors (b) (4)
L836: Based on results from the DDI study with itraconazole (strong CYP3A4 inhibitor) that showed a 49% (90% CI: 40, 59) increase in selumetinib AUC, the FDA recommends dose reduction by approximately 30%:
L837: - If patient is currently taking 25 mg/m2 BID, dose reduce to 20 mg/m2 BID
- If patient is currently taking 20 mg/m2 BID, dose reduce to 15 mg/m2 BID
L838: **Table 17 Dosing Schema for Selumetinib 20 mg/m2 Twice Daily and 15 mg/m2 Twice Daily**
L839: Body Surface Area	If the current dosage is 25 mg/m2 twice daily, reduce to 20 mg/m2 twice daily (mg/dose)	If the current dosage is 20 mg/m2 twice daily, reduce to 15 mg/m2 twice daily (mg/dose)
Morning	Evening	Morning	Evening
0.55 – 0.69 m2	10	10	10 mg once a day
0.70 – 0.89 m2	20	10	10	10

L840: Based on results from the DDI study with fluconazole (strong CYP2C19 inhibitor/moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor) that showed a 53% (90% CI: 44, 63) increase in selumetinib AUC, FDA recommends similar dose reductions as those for coadministration with strong CYP3A4 inhibitors. Based on PBPK modeling that showed concomitant use of erythromycin (moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor) is predicted to increase selumetinib AUC by ~40%, FDA recommends similar dose reductions as those for coadministration with strong CYP3A4 inhibitors.
L841: **Strong and moderate CYP3A4 inducers:** Given both selumetinib and the active metabolite N-desmethyl selumetinib are metabolized by CYP3A4 and both strong and moderate CYP3A4 inducers were observed and predicted to reduce selumetinib AUC by ~40%-50%, the FDA agrees with the Applicant that strong and moderate CYP3A4 inducers should be avoided when administering selumetinib to avoid potential loss of efficacy due to decreased drug exposure. An increase in selumetinib dose by exposure matching is not recommended due to the unknown effect of selumetinib solubility on its PK at higher doses and limited information on the safety of the N-desmethyl metabolite.
L842: **Acid reducing agents:** The FDA agrees that selumetinib does not exhibit pH-dependent solubility based on the in vitro dissolution testing on selumetinib hydra-sulfate capsule at pH 1.2, 4.5, 6.5. Therefore, a DDI study with an acid reducing agent does not need to be conducted.
L843: **Transporters:** The FDA agrees that selumetinib is a substrate of P-gp and BCRP, but clinically relevant interactions are unlikely.
L844: **Effect of Selumetinib on Other Drugs:** FDA agrees that selumetinib and its active N-desmethyl metabolite do not inhibit or induce CYP enzymes, and do not inhibit transporters to a clinically relevant extent based on in vitro studies.
