L871: ## 8 Statistical and Clinical Evaluation
L872: ## 8.1. Review of Relevant Individual Trials Used to Support Efficacy
L873: ## 8.1.1. SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1
L874: ## Trial Design
L875: ## The Applicant's Description:
L876: SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, is an open-label, single-arm, multi-centre study of selumetinib in children with NF1 and inoperable PN, with PN-related morbidity at enrolment. Pediatric oncologists with extensive experience in performing investigational drug studies in children conducted this study. For SPRINT Phase II, patients were enrolled on 1 of 2 strata based on whether they already had PN-related morbidity (Stratum 1) or had no significant clinical morbidity but the potential for significant clinical PN-related morbidity (Stratum 2) at the time of enrolment. At the time of data cut-off (DCO) for this application, Stratum 2 is insufficiently mature and therefore not included in this submission.
L877: Fifty patients aged \ge 2 and \le 18 years with NF1 and inoperable PN and PN-related morbidity present at enrolment were to receive selumetinib 25 mg/m^2 BID. Patients were to have a Karnofsky performance level of \ge 70\% (for patients aged &gt; 16 years) or a Lansky performance of \ge 70\% (for patients aged \le 16 years old). Patients were enrolled irrespective of their PN progression status at baseline (progressive PN were defined as those having \ge 20\% increase in PN volume within 12 to 15 months prior to enrolment). Clinically relevant PN-related morbidity at baseline was determined by the investigator at the time of study entry. Patients must have had a BSA of \ge 0.55 m^2, and the ability to swallow whole capsules. For patients who either had progressive PN at baseline or if they did not have progressive PN at baseline and did respond, then they continued on treatment as long as they were deriving clinical benefit or until they met at least 1 of the off-study criteria. If the patient did not have progressive PN at baseline nor had achieved a response with 2 years, then treatment was discontinued.
L878: Patients were to be followed up for 5 years after completing selumetinib, or for a total duration of 7 years, whichever was longer.
L879: ## Trial Location
L880: The study was sponsored by Cancer Therapy Evaluation Programme (CTEP) and conducted by the NCI POB (co-ordinating site) at 4 sites in the US.
L881: ## External Control Data
L882: ## NCI POB NF1 Natural History Study
L883: The Natural History study is a prospective study in patients with NF1, which is sponsored and conducted by the NCI POB. It enrolls both pediatric and adult patients aged \le 35 years old with a clinical diagnosis of NF1 or a confirmed NF1 mutation, patients are not required to have PN-related morbidity at enrolment.
L884: Patients had serial evaluations of their PN yearly until age 18 and thereafter at least every 3 years. Only 1 PN (most clinically-relevant “typical” PN) per patient was selected for analysis and only the volumetric magnetic resonance imaging (MRI) data of the same PN in each patient, which had been followed up for the longest period of time, not affected by surgery or a MEK inhibitor, was selected to be included in the Natural History analysis. If a patient received treatment with a MEK inhibitor (including selumetinib) during the Natural History study, data collected during the MEK inhibitor treatment period have been excluded from the analysis of Natural History study data.
L885: The PN volume data from patients with at least 2 volumetric MRIs in this study whose baseline volumetric MRI assessments occurred aged \ge 3 to \le 18 years (known as the age-matched population) were analysed by AstraZeneca.
L886: ## Placebo Arm of Tipifarnib Study 01-C-0222
L887: Study 01-C-0222 was sponsored by CTEP and coordinated by the NCI POB. This was a multi-centre, double-blinded, placebo-controlled, randomized, cross-over study of tipifarnib (R115777) in children and young adults (\ge 3 and \le 25 years) with a clinical diagnosis of NF1 and unresectable, progressive PN with the potential to cause significant morbidity (progressive \ge 20\% increase in PN volume, or \ge 13\% increase in 2-dimensional, or \ge 6\% increase in 1-dimensional measurement over last 2 consecutive volumetric MRI scans or within approximately 1 year prior to trial evaluation). Patients were randomized to receive tipifarnib (200 \text{ mg/m}^2 orally every 12 hours) or placebo (Phase A) and crossed over to the opposite treatment arm at the time of PN progression (Phase B). In each 28-day treatment cycle, study treatment was administered for 21 days followed by a 7-day rest period.
L888: PN growth during the study was monitored using volumetric MRI. Volumetric MRI of up to 3 most clinically relevant target PN was performed prior to the start of Cycles 1, 4, 7 and 10 and then after every 6 cycles (1 cycle=28 days). All volumetric MRIs were sent to the NCI POB for central analysis with review of the scans being performed by the same single expert reader as in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1. A volumetric increase of \ge 20\% in at least 1 PN compared with baseline was defined as progressive disease.
L889: The placebo arm from Phase A (first period of cross-over; N=29) of the study was used as an external control for patients from SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 for time to progression (TTP) and
L890: progression-free survival (PFS) endpoints. Patients in the placebo arm of Phase A ranged in age from \ge 3 to \le 18 years.
L891: All the volumetric MRIs in both the NCI POB NF1 Natural History study and in the placebo arm of the tipifarnib Study 01-C-0222 were evaluated by the same single expert reader as in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1. PN progression was defined as a \ge 20\% increase in PN volume compared with baseline.
L892: ## **Diagnostic Criteria:**
L893: Children with NF1 and inoperable PN, with PN-related morbidity at enrolment as determined by the NCI POB. Refer to study design section above.
L894: ## **Eligibility:**
L895: ## *Key Inclusion Criteria*
L896: For inclusion in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, patients had to fulfil all of the following criteria (per Protocol Amendment N):
L897: - Age: \ge 2 and \le 18 years at the time of enrolment to SPRINT Phase II. Body surface area \ge 0.55 \text{ m}^2 and ability to swallow whole capsules (assessed at screening using an empty capsule shell). The age criteria were chosen because early childhood and puberty are considered to be the greatest risk for disease progression, and selumetinib may provide the most benefit to this young group of patients.
- Diagnosis: patients with NF1 and inoperable PN, defined as PN that could not be surgically completely removed without risk for substantial morbidity due to encasement of, or close proximity to, vital structures, invasiveness or high vascularity of the PN. The PN had to cause significant morbidity, such as (but not limited to) head and neck PN that could have compromised the airway or great vessels, paraspinal PN that could have caused myelopathy, brachial or lumbar plexus PN that could have caused nerve compression and loss of function, PN that could have resulted in major deformity (e.g., orbital PN) or were significantly disfiguring, PN of the extremity that could have caused limb hypertrophy or loss of function, and painful PN.
- Measurable disease: patients must have had at least 1 measurable PN, defined as a PN of at least 3 cm measured in 1 dimension. Patients who had undergone surgery for resection of a PN were eligible, provided the PN had been incompletely resected and was measurable. Measurability and suitability for volumetric MRI analysis of the target PN had to be confirmed with the NCI POB prior to enrolment. The target PN was defined as the clinically most relevant PN, which had to be amenable to volumetric MRI analysis.
L898: - Prior therapy: patients with NF1 were only eligible if complete PN resection was not considered to be feasible without substantial risk or morbidity.Patients who had received previous investigational agents or biologic therapy, such as tipifarnib, pirfenidone, Peg-Intron, sorafenib, imatinib, or other targeted therapies were eligible for enrolment. At least 4 weeks must have elapsed since receiving medical therapy directed at the PN. Patients who received prior medical therapy for their PN must have recovered from the acute toxic effects of all prior therapy to Grade \le 1 Common Terminology Criteria for Adverse Events (CTCAE) v4 before entering this study.
- Performance status: patients &gt;16 years of age must have had a Karnofsky performance level of \ge 70\%, and children \le 16 years old must have had a Lansky performance of \ge 70\%.
- Cardiac function: normal ejection fraction (echocardiogram [ECHO] or cardiac MRI) \ge 53\% (or the institutional normal; if a range was given then the upper value of the range was used).
- Informed consent.
L899: ## *Key Exclusion Criteria*
L900: Any of the following was regarded as a criterion for exclusion from SPRINT Phase II:
L901: - Pregnant or breast-feeding females were excluded due to potential risks of fetal and teratogenic AEs of an investigational agent.
- Inability to swallow capsules, since capsules cannot be crushed or broken.
- Prior treatment with selumetinib or another specific MEK1/2 inhibitor (unless the patient met criteria for re-treatment).
L902: ## **Dose Selection:**
L903: Selection of the 25 mg/m2 BID on a continuous dosing schedule (28 days=1 treatment cycle) with no rest period between cycles as confirmed during SPRINT Phase I.
L904: ## **Dose Modification and Dose Discontinuation:**
L905: If the patient experienced a toxicity requiring dose modification, selumetinib was to be withheld. If the toxicity resolved to meet study parameters or CTCAE v4.0 \leGrade 1 within 21 days of drug interruption, the patient could resume selumetinib at a dose reduced by 25% to 33%.

L945: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L946: The FDA agrees with description of SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 trial design presented in this section.
L947: The FDA considers the external control data to be supportive in a descriptive manner. Therefore, the data from the NCI POB NF1 Natural History Study and the placebo arm of Tipifarnib Study 01-C-0222 were not independently confirmed and will not be included in FDA's assessment of the efficacy results for SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1.
L948: ## Study Endpoints
L949: ## The Applicant's Description:
L950: ## **ORR**
L951: The primary outcome variable pre-specified in the protocol was objective response rate (ORR).
L952: The target PN was selected by the assessing investigator as the most clinically relevant PN, amenable to volumetric MRI analysis, to follow during the study. Tumor response was evaluated at re-staging visits prior to Cycles 5, 9, 13, 17, 21, 25, and then after every 6 cycles until permanent discontinuation of selumetinib and response was classified as summarized in Table 20.
L953: **Table 20 SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1: Classification of PN Response**
L954: Classification	Description
CR	Disappearance of the target PN.
PR	Decrease in the volume of the target PN by 20% or more compared with the baseline. The PR is considered unconfirmed at the first detection, confirmed when observed again within 3 to 6 months.
Stable disease	Insufficient volume change from baseline to qualify for either PR or progressive disease.
Progressive disease	Increase in the volume of the target PN by 20% or more compared with baseline or the time of best response after documenting a PR. The appearance of new PN (with the exception of new discrete subcutaneous neurofibromas) or unequivocal progression of existing clinically relevant non-target PN is also considered progressive disease.
L955: The target PN was selected by the assessing investigator as the most clinically relevant PN to follow during the study.
L956: CR Complete response; PN Plexiform neurofibroma(s); PR Partial response.
L957: Following FDA request, a retrospective independent central review (ICR) of the SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 volumetric MRI assessments was conducted by [REDACTED] (b) (4) as a sensitivity analysis.
L958: ## Duration of Response, PFS, TTP and Time to Response
L959: Additional secondary endpoints in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 were duration of response (DoR), tumor volume changes, PFS (including comparison with PFS in placebo arm of tipifarnib Study 01-C-0222), TTP and time to response (TTR). These were based on the NCI POB centrally read volumetric MRI of target PN, according to Response Evaluation in Neurofibromatosis and Schwannomatosis (REiNS) criteria.
L960: ## Clinical Outcome Assessments (COAs)
L961: A range of COAs presented below were used to assess the clinical benefit of selumetinib:
L962: - **Pain:** Numeric rating scale-11 (NRS-11) and pain interference index (PII)
- **Motor function:** Strength manual muscle test (MMT), ROM, patient-reported outcomes measurement information system (PROMIS) mobility and upper extremity, grooved pegboard, grip strength, and leg length discrepancy
- **Airway function:** Apnoea-Hypopnoea Index (AHI, sleep study), forced expiratory volume in 1 second (FEV1), forced expiratory volume in 0.75 seconds (FEV0.75), and R20 (resistance)
- **Bowel/bladder function:** Dysfunctional voiding questionnaire (DVQ)
- **Visual function:** visual acuity, amount of exophthalmos
- **Disfigurement:** standardized photography at each re-staging visit
- **Global Impression of Change (GIC)**
- **HRQoL:** Pediatric Quality of Life Inventory (PedsQL)
- **Other:** 6-minute walk test (6MWT)
L963: Pain (NRS-11 and PII) evaluations, functional patient-reported outcome (PRO) assessments and PedsQL were done at: pre-study; prior to Cycles 3, 5, 9, and 13, then every 12 cycles; and at the end of selumetinib treatment, if possible.
L964: Functional evaluations, including 6MWT were done at: pre-study; prior to Cycles 5, 9, and 13, then every 12 cycles; and at the end of selumetinib treatment, if possible.
L965: ## Additional Analyses
L966: As specified in the statistical analysis plan (SAP), additional analyses included external comparison of growth data and PFS with data from the NCI POB NF1 Natural History study.
L967: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L968: The FDA agrees that the study endpoints presented in this section were specified in the protocol and/or in the statistical analysis plan (SAP). We have the following comments:
L969: - The FDA agreed with plan to assess ORR by both the NCI POB central analysis and by an external independent central review committee. In general, the FDA requests confirmation
L970: of investigator assessed ORR and DOR with independent central review for regulatory assessment in the context of a single-arm trial.

- The open-label trial design limits interpretability of the COA endpoints because patient's (or parent/caregiver's) knowledge of treatment assignment may lead to systematic overestimation or underestimation of treatment effect which is currently unknown. Other challenges to interpretation of these endpoints include lack of a comparator treatment arm, small sample size, and potential instrument limitations.
- The FDA considers time-to-event endpoints, such as PFS, TTP, TTR, to be uninterpretable in a single-arm trial. The analyses of these endpoints were considered exploratory and were not verified by the FDA.
L971: ## Statistical Analysis Plan and Amendments
L972: ## The Applicant's Description:
L973: The SAP was agreed upon and finalized prior to the conduct of any efficacy analysis and locking of the database. Ad hoc analyses were added following the database lock to support the clinical interpretation.
L974: ## Efficacy Analysis
L975: Efficacy analyses related to assessing response in terms of volumetric reduction of target PN (ORR, DoR, TTR, PFS, and TTP) were conducted on the full analysis set (FAS) that included all patients who received at least 1 dose of selumetinib. Unless stated otherwise by supplementary analyses, sources of data that have not contributed to the efficacy analyses include multiple partial volumes of target PN, off-schedule assessments and data collected after permanent discontinuation of selumetinib. COAs were conducted either on the FAS, or age-subsets of the FAS (pain \ge 8 years, physical functioning \ge 5 years) or a subset of the FAS with PN-related morbidity. Safety analyses were also conducted on the FAS.
L976: ## *Primary Endpoint*
L977: The primary efficacy endpoint of the study was ORR based on the NCI POB central analysis of volumetric MRI of target PN. ORR was defined as the percentage of patients with complete response (CR) or confirmed partial response (PR; defined as target PN decrease \ge 20\% compared with baseline; confirmed when observed again within 3 to 6 months), per REiNS criteria, and was based on patients who received at least 1 dose of selumetinib (i.e., FAS).
L978: FAS patients with no imaging assessments were counted as non-responders. ORR was presented with corresponding 2-sided exact 95% CIs based on the Clopper-Pearson method (Clopper and Pearson 1934).
L979: A sensitivity analysis of the ORR based on the ICR of the imaging data was also performed. Supplementary analyses were performed to derive ORR based on all available volumetric MRI scans.
L980: ## *Secondary Endpoints*
L981: ## DoR
L982: DoR was defined as the time from the pre-cycle volumetric MRI assessment of the first documented response (subsequently confirmed) until the pre-cycle volumetric MRI assessment of documented progression on treatment or death in the absence of disease progression. The Kaplan-Meier method was used to calculate median DoR and 95% CI and the number and percentage of patients remaining in response for at least 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, and 24 cycles. Only patients who had a CR or confirmed PR were included in this analysis.
L983: Two pre-specified sensitivity analyses of the DoR were also performed:
L984: - The DoR based on the ICRs was derived using the same statistical method as for the analysis of DoR based on the central NCI POB review of the 3D volumetric MRI images on target lesions only.
- The DoR based on the actual dates of the NCI POB-based volumetric MRI assessments or death (if any) was also derived to assess the impact of dose interruptions on the DoR.
L985: A supplementary analysis was performed to derive DoR based on all available volumetric MRI scans.
L986: ## *PFS, TTP, TTR*
L987: PFS was defined as the time from study treatment/placebo initiation until the pre-cycle volumetric MRI assessment/date of objective disease progression on treatment/placebo or death (by any cause in the absence of progression) for SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1/placebo arm of tipifarnib Study 01-C-0222, respectively. Patients not known to have progressed at the time of analysis are censored at the last evaluable on-treatment pre-cycle volumetric MRI assessment.
L988: TTP was defined as the time from study treatment initiation until the pre-cycle volumetric MRI assessment of objective disease progression on treatment. Patients not known to have progressed at the time of analysis are censored at the last evaluable on-treatment pre-cycle volumetric MRI assessment.
L989: TTR was defined as the time from study treatment initiation until the pre-cycle volumetric MRI assessment of the first documentation of CR or a subsequently confirmed PR. The pre-cycle volumetric MRI assessment of the first documented response was to coincide with that used for the DoR endpoint. The TTR time was derived based on scheduled pre-cycle volumetric MRIs. Only patients who had achieved a CR or a confirmed PR were evaluated for TTR.
L990: Kaplan-Meier curves were produced for
L991: - PFS, including ad hoc PFS analysis for SPRINT Phase I and comparison of PFS to the placebo arm of the tipifarnib study [01-C-0222]
- TTP, including TTP for SPRINT Phase I and comparison of TTP to the placebo arm of the tipifarnib study [01-C-0222]
- TTR
L992: The median PFS, TTP and TTR (calculated from the corresponding Kaplan Meier plot, with 95% CIs) were also summarized.
L993: A supplementary analysis was performed to derive PFS based on all available volumetric MRI scans for SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 and SPRINT Phase I.
L994: ## *Inter-reader Variability*
L995: The margins of error for target PN volume at baseline and for percentage change from baseline were assessed using the intraclass correlation coefficient (ICC), which compared the assessment of 2 independent readers from (b)(4) on 10 randomly chosen patients. Mixed models were used to estimate the inter-reader and inter-subject standard deviations (SDs).
L996: ## *Clinical Outcome Assessments*
L997: The primary analysis of the COAs was based on the descriptive statistics and mixed model repeated measures (MMRM) models that summarize the changes over time on key COAs. In addition, supportive analyses using clinically meaningful thresholds (CMT) were conducted to help with interpretation of clinical benefit. Thresholds for meaningful change were estimated using both distribution (one-half SD) and anchor-based (with GIC as the anchor) approaches. Whenever available, thresholds from published literature were also used.
L998: In order to characterize the relationship between the effect of selumetinib on key PRO and functional outcomes and target PN volume reduction, the correlation and association between changes in PROs and functional outcomes and percent target PN volume change from baseline to pre-Cycle 13 was also explored using scatterplots and Spearman rank correlation coefficients (Cohen 1998).
L999: In addition, the association between longitudinal changes in key PROs and functional outcomes and percentage change from baseline in target PN volume was evaluated using a mixed model analysis.
L1000: ## *Additional Analysis*
L1001: A comparison of PN growth rates and PFS of SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 to the NCI POB NF1 Natural History study age-matched population was performed. The analysis was done for
L1002: 2 time periods: all available follow-up data or aligned to the maximum follow-up duration in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1.
L1003: For the Natural History study, crude PN growth rate was defined as the percent change in PN volume from the first to the last volumetric MRI assessment over time period in years, where time period was defined from the first to the last available volumetric MRI assessment or last volumetric MRI assessment date prior to the first use of a MEK inhibitor (including selumetinib).
L1004: For SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, crude PN growth rate was defined as the percent change in target PN volume from the baseline volumetric MRI to the last volumetric MRI assessment over time period in years, where time period was defined from the baseline volumetric MRI assessment date to the last evaluable assessment date up to DCO or treatment discontinuation (whichever occurred first).
L1005: Adjusted estimates of growth rate were derived from a random coefficient mixed model for the Natural History study and a repeated measure mixed model for the SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1.
L1006: In the Natural History study, PFS was defined as the time from the first volumetric MRI assessment to the date of documented progression or death in the absence of disease progression.
L1007: ## *Individual patient reviews*
L1008: In addition to the endpoints described previously, data were also presented in the format of individual patient reviews (IPRs) to characterize the efficacy of selumetinib at an individual patient level in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1. The aim of each IPR is to provide a comprehensive and cohesive narrative of the patient's journey prior to and during treatment with selumetinib, including an investigator assessment of whether each patient derived clinical benefit from study treatment.

L1054: ## Protocol Violations/Deviations
L1055: ## Data:
L1056: See the Applicant's position below.
L1057: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1058: In SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, 2 (4.0%) patients had important protocol deviations; 1 patient did not comply with protocol requirements (return of diary and missed evaluations) and 1 patient did not repeat an ECHO for a Grade 2 AE of left ventricular ejection fraction (LVEF) within the protocol specified time. Neither of these important deviations influenced the overall efficacy conclusions.
L1059: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1060: The SPRINT protocol defined protocol deviation as "any change, divergence, or departure from the IRB-approved research protocol." The protocol additionally states that "any change or divergence in the scheduling or conduct of examinations, tests and scans or sporadic missing vital signs or laboratory tests described in this protocol will NOT be considered a protocol deviation and will not require reporting to the (b) (4) or FDA unless it meets the definition of unanticipated problem or serious non-compliance." Therefore, isolated missed laboratory or time PK assessments and missed selumetinib doses were documented but not reported as deviations. Unanticipated problems were reported as deviations and included events that were unexpected in terms of nature, severity or frequency in relation to research risks and the characteristics of the study population, or that were related to participation in the research, or that suggested that the research trial put the patients or others at greater risk of harm than previously known or recognized.
L1061: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's description of the two events that were classified as protocol deviations in the SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 cohort. The first patient was removed on Day 64 for noncompliance. The second patient had a Grade 2 AE of decreased LVEF pre-Cycle 9; however, the ECHO was not repeated 3 to 6 weeks later per protocol. It was rather repeated 16 weeks later. The ECHO showed LVEF improvement at this later assessment.
L1062: Two additional protocol deviations occurred during SPRINT Phase 1. In both cases, the patients had been dose-reduced for AEs and the subsequent tumor evaluations demonstrated regrowth (not meeting threshold for PD). NCI recommended an alternative dose-modification for both patients (full dose for 5 days on/2 days off) in an effort to more effectively block the MEK pathway. These dose modifications were discussed with CTEP and the IRB however the required protocol amendments were not submitted.

L1087: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1088: There were a range of target PN volumes at baseline (median 488 mL; range 5.6 to 3820 mL), which is not unexpected given the variety of locations that these could occur (Table 25). The size of the target PN at baseline is in line with that observed in the pediatric population where they have been documented to reach up to 20% of body weight (Korf 1999; Mautner et al 2008).
L1089: In addition, patients were required to have PN-related morbidity at baseline, and the range of target PN volumes demonstrates that small and large PN volumes could result in considerable clinical symptoms. Most patients had some restrictions in physically strenuous activity and
L1090: impairment to daily activity at baseline as reflected in the range of Lansky and Karnofsky scales at baseline.
L1091: At enrolment, 42.0% patients had a progressive PN (i.e., growth \ge20% in the 12 to 15 months prior to enrolment). Patients had a median number of 3 morbidities present at baseline, with approximately a quarter of patients (13 patients) having 4 morbidities assigned to their target PN at baseline illustrating the complex symptom burden from just 1 PN.
L1092: ## Symptoms at Baseline
L1093: Forty-seven (94.0%) patients had at least 1 disease-related symptom at baseline and over two-thirds of these patients had either a maximum Grade 2, Grade 3 or Grade 4 symptom at baseline (22 [44.0%], 8 [16.0] and 4 [8.0%] patients, respectively). The Grade 4 symptoms at baseline included: tracheal obstruction extrinsic (2 [4.0%] patients), glaucoma, optic nerve disorder and respiratory failure (each in 1 [2.0%] patient). The most commonly reported symptoms at baseline were muscular weakness (34.0%; Grade 3: 4.0%), limb asymmetry (32.0%; Grade 3: 4.0%), tumour pain (32.0%; Grade 3: 2.0%), joint ROM decreased (26.0%; Grade 3: 8.0%) and scoliosis (26.0%; Grade 3: 6.0%).
L1094: ## Treatment Prior to Study Entry
L1095: Overall, 39 (78.0%) patients had received a prior treatment for PN and/or another NF1-related tumor, with most patients having had either a medical therapy or surgery, or both. The 2 most common PN-directed medical treatments (&gt;20% of patients at the ATC level) received by these patients were interferons (including peginterferon) and imatinib. Eighteen patients had received a single medical therapy and the remaining patients had received multiple successive medical therapies. Medical therapies for other NF1 tumors have also been included in this dataset.
L1096: In total, 28 (56.0%) patients had at least 1 prior PN-related or an NF1-related surgical procedure. The most common prior surgical procedure was 'nervous system neoplasm surgery', which was mostly PN debulking or resection from various body locations. Many of these patients had multiple prior PN resections.
L1097: In addition, 5 patients had target PN affecting the head and/or neck who had PN-related tracheostomy at baseline, 1 of whom had a non-standard tracheostomy and another who was able to be decannulated following treatment.
L1098: The disease burden in this population of patients with symptomatic, inoperable PN was reflected in that the majority of patients had already had PN-related medical treatment (62.0%) and/or surgical treatment for PN debulking or resection from various body locations (56.0%) before enrollment. As the patients with inoperable PN only have the treatment option to try and manage their morbidities, many patients were taking concomitant medications for
L1099: morbidity management, including strong analgesic agents, such as pregabalin, oxycodone and amitriptyline.
L1100: ## **External Control Data**
L1101: Patient baseline disease characteristics were generally similar between SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 and external control data (the age-matched population of the Natural History study [N=92]) and also the placebo arm of tipifarnib Study 01-C-0222 [n=29]).
L1102: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1103: The FDA agrees with the description of demographics and patient characteristics for Study SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 presented in Table 25.

L1108: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1109: Treatment adherence was measured, using diary review, for Cycles 1 to 12 to improve compliance for the rest of the study (i.e., to allow initiation of adherence interventions, if needed). Overall, treatment adherence over Cycles 1 to 12 was high (mean: 94.13%), with few missed doses.

L1111: The FDA agrees with the description of adherence described in this section.
L1112: ## **Concomitant medication after study entry**
L1113: ## **Data:**
L1114: See the Applicant's Position below.
L1115: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1116: All patients received at least 1 concomitant medication during the study for treatment of medical conditions or AEs. The most common concomitant medications (&gt;40% of patients) were ondansetron (62.0%), paracetamol (62.0%), mupirocin (52.0%), clindamycin (48.0%), ibuprofen (48.0%), and cefalexin (44.0%). In total, 20 (40.0%) patients received at least 1 medication, which is listed as a medication to avoid, per the CSP, during the study for treatment of medical conditions or AEs; the most common was ketoconazole (18.0%).
L1117: During PK collection times, only 2 concomitant medications were used and both were unlikely to affect the PK characterisation of selumetinib. The majority of medications were used topically or for a short duration, which was unlikely to affect the evaluations in this study.
L1118: A pain medication survey was collected 1 week prior to enrollment; the most common pain medications (\ge 10\% patients) during enrollment were gabapentin (10 [20.0%] patients) and ibuprofen (5 [10%] patients). Patients were taking a number of different pain medications, including pregabalin, oxycodone and amitriptyline.
L1119: The use of concomitant medications was not considered to have impacted the study results.

L1142: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1143: There was a high ORR of 66.0% (33 of 50 patients by NCI POB central analysis; 95%CI 51.2%, 78.8%) observed in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 (Table 26). No patients had a best objective response of disease progression. Supplementary analyses were performed to derive ORR based on all available volumetric MRI scans; ORR by this analysis was 66.0%, i.e., the same as the analysis including only the scheduled scans.
L1144: ## **ORR by PN Status at Enrolment: NCI POB Central Analysis**
L1145: There were similar response rates for patients whose PN were known to be either progressive (i.e., \ge 20\% in the 18 months prior to enrolment; n=21) or non-progressive (n=15) at enrolment, showing that both groups benefited from selumetinib treatment (Table 27). PN status at enrolment appears to be independent from response rate and, therefore, not predictive of selumetinib efficacy.
L1146: ## **Sensitivity Analysis: ORR According to ICR**
L1147: Sensitivity analysis for SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 based on the ICR, reported an ORR of 44.0% (95%CI: 30.0, 58.7). No patient had a best objective response of progressive disease.
L1148: Waterfall plots of the NCI POB central and ICR analyses demonstrate the consistency of results obtained by NCI POB central analysis and ICR (Figure 5), confirming the validity and reproducibility of the NCI POB central analysis.
L1149: Both NCI POB central and ICR analyses agreed that:
L1150: - No patients with a best objective response of progressive disease
- Only 2 patients had an increase in target PN volume from baseline, but the analyses reported different magnitude in the volume increase of the target PN.
L1151: Of note, there were 6 patients assessed by ICR who had target PN shrinkage between -19.2% and -19.9% as their best objective response, which were approaching the PR threshold.
L1152: Although ORR by ICR analysis was lower (44.0%, 22 of 50 patients) than that reported by NCI POB central analysis (66.0%, 33 of 50 patients), the differences in categorisations of best objective response between the NCI POB central analysis and the ICR analysis were primarily in assignments of confirmed PR versus stable disease (based on the chosen threshold of reduction of 20% in target PN volume to determine response) as shown in Figure 5.
L1153: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1154: FDA agrees with the results for the ORR presented in this section. There were several patients in the study who had an initial decrease in target PN volume from baseline but had an increase in volume in later visits. The ORR in subgroups based on demographic and clinical characteristics are presented Table 28.

L1159: ## Data Quality and Integrity
L1160: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1161: Quality of SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 data was assured through provision of appropriate training for study personnel; this study was conducted at pediatric hospitals leading in patient care.
L1162: Monitoring was conducted by the [REDACTED](b) (4). Two NCI POB databases were used to collect data for SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1; LabMatrix was used for PN disease evaluation, PROs and functional evaluation data and Cancer Central Clinical Database (C3D) was used for demographic, laboratory, safety (with the exception of dual-energy X-ray absorptiometry [DEXA] data, which were collected in LabMatrix) and PK data. In addition, the Cancer Therapy Evaluation Programme-Adverse Event Reporting System was used for suspected unexpected serious adverse reaction (SUSAR) reporting.
L1163: Onsite audits were conducted at all participating sites by [REDACTED](b) (4). In addition, AstraZeneca carried out Quality Assurance GCP audits of SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 at all 4 sites, to ensure compliance with relevant procedures and to assess the adequacy of quality control measures. Audits, by a [REDACTED](b) (4) operating independently of the study monitors and in accordance with documented policies and procedures, are directed towards all aspects of the clinical study process and its associated documentation.
L1164: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1165: The FDA acknowledges the Applicant's description of trial monitoring and data collection methods, the data quality assurance procedures, and the independent audits that were conducted.
L1166: The submission contained all of the required components of the electronic Common Technical Document (eCTD) and was of adequate quality and integrity to allow for review of the clinical trial data supporting the proposed indication. The results from the primary safety and efficacy datasets and the updated safety databases submitted with the 90-Day safety update were reproducible. FDA was able to confirm the Applicant's analyses of the key primary and secondary endpoints discussed in this review.
L1167: Multiple information requests were sent to the Applicant during the review cycle. Examples of these were requests to resubmit specific data with a different organization for ease of review, to conduct additional specified safety analyses of the Applicant's global database for selumetinib, to submit additional patient safety narratives, and to provide alternative presentations of efficacy results. The Applicant was able to provide responses to satisfy all FDA information requests.

L1201: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1202: ## PFS
L1203: Of the 50 patients enrolled to SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, only 3 patients had progressed at the DCO (all 3 patients had progressive PN at enrolment); therefore, the median PFS was not reached and there was a very high probability (94.7% [95% CI: 80.6, 98.7]) of remaining without progressive disease after 2 years in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 (Table 29). Two progressions occurred at pre-Cycle 21 and 1 progression occurred at pre-Cycle 31.
L1204: ## *Supplementary Analysis*
L1205: A supplementary analysis was performed to derive PFS based on all available volumetric MRI scans. According to this analysis, 6/50 (12.0%) patients in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 progressed, i.e., 3 more patients than were found to have progressed in the main PFS analysis.
L1206: ## *External Control: Comparison with the Placebo Arm of Tipifarnib Study 01-C-0222*
L1207: Results for the efficacy endpoint of PFS from the 29 patients who had progressive PN in placebo arm of tipifarnib Study 01-C-0222 provide an external control for PFS data from SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1.
L1208: Based on the last evaluable volumetric MRI assessment in the placebo arm of tipifarnib Study 01-C-0222, 23/29 (79.3%) patients had progressed. In SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, the subgroup
L1209: of patients with progressive PN at enrolment had the probability of remaining without progression at 2 years was 88.9% (95% CI 62.4, 97.1), which was much higher than the 20.6% (95% CI 7.7, 37.8) in the placebo arm of tipifarnib Study 01-C-0222 (Figure 6). This analysis was repeated using all 50 patients from SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 and showed similar results.
L1210: ## ***External Control: Comparison with the Natural History Study***
L1211: Figure 7 shows that 76.1% of patients in the Natural History study had disease progression in their target PN compared with 6.0% of patients in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, over a 2.8-year period. Median PFS in the Natural History age-matched cohort was 1.3 years (95% CI: 1.1, 1.6) and was not reached in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1. In SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, in all patients, the probability of remaining without progression after 2 years was 94.7% (95% CI: 80.6, 98.7) compared with 30.4% (95% CI 21.0, 40.3) in the Natural History study (i.e., no treatment with MEK-inhibitor).
L1212: ## **TTP**
L1213: No deaths were observed in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 hence TTP results are the same as of PFS presented above.
L1214: ## **TTR**
L1215: In SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, in the 33 responders, the majority of patients (24 [72.7%] patients) had a response by 8 cycles from first dose, with almost half (14 [42.2%] patients) having a response detected by 4 cycles from first dose (Table 30).
L1216: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1217: These analyses are exploratory and were not verified by the FDA.

L1241: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1242: A Kaplan-Meier plot of DoR in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 by NCI POB central analysis is presented in Figure 8.
L1243: At DCO in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 (29 June 2018), 32/33 patients with a response had been followed for at least 12 months from onset of response. Median DoR from onset of response was not reached; at the time of DCO the median follow-up time was 24 cycles (Table 31). Of the 33 responders, the probability of remaining in response after 12 cycles, estimated using the Kaplan-Meier method, was 100%.
L1244: ## Sensitivity Analyses of Duration of Response
L1245: ## *Based on actual assessment dates*
L1246: For the primary analysis, DoR was estimated in cycles with each cycle defined as 28 days in duration. However, this estimation does not account for any delays between cycles or interruptions due to dose modifying toxicities, investigations or other reasons (e.g., surgery). A sensitivity analysis using the actual dates of the volumetric MRI assessments was conducted. This analysis showed that of the 33 responders, the probability of remaining in response after 16 months, estimated using the Kaplan-Meier method was 100%, which was consistent with the primary analysis of DoR.
L1247: ## ICR Assessment
L1248: An ICR analysis of DoR was conducted using the same REiNS criteria used by the NCI POB central analysis. Based on the ICR assessment, of the 22 patients with confirmed PR, 7 patients subsequently had disease progression. Median DoR was reached at the last event, 32 cycles after the onset of response, and therefore data are still immature. This was consistent with the primary analysis of DoR, which shows very durable responses.
L1249: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1250: In general, the FDA agrees with the results presented in this section. However, it is difficult to interpret estimates of duration of response measured in time by 28-day cycles. Instead, the FDA calculation of estimates of duration of response considers time as measured by dates. The FDA calculation of the median DOR as assessed by ICR was 21.9 months (95% CI: 17.6, 30.3). The FDA calculation of median DOR as assessed by NCI POB Central Analysis was not reached and the 95% CI was not estimable. )
L1251: It is also important to note that, the definition of disease progression in the NCI POB central analysis included non-target lesions and new lesions, whereas the ICR assessments of disease progression were based only on target PN volumes.

## Koselugo (selumetinib)

At the time of dose interruptions, there appeared to be a slight trend towards lower efficacy compared with the slope of tumor size change before interruptions. However, with limited numbers it is difficult to conclude as such.

L1303: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1304: ## **PRO Compliance**
L1305: Overall, compliance was high for all PROs, based on the number of patients/parents expected to complete each PRO, supporting the validity of any conclusions drawn.
L1306: ## **Pain**
L1307: The effect of selumetinib on pain was assessed through (1) self-evaluation of pain intensity (NRS-11) of the tumor (target PN) PN selected by the physician, of the tumor selected by the patient, overall tumor pain and the other pain and (2) how pain interferes with daily functioning (PPI), which was rated by the patient and by the parent. In addition, patients recorded the pain medication that was used in the 1 week prior to enrolment in a pain medication survey. During
L1308: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 213756
## Koselugo (selumetinib)
L1309: study treatment, pain medication/analgesia was recorded within concomitant medications, regardless of indication for its use. Pain palliation was based on 2 related components (1) reduction in pain intensity based on NRS-11 physician-selected tumor pain score and (2) stability or reduction in analgesic use, represented by no increase or reduction in analgesia use based on the World Health Organization Analgesia Ladder.
L1310: Reduction in pain intensity for self-selected and target tumor pain, overall tumor pain and other pain was seen as early as pre-Cycle 3 and maintained through pre-Cycle 25 (Figure 10).
L1311: There was a decrease from baseline in pain scores at each measurement cycle based on MMRM analysis (Table 33 and Figure 11). For the 24 patients with physician-selected target tumor scores who completed both baseline and pre-Cycle 13 assessments, the adjusted mean change from baseline was -2.07 (95% CI: -2.84, -1.31); 12/24 (50.0%) patients showed improvement of \ge 2 points (CMT). ; half of patients (12 of 24, 50.0%) showed a clinically meaningful reduction (improvement) in pain at pre-Cycle 13 (i.e., \ge 2-point decrease from baseline). Of the remaining 12 patients (50.0%) who experienced no change, 10 patients had a pain score of 0 or 1 at baseline and therefore had no possibility to improve their pain score by 2 points or more. No patients showed deterioration at pre-Cycle 13.
L1312: For patients with an NRS-11 score \ge 2 at baseline (i.e., they had the possibility to improve by 2 points or more; n=14), pain palliation occurred early, within 2 to 4 months of treatment, and was sustained over time. The median time to pain palliation was reached by pre-Cycle 3 (1.9 months using the Kaplan Meier technique). This contrasts with published data from the Natural History study (Gross et al 2018), which showed that in the absence of surgical resection no symptomatic patients had improvement of their co-morbidities.
L1313: A decrease of 2 points on the NRS-11 was considered the CMT for this study population as this was reported in several studies in other populations and was also supported by the CMTs evaluated based on the distribution-based approach.
L1314: The improvements in pain are supported by self- and parent-reported changes in PII. There was a decrease from baseline in pain interference at each measurement cycle based on MMRM analysis (all p-values were p&lt;0.05). Of the 29 patients who completed both baseline and pre-Cycle 13 assessments, 10 (34.5%) patients showed improvement of \ge 0.75 (CMT). Of the 18 (62.1%) patients who showed no change, 15 patients had a baseline score &lt;0.75 and therefore could not improve by the clinical meaningful threshold of 0.75 or more. One (3.4%) patient showed deterioration at pre-Cycle 13. Similar results were observed on parent-reported pain interference.
L1315: ## NDA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 213756
## Koselugo (selumetinib)
L1316: The improvements in pain are further supported by self- and parent-reported changes in tumor pain and overall pain as assessed by the GIC instrument (Figure 12). The proportion of parents who reported improvements (very much/much improved) in their child's tumor pain increased consistently from baseline (34.5% and 17.2%, respectively at pre-Cycle 13). Worsening of tumor pain and overall pain was seldom reported by patients or parents (only 1 patient at pre-Cycle 13 reported minimally worse pain; none reported much or very much worse).
L1317: ## Motor
L1318: The effect on motor function was evaluated in the strength of muscle groups and ROM of joints that were allocated in the same quadrant as the target PN. There were 33 patients with motor morbidity at enrollment; of these, 12 patients had their target PN at the unilateral lower quadrant, 1 patient at the bilateral lower quadrant, 11 patients at the unilateral upper and 7 patients at the bilateral upper quadrant. A sub-group analysis was performed based on PN body quadrant (upper and lower body quadrant) to allow for a more meaningful comparison to baseline.
L1319: There was a trend for improvement in strength and ROM (regardless of the location of the target PN) based on MMRM analyses.
L1320: In the subgroup of patients who had their target PN located in a unilateral upper body quadrant:
L1321: - Strength at pre-Cycle 13 (n=11): 1 (9.1%) patient showed improvement in strength of \ge 0.43 (CMT). Of the 10 (90.9%) patients who showed no change, 7 patients had a strength MMT score &gt;4.57 at baseline and therefore could not improve by the CMT of 0.43. No patient showed deterioration in strength at pre-Cycle 13.
- ROM at pre-Cycle 13 (n=9): 4 (44.4%) patients showed improvement in ROM of \ge 112.25 degrees (CMT) and 5 (55.6%) patients showed no change. No patient showed deterioration in ROM at pre-Cycle 13.
L1322: In the subgroup of patients who had their target PN located in a unilateral lower body quadrant:
L1323: - Strength at pre-Cycle 13 (n=10): 5 (50.0%) patients showed improvement and 5 (50.0%) patients showed no change at pre-Cycle 13; of these, 3 patients had a strength MMT score &gt;4.73 at baseline and therefore could not improve by 0.27 (CMT). No patient showed deterioration at pre-Cycle 13.
- ROM at pre-Cycle 13 (n=10): 1 (10.0%) patient showed improvement of \ge 33 degrees (CMT), 8 (80.0%) patients showed no change and 1 (10.0%) patient showed deterioration at pre-Cycle 13.
L1324: For PROMIS, there was a trend towards improvement in mobility (as rated by parents) at each time point and a trend towards improvement in upper extremity physical function at pre-Cycle 25. Some patients showed clinically meaningful improvement on self-reported
L1325: mobility total scores (6 of 20 patients, 30.0%) and upper extremity scores (5 of 19 patients, 26.3%), with only few patients showing deterioration from baseline to pre-Cycle 13 for the self-reported mobility and upper extremity scores (2 patients each). Several patients had a score that was too high at baseline and therefore could not improve by the required CMT. A similar trend was observed for parent-reported scores, where some patients showing clinically meaningful improvement on mobility total scores (9 of 28 patients, 32.1%) and upper extremity scores (4 of 27 patients, 14.8%); no patients and 2 patients showing deterioration from baseline to pre-Cycle 13 for the parent-reported mobility and upper extremity scores, respectively. Several patients had a score that was too high at baseline and therefore could not improve by the required CMT.
L1326: ## PedsQL
L1327: Patient reported (\ge 8 years of age) and parent reported (all patients) HRQoL was assessed using the PedsQL questionnaire. There was improvement in self- and parent-reported HRQoL over time based on mean change from baseline in PedsQL total score and domain scores. Based on MMRM analysis of PedsQL total score, patients and parents reported improvement in HRQoL for patients at pre-Cycle 13 which was maintained through pre-Cycle 25. There was an increase from baseline in PedsQL total scores at each measurement cycle, which indicates improved HRQoL. Based on self-reported PedsQL total scores, at pre-Cycle 13 (n=29), 11 (37.9%) patients showed improvement based on a CMT of \ge 10.33. Based on parent-reported PedsQL total scores, at pre-Cycle 13 (n=45), 24 (53.3%) patients showed improvement based on a CMT of 11.90.
L1328: ## Airway
L1329: In SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, 16 patients had airway dysfunction at baseline. The majority of these (14 of 16 patients) had 3 (7 patients) or 4 (7 patients) baseline PN-related morbidities assigned to their target PN, showing the complexity and burden of symptoms in these patients. Five of these patients required airway support at baseline (such as tracheostomy), which limited their ability to perform pulmonary functioning tests. Of note, 1 patient was able to be decannulated and was still in response at pre-Cycle 25 and was still receiving selumetinib at study DCO.
L1330: A percentage change of \ge 12\% in FEV1 (litres) was classified as an improvement, as recommended by the REINS functional group (Plotkin et al 2016). FEV1 showed a positive trend from baseline (i.e., improvement) to pre-Cycle 13. At pre-Cycle 13, most patients (63.6%) showed improvement, with no patients showing deterioration in FEV1.
L1331: AHI is the number of apnoeas or hypopnoeas recorded per hour of sleep. At baseline, no patient had an AHI of &gt;5 events per hour; therefore, no patient met the REINS criteria (&gt;5 events/hour) for inclusion in sleep studies as they would be unable to improve enough to meet criteria for functional improvement.
L1332: The mean baseline AHI was 1.407 events per hour (n=14). For the 13 patients who had both baseline and pre-Cycle 13 sleep assessments, 6 patients had an AHI &gt;1 event per hour at baseline (AHI &gt;1 event per hour indicates sleep disturbance) and, of these, none had an increase in AHI at pre-Cycle 13.
L1333: Patients with airway PN did not demonstrate an overall mean change from baseline in the 6MWT at pre-Cycle 13, however, 12 of the 16 patients with airway dysfunction also had motor dysfunction which may have affected the test.
L1334: ## Orbit
L1335: Relatively few patients (n=10) were assigned a visual (orbit) PN-related morbidity, and for half (5 of 10) of the patients, there was no possibility of improvement in visual acuity from baseline, since the affected eye had either been enucleated (2 patients), vision was limited to light perception and position (1 patient) or patients had no light perception (2 patients).
L1336: ## Bowel/Bladder
L1337: For bowel and bladder functioning, relatively few patients (n=10) were assigned a bowel and bladder functioning PN-related morbidity, with insufficient baseline data to evaluate mean change for self-report scores and only a trend of improvement for parent-reported scores in the DVQ questionnaire.
L1338: For patients assigned a visual morbidity, or for patients assigned a bowel and bladder functioning morbidity, it is challenging to try and draw conclusion at a population level, and the IPRs (see Section 8.1.1) are more informative to understand changes in these morbidities in the context of other assigned morbidities and changes in target PN volume.
L1339: ## Correlation Analyses
L1340: The Spearman rank correlation coefficients (r), which measure strength of rank preserving relationship, were assessed per Cohen (1988). Whilst a weak correlation coefficient indicates a lack of evidence of a rank preserving relationship, it should not be interpreted as lack of relationship in general (r=0 to 0.3 equates to a weak correlation, r=0.3 to 0.5 equates to a moderate correlation, r=0.5 to 1.0 equates to a strong correlation.)
L1341: A positive correlation is defined as both an improvement in the COA and a reduction in target PN volume.
L1342: ## Pain
L1343: To assess the correlation between change in NRS-11 and percent change in target PN volume from baseline to pre-Cycles 5, 9, 13, and 25, patients with a score of \ge 2 at baseline were analysed (Figure 13).
L1344: - At pre-Cycle 5, the majority of patients have shown reduction in tumor volume as well as reduction in NRS-11 score. However, the scatter plot is compact, with the deepest shrinkage at -20% and the strongest pain improvement at -4 (except for 1 patient). Weak correlation (r of 0.07) was observed at this point.
- At pre-Cycle 9, all but 2 patients have shown reduction in tumor volume, of whom all have shown reduction in NRS-11 score. The scatter plot expands as PN shrink more and even stronger pain improvement is observed (more patients beyond -4). A strong correlation can be observed at this point, with a correlation coefficient of 0.76.
- At pre-Cycle 13, the same pattern is observed with the exception of 2 outliers which neutralize the correlation coefficient (0.13, weak correlation).
- At pre-Cycle 25, the pattern is similar to pre-Cycle 9 and pre-Cycle 13 but without the 2 outliers, resulting in a strong correlation (r of 0.71).
L1345: Longitudinal analyses were also performed for NRS-11. After adjusting for age, number of morbidities and the baseline value of the clinical outcome, there was evidence of a meaningful correlation between percentage change from baseline in target PN volume and change in NRS-11 score (p&lt;0.001). This analysis demonstrates that improvement in pain intensity strongly correlates with reduction in PN volume.
L1346: ## *Motor*
L1347: Despite potential confounding factors affecting motor function (including the anatomical location of the PN, and the degree of other associated symptoms including pain at baseline); several correlations between percentage change in PN volume and motor function were observed.
L1348: In terms of strength, a weak correlation was observed between change from baseline to pre-Cycle 13 in strength MMT and percent change in target PN volume (r was 0.06). A correlation was observed between the change from baseline to pre-Cycle 13 in ROM and percent change in target PN volume (r was -0.24) indicating gain in ROM as the PN shrinks.
L1349: The Spearman rank coefficients (r) for the change from baseline for the PROMIS self-reported and parent-reported scores (mobility) and percent change in target PN volume at pre-Cycle 13 are 0.11 (weak correlation) and -0.43 (moderate positive correlation), respectively. The r for the correlations with change from baseline for the PROMIS self-reported and parent-reported scores (upper extremity) and change in target PN volume at pre-Cycle 13 are -0.30 (moderate positive correlation) and -0.16 (weak positive correlation), respectively. These correlations suggest that increasing PROMIS transformed scores, which indicate better physical functioning, were associated with decreasing target PN volumes.
L1350: Correlations with percentage change in target PN volume were also detected in the longitudinal models of change in parent-reported PROMIS scores (both mobility p=0.042 and upper extremity p=0.034).
L1351: ## *PedsQL*
L1352: A clear positive correlation was observed between percentage change in PN volume and parent-reported PedsQL total scores; with a similar observation for the physical, emotional and social functioning subscales. These results indicate that HRQoL improvement is associated with decreasing target PN volume. This correlation was also detected in the longitudinal mixed model of change in parent-reported PedsQL total score (p=0.033).
L1353: The correlation with self-report PedsQL total score is less apparent as it was impacted by 2 patients who were considered outliers (patients with a PN growth &gt;10\% who reported substantial improvement in HRQoL).
L1354: ## *Airway*
L1355: The Spearman rank coefficients (r) was 0.39, which indicates a moderate positive correlation between the change from baseline to pre-Cycle 13 in AHI and percent change in target PN volume. However, the baseline AHI was below the REINS criteria (\le 5 events/hour) for inclusion in sleep studies and, thus, the results should be interpreted with caution.
L1356: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1357: The FDA does not agree with the analyses and interpretation of the results presented by the applicant in this section for physician selected target PN pain intensity, pain interference, and health related quality of life. The trial collected a number of PROs, observer-reported outcomes (ObsROs) and performance observations (PerfO). The FDA analysis focused on pain NRS, pain interference and HRQL. Pain NRS was captured using 4 items that asked patients to select one number from 0 to 10 that best described their worst pain over the past week for the following: 1) overall pain, 2) overall tumor pain, 3) pain for a specific tumor patients choose, and 4) pain for a specific tumor physicians choose. The Pain Interference Index, which is 6-item measure that assesses the degree to which pain interfered with daily activities in the past week. And the PedsQL, a 23-item measure of global QOL with 4 subscales: physical functioning, emotional functioning, social functioning, and school functioning. For children aged 2 – 18 the parent-reported version was administered and for children 8 – 18 the self-report versions were administered. The completion rates for these instruments were high and are presented in FDA Table 34. However, all of these analyses are exploratory and there are several challenges associated with the analysis and interpretation of this data. These include lack of a comparator arm, and by extension, parent/caregiver's knowledge of treatment assignment, a small sample size, potential instrument limitations, thresholds used for defining clinically meaningful change, and assumptions of the MMRM.

L1360: 1Percentages are reported as percent of baseline PRO-evaluable patients
L1361: There were challenges with regard to the analysis of the pain NRS, as the primary focus of the analysis was on the results from the NRS-11 physician-selected tumor pain score. Due to a change in the protocol, only 24 patients of the 34 patients eligible to respond (i.e., patients age &gt; 8 years) completed this particular pain item. No deteriorations were reported at the pre-cycle 13 (i.e., approximately 1 year post baseline), and the majority of patients classified as stable had either no pain or pain that could not reduce by \le 2 points at baseline. As pain was measured infrequently, it is difficult to interpret the pain results, and this may help to explain
L1362: the weak correlation results with tumor shrinkage.
L1363: Results from the PII support the finding that pain did not appear to be getting worse while on treatment with only one patient reporting a deterioration on PII.
L1364: For PedsQL all items exhibit some degree of floor effects (i.e., more than 20% of patients responding to the lowest response option) indicating this tool may be limited in detecting change in HRQL and other domains. In addition, the total score of the PedsQL includes a number of items that are considered distal to the drug effect (e.g., “others tease me” or “it’s hard to pay attention in class”). In examining the physical function domain, mean change for physical functioning from the self-report PedsQL for participants 8 and older indicated that on average patients had improvements over the course of the treatment. The same pattern was observed for the parent-reported physical function score of the PedsQL.
L1365: The analyses of the other PRO and performance endpoints presented in this section are considered exploratory. FDA considered other endpoints presented in this section to be supportive but did not independently verify the results.

L1402: ## The Applicant's Position:
L1403: ## **Margin of Error for Target PN Measurements Estimated by Inter-reader Variability**
L1404: Following FDA request, the margin of error for target PN measurements at baseline and over time was also assessed.
L1405: The margin of error for target PN measurements at baseline between the 2 readers was assessed using the ICC, which indicated excellent agreement (ICC of 0.99; Table 35). The inter-reader SD (100.19 mL) is more than 12 times smaller than the inter-subject SD (1277.24 mL); this indicates a very small margin of error in estimating target PN volume at baseline.
L1406: The margin of error for percentage change in target PN volume between the 2 readers across timepoints was assessed using the ICC, which indicated excellent agreement (ICC of 0.90; Table 36). The inter-reader SD (5.75%) is approximately 3 times smaller than the inter-subject SD (16.82%); it indicates a relatively small margin error in estimating percentage change in PN volume. Therefore, the threshold to determine PR (a PN decrease \ge20% compared with baseline) is clearly outside of the margin of error for PN measurements.
L1407: ## **Volumetric PN Growth in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 and the NCI POB NF1 Natural History Study (Age-matched)**
L1408: The Natural History age-matched cohort (N=92) comprised patients aged 3 to 18 years at the time of enrollment who had at least 1 volumetric MRI within this age and at least 1 subsequent volumetric MRI.
L1409: Spaghetti plots showing individual patient PN volumes over 2.8 years and change in target PN volume over time are presented in Figure 14 (y-axis range: -60% to +60%) for SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 and the Natural History Study age-matched cohort.
L1410: The plot shows that patients treated with selumetinib in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 demonstrated durable and sustained PN responses (\ge20% reduction in target PN volume from baseline) or PN volume stabilisation (&lt;20% change in PN volume from baseline). In the Natural History age-matched cohort the vast majority of PN grew continuously over time or at best remained stable in size (&lt;20% change in PN volume from baseline). There was no spontaneous PN shrinkage of \ge20% within 1 year in the Natural History study.
L1411: Over a 2.8-year period, the median PN volume change per year in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 was -10.2% (range -27.3% to +19.0%), whereas in the Natural History age-matched cohort it was +21.3% (range -4.1% to +147.9%). The adjusted mean annual PN growth rate was -16.9% (95% CI: -20.2, -13.5) per year in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, versus +21.3% (95% CI: +15.9, +26.8) in the Natural History age-matched cohort (Table 37).
L1412: The natural course of a PN is to grow over time and, as shown in this Natural History age-matched cohort, at least half of these children have an annual growth of their PN by over
L1413: +21%. Spontaneous PN shrinkage has been documented for few patients at -4% per year. In comparison, the median tumor reduction was -10% per year in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, indicating that this degree of shrinkage can be attributed to selumetinib treatment, rather than to natural course of the disease.
L1414: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1415: The analyses presented in this section are exploratory and were not verified by the FDA.
L1416: ## 8.1.3. SPRINT Phase I
L1417: ## Trial Design
L1418: ## The Applicant's Description:
L1419: This was a CTEP-sponsored Phase I, open-label, single-arm, multiple dose, dose-escalation, multi-centre study of selumetinib in pediatric patients with NF1 and inoperable PN, and was conducted by the NCI POB. At least 21 patients aged \ge 3 and \le 18 years, with the ability to swallow whole capsules (as capsules cannot be crushed or broken), were planned for enrolment. Selumetinib was administered orally BID (approximately every 12 hours) on a continuous schedule; the dose was capped at 50 mg when BSA was \ge 1.9 \text{ m}^2. A cycle of treatment was 28 days with no rest periods between cycles.
L1420: The schedules for volumetric MRI assessments was pre-study; prior to Cycles 6 and 11, and every 6 cycles thereafter.
L1421: The same volumetric MRI method and analysis was used to measure target PN size and change in volume in SPRINT Phase I and Phase II Stratum 1, including review of volumetric MRIs by the same NCI POB central reviewer.
L1422: Efficacy endpoints evaluated in Phase I include ORR, DoR and TTR (all exploratory).
L1423: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1424: The FDA agrees to the Applicant's description of the trial design.

L1436: ## Patient Disposition
L1437: ## **Data:**
L1438: See the Applicant's Position below.
L1439: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1440: In SPRINT Phase I, at DCO in SPRINT Phase I (29 June 2018), 13 (54.2%) patients remained on selumetinib treatment; 3 (12.5%) patients had discontinued selumetinib but remained on-study. In total, 11 patients discontinued selumetinib; the reasons were: AE (3 patients), disease progression (2 patients), patient not willing to continue (2 patients), treatment period completed (1 patient) and other (3 patients). Three patients discontinued and re-started selumetinib during the study; 2 of these patients remained on selumetinib at DCO and 1 patient discontinued a second time. Eight patients terminated the study; the reasons were voluntary discontinuation by patient (4 patients), disease progression (2 patients), AE (1 patient) and other (1 patient). There were no deaths during SPRINT Phase I.

L1462: ## **Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., disease characteristics, important concomitant drugs)**
L1463: ## **Data:**
L1464: See the Applicant's Position below.
L1465: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1466: In SPRINT Phase I, the median time from diagnosis of PN to start of selumetinib treatment was 8.28 years (range: 0.79 to 17.00 years). The target PN volume at baseline ranged from 29.4 to 8744.0 mL and the median target PN volume was 1204.50 mL and most patients had minor restrictions in physically strenuous activity at baseline. Target PN were located in different body sites for different patients and 1 patient had a whole body PN.
L1467: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1468: The FDA agrees with the results presented in this section

L1491: ## **Data Quality and Integrity**
L1492: ## **Data:**
L1493: SPRINT Phase I was conducted at pediatric hospitals leading in care of patients with NF1-related PN. Phase I was monitored by CTEP via quarterly data submissions in Clinical Data Update System. On-site audits were to be conducted at all participating sites by the (b) (4)
L1494: conducted 1 tri-annual audit in May 2014 and monitored 11 patients.
L1495: The C3D was used to collect safety and efficacy data from SPRINT Phase I.
L1496: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1497: Study conduct, quality and integrity were appropriate for this study.
L1498: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1499: The FDA acknowledges the Applicant's description of CTEP trial monitoring procedures, data collection methods, and the independent audits that were conducted for the phase 1 portion of SPRINT. See Section 8.1.2 *Data Quality and Integrity* for the FDA's assessment of overall data quality and integrity in the NDA.
L1500: ## **Efficacy Results – Secondary and other relevant endpoints**
L1501: ## **Data:**
L1502: See the Applicant's Position below.
L1503: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1504: ## **ORR**
L1505: The ORR observed in SPRINT Phase I study was 66.7% [16 of 24 patients] 95% CI 44.7%, 84.8%.
L1506: Supplementary analyses were performed to derive ORR based on all available volumetric MRI scans. For SPRINT Phase I, one more confirmed PR was observed when including all available volumetric MRI scans (17/24 patients [70.8%]).
L1507: ## **PFS**
L1508: In SPRINT Phase I, only 2 patients progressed hence the median PFS was not reached.
L1509: A supplementary analysis was performed to derive PFS based on all available volumetric MRI scans. According to this analysis, in SPRINT Phase I, 4/24 (16.7%) patients progressed, i.e., 2 more patients than were found to have progressed in the main PFS analysis.
L1510: ## **TTP**
L1511: No deaths were observed SPRINT Phase I, hence TTP results are the same as of PFS presented above.
L1512: ## **TTR**
L1513: In SPRINT Phase I, 81.3% of the patients with confirmed objective response had target PN shrinkage of \ge 20\% from baseline within 10 cycles. Since there were 5 cycles between the first and second volumetric MRI scans, the median time to onset of response was 7.5 cycles.
L1514: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1515: The FDA agrees with the results for the overall response rate from the SPRINT Phase I study presented in this section; however, this data is considered descriptive only.
L1516: Additionally, the FDA considers time-to-event endpoints uninterpretable in a single-arm trial. These analyses are considered exploratory and were not verified by the FDA.
L1517: ## **Dose/Dose Response**
L1518: ## **Data:**
L1519: The PK exposure from the doses evaluated in SPRINT Phase I indicated a dose proportional increase; however, as the difference in dose was approximately 20% between doses, the exposures were overlapping across these doses. The dose-efficacy relationship from SPRINT Phase I indicated that ORR was broadly similar across doses.
L1520: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1521: Not applicable.
L1522: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1523: See FDA assessment under Section 6.2.2.1.
L1524: ## **Data:**
L1525: See Applicant's Position below.
L1526: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1527: At the DCO for SPRINT Phase I, all patients with a confirmed response had been followed up for at least 16 months from the onset of response; follow-up time from the onset of responses ranged from 18 to 65 cycles. Similar to SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, median DoR from onset of response was not reached as only 1 of the 16 confirmed responders (i.e., cPR) had subsequently progressed by the study DCO. All 16 patients who were confirmed responders were still in
L1528: response after 16 cycles from the onset of response. The estimated proportion of patients remaining in response after 52 cycles, estimated using the Kaplan-Meier method, was 100%.
L1529: Supplementary analyses were performed to derive DoR based on all available volumetric MRI scans confirmed the responses to be highly durable with the probability of still being in response 46 months after onset of response was 90.0% (95% CI: 47.3, 98.5). Median DoR was not reached (95% CI:41.8, not calculated)
L1530: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1531: Given the small sample of patients with a confirmed response, this analysis is considered exploratory. The FDA calculation of estimates of duration of response considered time as measured by dates rather than treatment cycle. Per the FDA calculation, the median DOR was not reached, all 16 responders remained in response for more than 12 months from the onset of response, and the duration of response ranges from 16.4 to 63.3 months. The FDA did not verify the supplementary analyses results presented in this section.

L1544: ## **8.1.5. Integrated Review of Effectiveness**
L1545: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**

L1547: ## 8.1.6. Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials
L1548: ## Primary Endpoints
L1549: ## **Data:**
L1550: See the Applicant's Position below.
L1551: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1552: ## **Objective Response**
L1553: Selumetinib demonstrated a confirmed ORR by NCI POB central analysis of 66.0% (95% CI: 51.2, 78.8), based on 33 responders from 50 evaluable patients in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1. A further 4 (8.0%) patients had an unconfirmed partial response (PR). No patients had progression as their best response. Reduction in target PN volume was observed early, with almost all patients having a reduction in target PN volume by 4 cycles (i.e., pre-Cycle 5, median percentage change in target PN volume from baseline was -14.31% at this time point). Natural History study data indicate that the PN shrinkage observed in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 can be attributed to selumetinib, rather than the natural course of the disease. The adjusted mean annual growth rate was +21.3% (95% CI: 15.9, 26.8) per year in the Natural History study age-matched cohort compared with -16.9% (95% CI: 20.2, -13.5) per year in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1. Supportive ORR data from SPRINT Phase I (66.7% [16 of 24 patients]) confirmed the high ORR observed in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1.
L1554: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L1555: The FDA agrees with the response results for SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1; however, considers any comparison of results between the SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 and the Natural History study as exploratory due to the lack of information available to adjust any comparison for potential confounding or bias.
L1556: ## Secondary and Other Endpoints
L1557: ## **Data:**
L1558: See the Applicant's Position below.
L1559: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L1560: ## **Duration of Response**
L1561: The median DoR from onset of response based on NCI POB central analysis was not reached in either the pivotal SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 or supportive Phase I data. Of the 33 responders in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 and the 16 responders in SPRINT Phase I, the probability of remaining in response, after 12 cycles and 52 cycles, respectively, estimated using the Kaplan-Meier method, was 100%.
L1562: ## Progression-free Survival
L1563: Very few patients progressed in either SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 (3 patients) or SPRINT Phase I (2 patients). Median PFS was not reached in either trial. The PFS benefit of selumetinib treatment observed in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 was confirmed by the Natural History age-matched cohort as most patients in this cohort had progressed (76.1%), median PFS was 1.3 years (95% CI: 1.1, 1.6) with the probability of remaining without progressive disease after 2 years of 30.4% (95% CI: 21.0, 40.3). In addition, the PFS benefit of selumetinib treatment in observed in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 was also confirmed by the placebo arm of tipifarnib Study 01-C-0222, (patients with progressive PN) where most patients in this control group had progressed (23 of 29 patients, 79.3%), median PFS was estimated as 1.0 year, with the probability of remaining without progressive disease after 2 years of 20.6% (95% CI: 7.7, 37.8).
L1564: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L1565: The FDA agrees with the results for DOR in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 presented in this section, but considers other results not interpretable.
