L2300: ## 8.2.9. Additional Safety Explorations
L2301: ## Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
L2302: ## Data:
L2303: See the Applicant's Position below.
L2304: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2305: No human carcinogenicity data are available. Based on pre-clinical studies selumetinib is not considered carcinogenic (see Section 5.5.3) and the number of patients on SPRINT who
L2306: developed MPNST does not exceed the known background incidence of malignant transformation of PN to MPNST in NF1 population.
L2307: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2308: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L2309: ## **Human Reproduction and Pregnancy**
L2310: ## Data:
L2311: See the Applicant's Position below.
L2312: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2313: Based on nonclinical data (Section 5.5.4), it is recommended that a pregnancy test should be performed on female patients of child bearing potential prior to treatment with selumetinib. In addition, female patients of child bearing potential must use adequate contraception during treatment and for at least 1 week after completion of treatment. Lactating mothers should be advised not to breast-feed during treatment with selumetinib. Male patients with sexual partners who are pregnant or who could become pregnant (ie, females of child bearing potential) should use acceptable methods of contraception for at least 1 week after completing treatment with selumetinib to avoid pregnancy and/or potential adverse effects on the developing embryo. Acceptable methods of contraception include implants, injectables, combined oral contraceptives (which must all be combined with barrier methods of contraception as any interaction with selumetinib is unknown), some IUDs and vasectomized partner. Sexual abstinence is also an acceptable method of contraception according to ICHM3.
L2314: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2315: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's position.
L2316: ## **Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth**
L2317: ## **Data:**
L2318: See the Applicant's Position below.
L2319: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2320: Given that the NDA is for a pediatric indication, Section 8.2.4 provides substantial safety information for this patient population.
L2321: With respect to effects on developing organ systems, microscopic findings consistent with physeal dysplasia were observed in the femora-tibial joint in rats. These effects were observed at exposure multiples 11-fold greater than the free exposure levels in humans. Physeal dysplasia has not been observed in monkeys or mice treated with selumetinib for periods of up to 6 months, the latter which were of an age and immaturity to have had open growth plates. A juvenile toxicity study is not considered likely to add further to the risk assessment for this finding. As a result of these nonclinical findings, physeal dysplasia has been prospectively identified as an AESI. Pediatric patients have been monitored for signs of physeal dysplasia and bone growth effects. To date no significant changes in these parameters have been reported in pediatric patients following treatment with selumetinib.
L2322: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2323: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment. Growth and development parameters will continue to be assessed as part of the SPRINT protocol's long-term safety follow-up. See Section 8.2.10.
L2324: ## **Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound**
L2325: ## **Data:**
L2326: Not applicable.
L2327: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2328: ## **Overdose**
L2329: Inadvertent misdosing of selumetinib, such as administration of a higher selumetinib dose than stated in the protocol, should be followed up and treated with appropriate supportive care until recovery. Selumetinib overdose is defined as any dose received above 25 mg/m2. There is no specific treatment for overdose. Due to low elimination of selumetinib related material in urine, dialysis is unlikely to influence the elimination during overdose. Physicians should follow general supportive measures and should treat the patient symptomatically.
L2330: ## Drug Abuse
L2331: Based on its mode of action, physiological and pharmacological activity, and lack of stimulant properties, selumetinib does not have a potential for drug abuse, and no findings during the clinical study program indicate that selumetinib induces drug abuse.
L2332: ## Withdrawal and Rebound
L2333: In SPRINT, all patients are mandated to complete a safety visit approximately 30 days following the last dose of selumetinib. Based on the available data, no evidence of withdrawal or rebound effects was noted following discontinuation of treatment with selumetinib therapy.
L2334: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2335: The FDA agrees with the Applicant's assessment.
L2336: ## 8.2.10. Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L2337: ## Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
L2338: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2339: This sub-section is not applicable as selumetinib is not currently registered (or approved) in the US or in any other part of the world.
L2340: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2341: Not applicable.
L2342: ## Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
L2343: ## The Applicant's Position:
L2344: AstraZeneca has comprehensive processes for signal detection, regular safety reviews, identifying and evaluating issues potentially affecting patient safety, and developing safety recommendations (including changes to the reference safety information). In addition, these processes enable the identification of safety topics that need to be kept under close surveillance. The safety signal detection activities include review of reported AEs from post marketed sources, and a review of the published literature relevant to selumetinib. The post market data for selumetinib will be regularly reviewed for new findings or trends.
L2345: In addition to routine pharmacovigilance, the following post-authorization measures are planned for selumetinib in the treatment of patients with NF1 and PNs:
L2346: - Long term safety follow-up from the SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 study.
L2347: ## SPRINT: Planned Long Term Follow-up
L2348: The CSP for the SPRINT study (version in place at the DCO date, following Amendment M; dated 28 September 2016) for Phase II Stratum 1 has a primary objective to evaluate the confirmed
L2349: PR and CR rate of selumetinib in children and young adults with NF1 PN using volumetric MRI analysis. The primary analysis has been conducted after all patients with a confirmed response had been followed-up for at least 12 months from the onset of response.
L2350: At the DCO for the primary analysis (29 June 2018), 50 patients in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 had received selumetinib treatment for a median total duration of 802 days (approximately 26 months; range 28 days [approximately 1 month] to 1053 days [approximately 35 months/3 years]). The median actual durations were similar which shows that most patients were able to receive assigned treatment and that the duration of any treatment interruptions were usually short. Of the 50 patients in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, 34 patients remain on selumetinib treatment. At the same DCO date, 24 patients in SPRINT Phase I had received selumetinib treatment for a median total duration of 1606 days/53 treatment cycles (approximately 53 months/4.4 years; range: 158 days [approximately 5 months] to 2169 days [approximately 71 months/5.9 years]). Of these 24 patients, 13 patients remain on selumetinib treatment.
L2351: Safety data will continue to be reported: AstraZeneca employs routine pharmacovigilance activities which are designed to proactively identify and/or characterize safety concerns of selumetinib and will inform risk mitigation strategies for the important identified risks. These activities include coordination with the study sponsor for ongoing receipt and analysis of AE data from SPRINT.
L2352: The long duration of selumetinib exposure in SPRINT did not affect the ability of the patients to remain on treatment and the fact that AEs have been managed by dose modifications over this long duration provides valuable evidence in support of tolerability and safety of selumetinib in long term use. Per protocol, all patients in Phase II Stratum 1 will enter long-term follow-up for a duration of 7 years from the initiation of treatment or 5 years after completion of selumetinib, whichever takes longer. If a patient is no longer on treatment but remains on the study, during long term follow-up, these patients will have annual visits where a complete physical examination will be conducted (including assessment of height, weight, BSA, and performance status), together with: a complete blood count; a differential platelet count; serum electrolytes; renal and liver function tests; CPK level; and urinalysis assessment. Additionally, ophthalmology examinations and echocardiography will be performed if clinically indicated.
L2353: The follow-up assessments will note whether there have been any significant medical issues in the preceding 12 months with a focus on possible AEs related to selumetinib and safety follow-up will specifically collect data on whether patients have seen a pediatric oncologist in the past year (with reasons why, if the answer is affirmative). Moreover, for patients who remain on selumetinib treatment, all of the above safety assessments will be carried out as per the following schedule: prior to Cycles 2 to 5, then every other cycle (prior to Cycles 7, 9, 11,
L2354: 13); then every 4 cycles (prior to Cycles 17, 21, 25); then every 6 cycles (prior to Cycles 31, 37, 43, etc.).
L2355: As part of the long-term follow-up of the SPRINT study, all SAEs (including SUSARs) that occur following database lock are reported to the AstraZeneca Patient Safety Department by NCI/CTEP; all SAEs are followed-up as necessary. In addition, a line listing of all AEs occurring in SPRINT will be provided to the AstraZeneca Patient Safety Department on a quarterly basis for inclusion in safety surveillance. The line listing of AEs will be analyzed by AstraZeneca to provide information regarding signal evaluation and evaluation of risks and new information as applicable will be analyzed and presented in the Periodic Safety Update Report. In addition, and in accordance with NCI standard AE reporting procedures, any AE which has a change in CTCAE grade is reported as a separate AE.
L2356: ## **The FDA's Assessment:**
L2357: The FDA agrees with the long-term follow-up safety monitoring that is part of the SPRINT protocol. See Section 13 for additional clinical safety post-marketing requirements (PMRs).
L2358: ## **8.2.11. Integrated Assessment of Safety**
L2359: ## **Data:**
L2360: See the Applicant's Position below.
L2361: ## **The Applicant's Position:**
L2362: The safety profile of selumetinib 25 mg/m2 BID (approximately every 12 hours) in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, is consistent with the well characterized safety profile of selumetinib (20 to 30 mg/m2 BID) in 74 pediatric patients with NF1 and inoperable PN and with the known safety profile in the MEK drug class. These safety findings are supported by data from 347 adult patients with cancer who received selumetinib 75 mg BID.
L2363: - Overall the safety profile for selumetinib as treatment for pediatric patients aged 3 to 18 years and above with NF1 and symptomatic, inoperable PN, including commonly reported events is considered manageable, acceptable and well tolerated.
- Overall, the type of AEs reported were consistent with the safety profile for the MEK inhibitor class of drugs. The safety profile of selumetinib with respect to the frequency and CTCAE severity of AEs in the SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 pediatric population was consistent to that observed in the adult populations.
- The majority of AEs reported with selumetinib were manageable with either selumetinib dose modifications and/or supportive therapy. Most resolved during the course of the study and generally did not affect the ability of patients to remain on treatment for long periods of time.
- The frequently observed AEs (&gt;45%) that characterize the selumetinib safety profile (acneiform rashes, gastrointestinal events [diarrhoea, nausea and vomiting], paronychia,
L2364: and pyrexia, AST increased, and fatigue/asthenia) did not generally lead to discontinuation of selumetinib in pediatric patients

- Considering the prolonged duration of exposure, the incidence of treatment-related SAEs (12.0%) and treatment-related permanent discontinuation of selumetinib treatment (12.0%) in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1, with similar incidences seen in the Pediatric pool, supports a well-tolerated safety profile. No deaths were reported on this study.
- AES of CTCAE Grade \ge 3, by PT (\ge 5\%) in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 were diarrhoea, hypoxia, pyrexia, blood CPK increased, paronychia, vomiting, and weight increased. A similar profile was seen in the Pediatric pool.
- The AESIs noted in the Pediatric pool are consistent with those seen in the Adult monotherapy pool. No new significant safety concerns were identified.
- No evidence of the retinal events of csr/RPED or RVO, or events suggestive of physeal dysplasia associated with selumetinib have been identified in the context of considerable duration of exposure in pediatric patients.
L2365: The majority of the causally attributed AEs appear to occur in the first year of selumetinib treatment. The safety profile of selumetinib does not appear to significantly worsen over time. Pediatric specific AE management guidelines are in place to ensure consistency in AE management. Long term safety will be continually assessed as part of routine pharmacovigilance.
L2366: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2367: The FDA generally agrees with the Applicant's integrated assessment of safety except the statement that the frequency and severity of AEs in the pediatric population was similar to the adult population.
L2368: The pediatric safety data from SPRINT, the supportive adult monotherapy pooled data, the supplemental data from the selumetinib expanded access program and externally-sponsored pediatric selumetinib studies, and the additional safety analyses of the Applicant's global selumetinib database submitted during the review allowed for an informed risk assessment of selumetinib in the intended NF1 PN population.
L2369: The primary safety data was from the NCI SPRINT trial, an open-label, multicenter, single arm trial in 74 patients 3 to 18 years of age with NF1 and inoperable PN. Although the sample size was relatively small, the median duration of treatment was approximately 2 years, permitting a reasonable evaluation of risk for cumulative toxicities with chronic dosing.
L2370: There were no fatal AEs in SPRINT. SAEs occurred in 23% of patients including 11% who experienced selumetinib-related SAEs. AEs leading to permanent discontinuation occurred in 12%, and approximately one third of patients required dose reduction for an AE. Grade 3-4 AEs occurred in 68% of patients including 43% of patients who experienced Grade 3-4 AEs
L2371: considered at least possibly related to selumetinib. The most common AEs (more than 30% of patients) in the pediatric pool included vomiting, rash, abdominal pain, diarrhea, nausea, dry skin, musculoskeletal pain, fatigue, pyrexia, dermatitis acneiform, stomatitis, headache, oropharyngeal pain, paronychia, pruritus, cough, dermatitis, constipation, nasal congestion, hair disorder, influenza like illness, and allergic rhinitis. Given the relatively low discontinuation rate over a fairly long treatment duration, it appears that AEs were tolerable and manageable with dose modifications and supportive care in pediatric patients treated with selumetinib.
L2372: Important risks of selumetinib are similar to those of other MEK inhibitors including ocular, cardiac, musculoskeletal, GI and dermatologic toxicities. Some rare, but serious, class-related safety signals (e.g., RVO, RPED, rhabdomyolysis, PPES) were not identified in the SPRINT pediatric data. Supportive adult data and additional pediatric data from the selumetinib expanded access program for PN and from externally-sponsored pediatric trials provided a larger sample size to evaluate less common adverse reactions. The review of these additional data sources suggests that selumetinib has a similar safety profile to commercially approved MEK inhibitors. The pediatric PN population under the labeled indication likely has similar risk for developing these class-related toxicities, and the product label attempts to mitigate this risk by including relevant warnings, precautions and dose modification guidelines.
L2373: As part of the overall risk assessment, the FDA additionally considered the benign nature of the NF1 PN indication and the intention for chronic dosing of selumetinib in pediatric patients. The recommended indication is restricted to patients with inoperable PN that is symptomatic. The patients in SPRINT had substantial PN-related morbidity and had long treatment durations with a low discontinuation rate suggesting that selumetinib at the recommended dose was reasonably safe and tolerable. The longer term safety profile of selumetinib for chronic dosing in a pediatric population will be further assessed through post-marketing requirements.
L2374: ## SUMMARY AND CONCLUSIONS
L2375: ## 8.3. Statistical Issues
L2376: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2377: While there were no major statistical issues with this application, there are some limitations to deriving inference from a small, non-randomized study. The response rate of patients treated with selumetinib varied by assessor (NCI central review vs. independent central review); however, regardless of assessor, the estimated response rate was considered clinically and statistically meaningful. Additionally, the responses are durable, but the study follow-up was not sufficiently mature to characterize median duration of response. Results for any other endpoints in the SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 trial are considered descriptive.
L2378: FDA considered the comparisons of efficacy endpoints in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 to the placebo arm of Study 01-C-0222 or the Natural History study to be exploratory, due to potential
L2379: between-study differences including patient eligibility criteria, assessment frequencies of endpoint, and endpoint definition. Furthermore, the lack of covariate information available for these external data limited the ability to compare data sources or to adjust for potential confounding or bias, and so the results of any comparisons are not interpretable.
L2380: ## 8.4. Conclusions and Recommendations
L2381: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2382: NF1 PN is a rare, benign tumor that can cause substantial morbidity for patients starting at a young age. Surgical intervention is common and typically based on progressive PN-related symptoms or functional impairment because it is rarely curative and regrowth is common. There are currently no FDA-approved systemic therapies for treatment of PN. The absence of functional neurofibromin and the resulting upregulation of RAS signaling of the RAF/MEK/ERK pathway in patients with NF1 provides a sound biologic rationale for MEK inhibition as a treatment approach for NF1 related tumors, including PN.
L2383: The clinical benefit of selumetinib for pediatric patients with inoperable and symptomatic NF1 PN is based on the results of the NCI SPRINT trial. Efficacy results from SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 demonstrated a confirmed ORR per REiNS criteria of 66% (95% CI: 51, 79) based upon NCI central review and 44% (95% CI: 30, 59) based upon independent central review (ICR). Among the 33 responding patients included in the NCI assessment, the median DOR was not reached and 82% had a DOR of at least 12 months. The observed ORRs in SPRINT Phase 1 [67% (95% CI: 45, 84)] and SPRINT Phase II Stratum 2 [68% (95% CI: 47, 85)] were consistent with the primary Phase II Stratum 1 results and similarly durable. Additionally, a natural history study of NF1 conducted by the NCI Pediatric Oncology Branch (POB) and submitted as part of the application demonstrated that a key characteristic of NF1 PN is the uncommon occurrence of spontaneous regression such that the observed tumor responses in SPRINT can be concluded to be the effect of selumetinib.
L2384: Clinical outcomes data were considered essential for demonstrating the clinical benefit of selumetinib, given the benign, albeit morbid, nature of NF1 PN. The SPRINT trial evaluated selumetinib effects on PN-related functional impairment, symptoms, and disfigurement via various COA measures and photographic evaluations that were conducted with scheduled tumor response assessments. Acknowledging the limitations of the single arm trial design and the relatively small sample sizes available for analysis of specific morbidities, the FDA observed a general trend toward an association between reduction in tumor volume and durable improvement in clinical outcomes during SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1.
L2385: The primary safety risks of selumetinib are consistent with the profiles of other approved MEK inhibitors. ADRs in pediatric patients receiving selumetinib were frequent and varied; however, most were low-grade and manageable with dose interruptions, reductions and supportive care. Selumetinib appears to be tolerable in patients with NF1 PN given the relatively low incidence
L2386: of SAEs in the context of a long duration of exposure for the patients who were treated in SPRINT. However, there is no evidence that pediatric patients are at less risk for uncommon, but serious, MEK class toxicity effects that were not identified in the pediatric pool; therefore, specific safety precautions, dose-modifications, and management guidelines are included in product labeling, and the marketing approval includes post-marketing requirements for long-term safety evaluations in pediatric patients.
L2387: In conclusion, the benefit:risk assessment for selumetinib is favorable, and the FDA review team recommends regular approval for selumetinib for the treatment of pediatric patients 2 years of age and older with NF1 who have symptomatic, inoperable PN at a dose of 25 mg/m2 administered twice daily on a continuous basis. Selumetinib has demonstrated statistically significant and clinically meaningful efficacy, including confirmed ORR that is durable and correlates with improvements in PN-related clinical morbidities. These results represent direct clinical benefit for pediatric patients with NF1 PN. The safety profile of selumetinib is consistent with other MEK inhibitors and is acceptable in view of the serious nature of inoperable PN.
L2388: The clinical reviewers do not recommend a REMS be implemented for selumetinib given the current safety profile and the experience of the medical community in managing expected MEK inhibitor ADRs. Long-term monitoring will be conducted via PMRs for safety, growth and development given the intended chronic use for pediatric patients. Risk management based on labeling and routine and enhanced pharmacovigilance will be employed to ensure the safe and effective use of selumetinib in patients with NF1 PN.

L2411: ## 12 Risk Evaluation and Mitigation Strategies (REMS)
L2412: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2413: The label includes listings of important adverse reactions that have been observed across the MEK inhibitor class as well as dose modifications and management guidelines for these events. Prescribers are increasingly experienced with monitoring and treating expected MEK inhibitor safety effects given recent approvals of this drug class, and oncologists are generally experienced in the monitoring and management of serious risks.
L2414: The FDA does not recommend a REMS be implemented for selumetinib given the current safety profile and the experience of the medical community in managing expected MEK inhibitor related ADRs.
L2415: A plan for enhanced pharmacovigilance is include as part of postmarketing requirements for selumetinib, as follows:
L2416: *"For a period of 5 years from the U.S. approval date, submit all cases reported of adverse events of special interest (AESIs), including ocular toxicity events including but not limited to retinal pigment epithelial detachment (RPED), retinal vein occlusion (RVO), cardiac toxicity events (including but not limited to ventricular dysfunction), muscle toxicity events (including but not limited to rhabdomyolysis), gastrointestinal toxicity events (including but not limited to colitis, ileus, intestinal obstruction, and intestinal perforation) with serious outcomes in pediatric patients receiving Koselugo® (selumetinib) for the treatment of NF-1 as 15-day Alert reports [as described under 21 CFR 314.80(c)(1)], and provide detailed analyses of these AESIs reported from clinical study, and post-marketing reports in your periodic safety report [i.e., the Periodic Adverse Drug Experience Report (PADER) required under 21 CFR 314.80(c)(2) or the ICH E2C Periodic Benefit-Risk Evaluation Report (PBRER) format]. These analyses should show cumulative data relative to the date of approval of Koselugo® (selumetinib) as well as relative to prior periodic safety reports. These analyses should include case narratives, with the following information in a table format: demographics, predisposing risk factors/comorbidities, signs, symptoms and relevant laboratory data leading to diagnosis, rechallenge/dechallenge information. Medical literature reviews for case reports/case series of these AESIs reported with Koselugo® (selumetinib) should also be provided in the periodic safety report."*
L2417: The applicant has agreed to provide the information requested for the time requested in a document submitted by email on March 13, 2020, with a plan for an official submission to the NDA on March 17, 2020.
L2418: ## 13 Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment
L2419: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2420: The following postmarketing requirements, reviewed and agreed to by the Applicant, will be included in the action letter for this NDA:
L2421: ## FDA PMR #1
L2422: Characterize and evaluate the long-term safety effects and any potential for serious adverse risks of selumetinib on the growth and development of pediatric patients. Submit the complete final report and long-term follow-up data from pediatric patients enrolled on SPRINT and ongoing or completed studies of selumetinib.
L2423: All patients must be evaluated for growth and development milestones annually for at least 7 years from initiation of selumetinib. Evaluations must include: growth as measured by weight, height, height velocity, height standard deviation scores (SDS), age at thelarche (females), age at adrenarche (males), age at menarche (females), and Tanner Stage progression. Descriptive statistics (including mean and standard deviation values) of on-study data for growth velocity must be presented. Growth velocity during the trial should be compared with growth velocity at baseline (if pre-baseline data are available). Provide analyses of height and weight data that assess measures of central tendency and outlier analyses using height and weight z-scores.
L2424: ## PMR Schedule Milestones
L2425: **Trial Completion: September 2025**
L2426: **Final Report Submission: March 2026**
L2427: ## FDA PMR #2
L2428: Characterize and evaluate the long-term safety effects and any potential for specific serious adverse risks of selumetinib in pediatric patients. Submit the complete final report and long-term follow-up safety data (minimum of 7 years) from pediatric patients enrolled on SPRINT and all other ongoing or completed studies of selumetinib to include an analysis of the following toxicities in pediatric patients: ocular toxicity (including but not limited to retinal pigment epithelial detachment and retinal vein occlusion), cardiac toxicity (including but not limited to ventricular dysfunction), muscle toxicity (including but not limited to rhabdomyolysis and symptomatic and asymptomatic CPK elevation), serious gastrointestinal toxicity (including but not limited to colitis, ileus, intestinal obstruction, and intestinal perforation), and serious dermatologic toxicity.
L2429: ## PMR Schedule Milestones
L2430: **Trial Completion: September 2025**
L2431: **Final Report Submission: March 2026**
L2432: ## **FDA PMR #3**
L2433: Submit the final report and datasets from a pharmacokinetic trial in pediatric patients to confirm the effect of a low-fat meal on selumetinib exposure with the marketed capsule formulation and evaluate whether administration of selumetinib with food may alleviate gastrointestinal toxicities. Confirm appropriate dosing recommendation of selumetinib with a low-fat meal that maintains efficacy with acceptable safety.

L2532: ## 19.4. OCP Appendices (Technical documents supporting OCP recommendations)
L2533: ## The FDA's Assessment:
L2534: ## 19.4.1. Summary of Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
L2535: ## Were relevant metabolite concentrations measured in the clinical pharmacology and biopharmaceutics studies?
L2536: Yes. Plasma and urine concentrations of the active parent drug selumetinib and its active N-desmethyl metabolite were measured in the clinical pharmacology and biopharmaceutics studies.
L2537: ## For all moieties measured, is free, bound, or total measured? What is the basis for that decision, if any, and is it appropriate?
L2538: Total plasma concentrations of selumetinib and its N-desmethyl metabolite were measured in all studies. The unbound plasma concentration of selumetinib was measured in both the hepatic impairment study (D153200082) and renal impairment study (D1532C00081). In both of the organ impairment studies, a validated LC-MS/MS analytical method was available for measuring the concentration of unbound moieties in human plasma ultrafiltrate to evaluate protein binding. In the renal impairment study, plasma protein binding in the ESRD group (99.4%) was ~35% lower than patients with normal renal function (99.6%). In the hepatic impairment study, the fraction unbound for selumetinib was similar in normal, mild, and moderate groups: 0.00343, 0.00277, 0.00295, respectively, while the severe hepatic impairment group had a higher unbound ratio of 0.00691.
L2539: ## **What bioanalytical methods are used to assess concentrations?**
L2540: Bioanalytical methods in human matrices for the determination of selumetinib (AZD6244, AR142886) and its active N-desmethyl metabolite were initially developed and validated on behalf of Array Biopharma (Colorado USA) and AstraZeneca at [REDACTED] (b) (4)
L2541: Table 59 summarizes the bioanalytical methods validated and used to quantify selumetinib and its N-desmethyl metabolite. Overall, the precision, accuracy, selectivity and performance of the methods used to analyze selumetinib and metabolite in plasma, plasma ultrafiltrate, and urine were acceptable. See Table 60 below for accuracy and precision summary data and additional stability data for the two analytes.

L2556: ## 19.4.2. PK and PD Assessments
L2557: ## Clinical Pharmacokinetics
L2558: The clinical PK of selumetinib and its N-desmethyl metabolite in pediatric patients with NF1 and inoperable PN who were 3 to 18 years old at enrollment was evaluated in the SPRINT study. The SPRINT study included Phase I dose identification across 3 doses levels 20, 25 and 30 mg/m2 in 24 pediatric patients, and Phase 2 pivotal trial in 50 pediatric patients at dose level 25 mg/m2. Blue capsules (25 mg dose strength) in combination with white capsules (10 mg dose strength) were used to make up to the nearest BSA-based dose. Table 61 and Table 62 represent selumetinib and its N-desmethyl metabolite PK parameters in pediatric patients after single dose.

L2584: ## Metabolism
L2585: The relative contribution to selumetinib turnover by CYP and UGT enzymes was assessed in cryopreserved human hepatocytes in Study BE000021-21, using specific CYP enzyme inhibitors for 3A4 (ketoconazole), 2C19 (ticlopidine), a pan-CYP inhibitor (1-aminobenzotriazole) and a pan-UGT inhibitor (salicylamide). Results indicated that CYP mediated metabolism contributes 56% to the overall metabolism of selumetinib and UGT metabolism contributes 29%.
L2586: Study BS0001696-61 was performed to assess the relative contribution of the major CYP isoform involved in the metabolism of selumetinib using recombinant human CYPs (rhCYP) heterologously expressed in baculovirus infected insect cells at 2 and 5 µM selumetinib and 1 µM N-desmethyl selumetinib. Results showed that CYP3A4 accounts for over 85% of selumetinib metabolism in vitro with lesser contributions from CYPs 3A5, 2C19, 2C9, 2E1 and 2D6. The N-desmethyl metabolite formation is predominantly mediated by CYP2C19 with lesser contributions from CYP1A2, 2A6 and 2C9. About 93% of CYP mediated metabolism of N-desmethyl selumetinib is catalyzed by CYP3A4. Moreover, Study KMN012 was also conducted to investigate the metabolism of selumetinib in HLM and heterologously expressed human CYP isoforms. Results indicated that CYP1A2, 2C9 and 2C19 were all capable of N-desmethylation of selumetinib.
L2587: Study KMN045 was performed to assess the relative contribution of the major UGT isoform involved in the metabolism of selumetinib by incubations with a range of expressed UGT enzymes in vitro. Results indicated that selumetinib metabolism by hepatic UGT enzymes were extensive and mediated by UGT1A1 and UGT1A3.
L2588: ## *Intrinsic Factors*
L2589: The effect of **ethnicity** on exposure to selumetinib was assessed using pooled data taken from a specific ethnicity study in Asian subjects (Study 86) including Chinese, Japanese, Filipino, Malay, Malaysian, Maldivian, Singaporean, Thai, Vietnamese, Indian and Pakistani, and 9 clinical pharmacology studies in Western subjects (White and Black ethnicities) pooled from Studies 66, 69, 71, 78, 80, 81, 82, 83 and 85. Selumetinib doses were used as 25, 35, 50 and 75 mg single dose in these studies. Table 66 below lists AUC and C_{max} for ethnic comparisons. It is found that after BW or BSA normalization, the differences of mean PK exposure (AUC and C_{max}) between Japanese and Western subjects were reduced from approximately 1.6-1.8-fold higher to approximately 1.4- to 1.7-fold higher. Further comparison of selumetinib PK in Japanese, Chinese, and Western subjects (not shown here) revealed that BW or BSA based dose has brought a general overlap of distribution of individual AUC or C_{max} among Chinese, Japanese, and Western subjects.

L2599: The **Renal Impairment** study was conducted with a single oral 50 mg selumetinib dose (capsule) in adult subjects with normal renal function and in subjects with end stage renal disease (ESRD) in Study 81. Table 67 shows selumetinib PK exposure in the study. Results showed that both
L2600: selumetinib exposure and plasma protein binding were lower in the ESRD group, leading to a similar unbound selumetinib exposure. In the ESRD group, selumetinib was administered to subjects with ESRD both before and after a dialysis session to investigate whether dialysis would make a change of selumetinib PK. Results showed that total selumetinib exposure was slightly lower (~ 17% for AUC and 30% for C_{max}) when dosed before dialysis than after dialysis, however exposure was highly variable and a decrease of &gt;15% was only observed in 7 out of 12 subjects. Similar results were found for the N-desmethyl metabolite. The data suggest that dialysis have minimal effect on the removal of systemic selumetinib and N-desmethyl selumetinib.

L2664: Additionally, the Applicant calculated exposure matrices with consideration of exposure and potency of each active moiety, i.e., total and unbound selumetinib and N-desmethyl metabolite respectively as listed in Table 69, based on the following equations:
L2665: \text{Total moiety AUC} = \text{AUC}_{\text{parent}}/\text{MW}_{\text{parent}} + \text{AUC}_{\text{met}}/\text{MW}_{\text{met}}
L2666: \text{Adjusted total moiety AUC} = \text{AUC}_{\text{parent}}/\text{MW}_{\text{parent}} \times \frac{1}{\text{IC}_{50\text{parent}}} + \text{AUC}_{\text{met}}/\text{MW}_{\text{met}} \times \frac{1}{\text{IC}_{50\text{met}}}
L2667: \text{Adjusted unbound moiety AUC} = \text{AUC}_{\text{parent}}/\text{MW}_{\text{parent}} \times \frac{f_{u\text{parent}}}{\text{IC}_{50\text{u, parent}}} + \text{AUC}_{\text{met}}/\text{MW}_{\text{met}} \times \frac{f_{u\text{met}}}{\text{IC}_{50\text{u, met}}}
L2668: \text{MW}_{\text{parent}} = 457.7 \text{ g/mol}; \text{MW}_{\text{met}} = 443.7 \text{ g/mol};
L2669: In vitro unbound fraction: 0.016 (parent) and 0.021 (metabolite);
L2670: \text{IC}_{50} (parent) = 5.308 nM; \text{IC}_{50}(metabolite) = 1.561 nM, based on average of 6 cell lines in GSR 2.2.2.6.2.

L2678: ## In Vitro Studies
L2679: The potential inhibitory effect of selumetinib (0.05 to 50 µM) on the major human hepatic cytochrome P450 enzyme activities was evaluated in pooled human liver microsomes. Selumetinib showed little or no inhibitory effect against any of these CYP enzymes to a clinically relevant extent.

L2691: The potential induction of selumetinib on CYP450 enzymes were assessed in human hepatocytes. Selumetinib induction of CYP1A and CYP3A were only observed at the higher concentrations tested (10 and 30 \muM, 5 to 15-fold higher than Cmax after 75 mg dose). No significant induction of activity was observed for CYP2C9 in donor I (&lt;10% of rifampicin response in donor I). Selumetinib showed no induction of CYP1A2 mRNA or CYP2B6 mRNA expression or enzyme activity at concentrations up to 100 \muM.
L2692: Following co-incubation of UGT1A1 and UGT2B7 in HLM with selumetinib at concentrations up to 100 \muM, weak inhibition of enzyme activity was observed with IC50 &gt;100 \muM.
L2693: The effect of selumetinib on the inhibition of the hepatic transporters OCT1, OATP1B1 and OATP1B3 was investigated in vitro (making the assumption that IC50=Ki).

L2702: An in vitro study using the MDCKII-MDR1 cell system indicated that selumetinib (tested at 1, 10, 25 and 50 \muM) is a substrate of MDR1 (P-gp). The MDCKII-corrected efflux ratio (ER) was between 6.1 to 12.7.
L2703: The potential inhibitory effect of N-desmethyl selumetinib was assessed with individual human CYP isoforms in vitro using selective marker substrates. N-desmethyl selumetinib showed minor inhibition of CYP2C8, 2C9, 2C19, 2D6 and 2E1, and was found to be a direct inhibitor of CYP1A2 and CYP3A4 (testosterone), with IC50 values of 18.9 \muM and 26.3 \muM for CYP1A2 and CYP3A4 (testosterone) respectively. The results suggested a minimal inhibition effect on CYP1A2 and 3A4 substrates at steady state by using the estimated free plasma concentration (28.5 ng/mL) from available clinical data (total Cmax 1.5 \mug/mL).
L2704: ## Clinical Pharmacodynamics
L2705: Given selumetinib is a MEK1/2 inhibitor and ERK is the substrate of MEK, measuring the inhibition of phosphorylation of ERK following selumetinib administration is considered an indirect measure of MEK inhibition by selumetinib, as assessed in Study 05 at 4 time points post single dose administration in both Part A (Day 1) and Part B (Days 1 and 8) in all patients in adult patients with advanced malignancies. There is a direct relationship between selumetinib plasma concentration and the inhibition of pERK, with the highest inhibition occurring at the higher selumetinib plasma concentrations.
L2706: **Figure 19 pERK inhibition versus selumetinib plasma concentration in Study 05 in adult patients**
L2707: Source: Figure 13, Clinical Study Report D1532C00005.
L2708: The potential contribution from the active N-desmethyl metabolite, which is ~7% of parent drug plasma concentration but 3-5 fold potent than selumetinib was further considered. Therefore, plasma concentration data for selumetinib and N-desmethyl selumetinib were combined to provide a plasma concentration for the active moiety, with the metabolite concentrations increased with a weighting to account for an increase in *in vitro* potency of 3-fold compared to parent. An E_{max} model was used to describe the relationship between active moiety concentrations and pERK inhibition.
L2709: **Figure 20 Relationship between pERK inhibition and plasma concentration of selumetinib active moiety**
L2710: Source: GSR summary of clinical pharmacology.
L2711: ## 19.4.3. Population PK and Exposure-Response Analysis
L2712: ## Key Review Questions
L2713: 1. Is the proposed dosing regimen acceptable for patients with lowest BSA (0.55-0.69 m^2)?
L2714: No. New dose recommendation of morning dose 20 mg and evening dose 10 mg for this subgroup of patients. Sponsor proposed
L2715: We recommend morning dose of 20 mg and evening dose 10 mg for this subgroup and it could bring AUC comparable with other groups (Figure 21). Notably, the two individuals in clinical trial has 0.67 m^2 BSA received 20mg BID dosing regimen instead of
L2716: Figure 21 Plot of Patients AUC Across BSA Bands
L2717: Source: Reviewer's independent analysis.
L2718: ## 2. Exposure-response analysis relationship for efficacy.
L2719: The final E-R analysis dataset for ORR included 68 subjects in the NCI central analysis dataset and 50 subjects in the independent central review dataset. NCI central analysis dataset included patient data from both Phase I and Phase II Stratum 1 studies, and independent central review only analyzed efficacy data from patients in the Phase II study.
L2720: Binomial logistic regression was further performed on AUCs of selumetinib versus response to assess if there is any significant E-R relationship in the NCI central analysis dataset (Figure 22 Left), as well as independent central review dataset (Figure 22 Right). The logistic regression suggested there was no significant exposure-ORR relationship in the NCI central analysis dataset (p-value of coefficient: 0.203), while a barely significant exposure-ORR relationship was found in the independent central review dataset (p-value of 0.0428).
L2721: **Figure 22** Binomial logistic regression on AUCss of selumetinib (Left: NCI central analysis. Right: Independent central review)
L2722: Source: figure 13 of Pop-PK report.
L2723: **Reviewer's comment:** The Applicant's ER analysis is acceptable. Exposure-response assessment demonstrated a positive trend for AUCss-efficacy (ORR) association. The exposure-response assessment should be interpreted with caution because of the limitation of the study design (e.g., a single dose level pivotal trial with a small number of subjects and a high dose interruption rate).
L2724: ## 3. What is the exposure response relationship for safety?
L2725: No clear E-R relationship between selumetinib and/or N-desmethyl metabolite exposure and 13 relevant AEs (rash acneiform, rash non-acneiform, nail disorders, oral mucositis, leukopenic events, thrombocytopenic events, erythropenic events, LVEF reduction, CK elevation, nausea, vomiting, diarrhoea and hepatic events).
L2726: No clear E-R relationship between exposure and AEs leading to discontinuation, dose interruption or reduction.
L2727: **Reviewer's comment:** The Applicant's ER analysis for safety is acceptable.
L2728: ## 4. Population PK model
L2729: The PK of selumetinib was well-described by a two-compartment model with first-order elimination and sequential zero- and first-order delayed absorption. A one-compartment model reasonably described the N-desmethyl metabolite plasma concentrations over time.
L2730: For all covariates evaluated, BSA had an impact on CL, V2, Fm/V4 and V3. AGE and RACE were also found to have a minor impact on selumetinib PK, while only BSA had a &gt;20% impact on CL, which is considered clinically relevant. None of other covariates including formulation, gender,
L2731: CrCL, bilirubin, albumin, ALT, has any significant impact on selumetinib and/or N-desmethyl metabolite PK.
L2732: There was no relevant difference in PK between patients and healthy subjects.

L2737: **Reviewer's comment:** The Applicant's population PK model is acceptable. The recommended BSA-based dosing is supported by the clinically relevant BSA effect on PK.
L2738: ## 19.4.4. Physiologically based Pharmacokinetic Modeling Review
L2739: ## Executive Summary
L2740: The objective of this review is to evaluate the adequacy of the Applicant's PBPK report, entitled "PBPK Modeling Report-MS-02" to predict the effects of 1) CYP3A inhibitors (erythromycin and diltiazem), 2) a CYP2C19 inhibitor (fluoxetine) and 3) CYP3A inducers (efavirenz and dexamethasone) on the PK of selumetinib.
L2741: The Division of Pharmacometrics has reviewed the submitted PBPK reports, supporting modeling files, and the Applicant's responses to FDA's information requests (IRs) submitted on October 30, 2019 and December 19, 2019. FDA reviewer conducted additional PBPK analyses and concluded the followings.
L2742: - The PBPK analyses describe the observed selumetinib PK profiles following a single intravenous infusion (0.08 mg, 0.25hr), a single oral dose administration (25 and 75 mg) and multiple oral dose administration (25, 50, and 75 mg, bid).
- The analyses are adequate to predict the effect of erythromycin and diltiazem on the PK of selumetinib in healthy subjects. The model predicts an approximate 40% or 30% increase in selumetinib AUC when coadministered with erythromycin or diltiazem, respectively.
- The analyses are adequate to predict the effect of efavirenz and dexamethasone on the PK of selumetinib in healthy subjects. The model predicts an approximate 40% reduction in selumetinib AUC when coadministered with efavirenz, while no significant change in selumetinib exposure was predicted when coadministered with dexamethasone.
L2743: ## Applicant's PBPK Modeling Effort
L2744: ## PBPK software
L2745: Simcyp V17 (Simcyp Ltd, UK) was used to develop the Applicant's selumetinib PBPK models and predicted the effects of CYP3A inhibitors (erythromycin and diltiazem), a CYP2C19 inhibitor (fluoxetine) and CYP3A inducers (efavirenz and dexamethasone) on the PK of selumetinib.
L2746: ## Model development
L2747: ## Selumetinib
L2748: The first order absorption model was used. In vitro and in silico dataset (such as MDCKII assay) were used to predict the effective passive intestinal permeability (Peff), fraction absorbed (fa), absorption rate constant (ka) and nominal flow in gut (Qgut). The predicted Peff, fa and Qgut were 6.88 \times 10^{-4} cm/s, 0.99 and 15.0 L/h, respectively. The Applicant assigned the fugut (unbound fraction in enterocytes) value to be 1.0. A minimal PBPK model with a single adjusting compartment (SAC) was applied to capture the distribution phase of selumetinib PK profile. For the SAC compartment, inter-compartment clearance (Q, 8L/h) was predicted using Pop PK model and the apparent volume associated with the SAC (Vsac, 1 L/kg) was estimated based on
L2749: the observed selumetinib Cmax and PK profile (Study 83). The predicted volume of distribution (Vss) was 1.37 L/kg using the method 2 in Simcyp (Rodgers and Rowland method).
L2750: The Applicant reported the average oral clearance of selumetinib (CLpo) is 19.6 L/hour based on 9 clinical studies. The percentages of selumetinib metabolized by CYP3A4 and CYP2C19 were assigned as 25% and 15% according to the DDI study with itraconazole and fluconazole, respectively. The intrinsic clearance values of 0.568 and 3.335 \mul/min/mol were estimated for CYP3A4 and CYP2C19, respectively. The rest of the metabolism was assigned as the additional human liver microsome clearance (186.8 \mul/min/mg). Renal elimination was not considered in the model because less than 1% of the dose eliminated as unchanged drug in urine (Study YAU169).
L2751: In-vitro data showed that selumetinib is not a perpetrator of enzyme- or transporter- mediated pathways at clinically relevant concentrations of selumetinib. Selumetinib is a substrate of P-gp with low affinity. P-gp mediated drug efflux was not incorporated in the selumetinib PBPK model.
L2752: ## Victim drug models
L2753: The default PBPK models of erythromycin, diltiazem, fluoxetine, efavirenz, and dexamethasone in SimCYP were used without any modification for DDI prediction.
L2754: ## FDA's assessment
L2755: - The mass balance study showed that unchanged selumetinib accounted for about 20% of the dose in the feces. However, the fa value for selumetinib in Applicant's model was 0.99 and no biliary secretion was considered in the model. An information request was issued requesting the Applicant to provide justification for a fa value of 0.99 derived in the model.
- Selumetinib is highly bound to plasma protein with an unbound fraction of 0.016 in plasma. The fugut was set to 1 in Applicant's model which may increase the selumetinib extraction across the intestine, and thus the estimated fmCYP3A would be lower than that estimated using a model with a lower fugut. An information request was issued requesting the Applicant to evaluate the effect of fugut on the predicted selumetinib PK and exposure change of selumetinib with itraconazole, fluconazole or rifampicin.
- The Applicant's estimated fmCYP3A4 and fmCYP2C19 for selumetinib were 0.25 and 0.15 according to the DDI study results with itraconazole and fluconazole, respectively. An information request was issued to suggest the Applicant to re-estimate the fmCYP3A4 and fmCYP2C19 by considering the AUC metabolite (N-desmethyl selumetinib) to parent drug ratio.
- An information request was issued requesting the Applicant to verify model with selumetinib PK data following both intravenous (Study D1532C00080) and oral administration.
L2756: - In vitro study indicated that selumetinib is a P-gp substrate with a low affinity. P-gp mediated drug efflux was not incorporated in the selumetinib PBPK model. An information request was issued requesting the Applicant to provide justification or the simulated result to indicate the effect of P-gp is negligible.
L2757: ## Applicant's response to FDA's IR and FDA's assessment
L2758: - Fraction absorbed (fa)
L2759: In the response to the FDA's Information Request, the Applicant stated that a fa value of 0.99 was assigned according to the preclinical and clinical studies as summarized below:
L2760: There is no glucuronide conjugate in rat feces after intravenous or oral administration of selumetinib, but the glucuronide conjugate is the major component in the bile following bile-duct cannulation in rats. This result suggests that the glucuronide conjugate is likely converted back to the parent drug in the GI tract, which is thereafter being reabsorbed and return to the systemic circulation. In human, 20% of unchanged selumetinib was excreted in feces while no direct glucuronide conjugates were detected. The Applicant concluded that the unchanged selumetinib observed in human feces may be from the deconjugation of the glucuronide conjugates in GI tract, thus a high fa value (0.99) was assigned in the Applicant's PBPK model.
L2761: Reviewer note: it remains unclear how much unchanged selumetinib in feces was due to the deconjugation of the glucuronide conjugate and how much was from the drug remaining unabsorbed in the GI tract after oral administration. Thus, the FDA reviewer discussed the scenario where selumetinib absorption was not complete. Refer to 'FDA's reviewer's analysis'.
L2762: - Unbound fraction in enterocyte (fugut)
L2763: Fugut value is not routinely measured, and it is often assigned by the modeler using different assumption or in silico methods. When a smaller fugut value was assigned in the model, more drug would pass through the gut wall without metabolism. Therefore, the estimated fmCYP would be different using different fugut values for those enzymes which are present in both intestine and liver. In the response to the FDA's Information Request, the Applicant discussed a scenario where fugut was assumed to equal fup (0.016). FDA reviewer conducted additional analysis to evaluate the alternative modeling approaches to assign fugut value(s) in the model. Refer to 'FDA's reviewer's analysis'.
L2764: - fmCYP2C19
L2765: In the response to the FDA's Information Request, the Applicant re-assigned fmCYP2C19 (0.07) to the value reported for the AUC ratio of metabolite (N-desmethyl selumetinib) to parent drug in plasma (0.07). It was assumed that CYP2C19 is the only enzyme responsible
L2766: for the formation of N-desmethyl selumetinib and both selumetinib and N-desmethyl selumetinib have similar elimination rates.
L2767: ## o P-gp
L2768: In the response to the FDA's Information Request, the Applicant developed a full PBPK model with ADAM absorption and incorporated P-gp kinetics in the intestine and liver. The in vitro estimated P-gp Jmax (9.9 pmol/min) and Km (15.8 \muM) values was used as initial values in the model development and a sensitivity analysis was performed. The sensitivity analysis suggests that the impact of P-gp on the PK of selumetinib is likely to be negligible. The Applicant's modeling analysis is adequate to assess the impact of P-gp on selumetinib PK.
L2769: ## FDA reviewer's analysis
L2770: Given the limitations identified in Applicant's modeling approach, the FDA's reviewer further refined the model by re-optimizing the model parameters to better capture the clinical DDI study results with itraconazole, fluconazole, and rifampicin.
L2771: The model refinement was based on the liver availability (Fh) and absolute bioavailability (F). Fh can be calculated from the following equation,
L2772: F_h = 1 - E_h = 1 - \frac{CL_{h,p}}{Q_h \cdot B/P}
L2773: Where CLh,p is the plasma clearance of selumetinib following intravenous administration which can be obtained from Study 80 (11.4 L/h); Qh is the blood flow in the hepatic portal vein (90 L/h); B/P is the concentration ratio of drug in blood to plasma (0.625); Eh is the fraction of the drug that is extracted by the liver. The Fh was calculated to be 0.797. The observed absolute bioavailability in Study 80 was 0.621. Thus, the lowest possible fa is 0.8 given a F value of 0.621 and Fh is around 0.8.

## Koselugo (selumetinib)
L2777: Two scenarios were discussed regarding the different f_a values:
L2778: 1) **Model 1**: assume complete oral absorption (f_a = 1); and
L2779: 2) **Model 2**: assume incomplete oral absorption (f_a \sim 0.8)
L2780: As shown in Table 77, F_g can be calculated based on the known F, F_h, and the assumed f_a. The corresponding fugut, f_mCYP3A4 f_mCYP2C19 in Model 1 and Model 2 can be derived based on the clinical DDI result with itraconazole and fluconazole. The AUC ratio of metabolite (N-desmethyl selumetinib) to parent drug was also considered. Both models were verified with the observed selumetinib PK following intravenous and oral administration (Table 74). It should be noted that the model predicted DDI of selumetinib with rifampicin was overestimated as compared to the clinical observed results (AUCR: 0.39 vs 0.49) (Table 76). Rifampicin is a pleiotropic inducer. It may interact with multiple pathways which could affect the exposure of selumetinib, whereas the rifampicin model used in this analysis may not account for all the interaction mechanisms. The model verification results were shown in Figure 24 and Table 78. After model verification with the clinical PK and DDI data, Model 1 and Model 2 were applied to assess the DDI potential of selumetinib as a victim with CYP3A4 and CYP2C19 modulators.

L2791: ## **PBPK model application**
L2792: The developed PBPK models were used to simulate the DDI for selumetinib in the following scenarios.
L2793: - To predict the effect of erythromycin (a moderate CYP3A inhibitor) and diltiazem (a moderate CYP3A inhibitor) on the PK of selumetinib in healthy subjects.
- To predict the effect of fluoxetine (a strong CYP2C19 inhibitor) on the PK of selumetinib in healthy subjects.
- To predict the effect of efavirenz (a moderate CYP3A inducer) and dexamethasone (a weak CYP3A inducer) on the PK of selumetinib in healthy subjects.
L2794: ## **Results**
L2795: ## **1. Can FDA refined selumetinib PBPK models describe selumetinib PK in healthy subjects?**
L2796: Yes. The refined selumetinib models (Model 1 and Model 2) were able to capture the observed selumetinib PK following a single intravenous infusion (0.08 mg, 0.25hr) and a single oral dose administration (25 and 75mg) in healthy subjects and multiple dose administration (25, 50 and 75mg, bid) in patients with advanced solid malignancies. The model predicted selumetinib bioavailability after oral dosing is similar to the observed absolute bioavailability (0.62). The potential effect of uncertainty associated with the fraction absorbed (fa) on the selumetinib PK prediction was also explored using Model 1 and 2. No significant difference was found in the selumetinib PK prediction using Model 1 and Model 2 (Table 77 and Table 78).
L2797: ## **2. Can FDA refined selumetinib PBPK models predict the effect of erythromycin (a moderate CYP3A inhibitor) and diltiazem (a moderate CYP3A inhibitor) on the PK of selumetinib in healthy subjects?**
L2798: Yes. The models (Model 1 and Model 2) are adequate to predict the effect of erythromycin and diltiazem on the PK of selumetinib in healthy subjects. About 40% or 30% increase in selumetinib AUC were predicted with erythromycin or diltiazem, respectively (Table 79).
L2799: **Table 70 Model predicted selumetinib Cmax and AUC ratios in the presence and absence of a CYP3A modulator**
L2800: CYP3A modulator	Model 1	Model 2
CmaxR	AUCR	CmaxR	AUCR
Erythromycin (a moderate CYP3A inhibitor), 500 mg, TID	1.16	1.39	1.17	1.35
Diltiazem (a moderate CYP3A inhibitor), 60 mg, TID	1.12	1.28	1.14	1.28
Efavirenz (a moderate CYP3A4 inducer), 600 mg, QD	0.79	0.56	0.80	0.61
Dexamethasone (a weak CYP 3A4 inducer), 8 mg, QD	1.00	1.00	1.00	1.00
L2801: ## **3. Can FDA refined selumetinib PBPK models predict the effect of fluoxetine (a strong CYP2C19 inhibitor) on the PK of selumetinib in healthy subjects?**
L2802: The predicted selumetinib AUC was increased about 35% with fluoxetine. Based on the model analysis, about 10% increase in the selumetinib AUC can be potentially attributed to the fluoxetine mediated CYP2C19 inhibition, whereas about 20% increase in the selumetinib AUC was due to the fluoxetine mediated CYP3A4 inhibition.
L2803: The default fluoxetine PBPK model in SimCYP incorporated both fluoxetine and its metabolite norfluoxetine mediated competitive inhibition and time dependent inhibition of CYP3A4 based on in vitro study results1,2 and model was verified using clinical DDI study results3. The clinical DDI study4 showed that alprazolam (a moderate sensitive CYP3A4 substrate) AUC was increased by 32% after co-administration of fluoxetine. However, Sager et al5 recently reported that fluoxetine had no effect on midazolam AUC (a sensitive CYP3A4 substrate) in vivo. The magnitude of fluoxetine mediated CYP3A4 inhibition remained to be confirmed in future studies. Therefore, the simulated DDI effects of fluoxetine on selumetinib exposure was not used to support the dosing recommendation.
L2804: ## 4. Can FDA refined selumetinib PBPK models predict the effect of efavirenz (a moderate CYP3A inducer) and dexamethasone (a CYP3A inducer) on the PK of selumetinib in healthy subjects?
L2805: Yes. The models (Model 1 and 2) are adequate to predict the effect of efavirenz and dexamethasone on the PK of selumetinib in healthy subjects. About 40% reduction in selumetinib AUC was predicted with efavirenz. The selumetinib exposure would not be expected to be significantly affected by concomitant dexamethasone (Table 78).
L2806: ## Conclusion:
L2807: The Applicant's selumetinib model was refined by the FDA reviewer to explore the alternative PBPK parameters (such as fmCYPs and fa) and their impacts on DDI prediction. The selumetinib PBPK model described the observed DDI of selumetinib with itraconazole (a strong CYP3A4 inhibitor) and fluconazole (a strong CYP2C19 inhibitor and a moderate CYP3A4 inhibitor). The PBPK analyses are adequate to predict the effect of various CYP3A and CYP2C19 modulators on the selumetinib PK.
L2808: 1 Mayhew BS, Jones DR, Hall SD. An in vitro model for predicting in vivo inhibition of cytochrome P450 3A4 by metabolic intermediate complex formation. Drug Metab Dispos. 2000 Sep;28(9):1031-7.
L2809: 2 Albaugh DR, Fullenwider CL, Fisher MB, Hutzler JM. Time-dependent inhibition and estimation of CYP3A clinical pharmacokinetic drug-drug interactions using plated human cell systems. Drug Metab Dispos. 2012 Jul;40(7):1336-44.
L2810: 3 Hall J, Naranjo CA, Sproule BA, Herrmann N. Pharmacokinetic and pharmacodynamic evaluation of the inhibition of alprazolam by citalopram and fluoxetine. J Clin Psychopharmacol. 2003 Aug;23(4):349-57.
L2811: 4 Hall J, Naranjo CA, Sproule BA, Herrmann N. Pharmacokinetic and pharmacodynamic evaluation of the inhibition of alprazolam by citalopram and fluoxetine. J Clin Psychopharmacol. 2003 Aug;23(4):349-57.

L2833: ## 1 Summary
L2834: This review evaluates statistically the data of the 2-year oral carcinogenicity study in Han Wistar CRL: WI(HAN) rats and 6-months oral carcinogenicity study in CByB6F1/TgrasH2 transgenic mice. The review analyzes the dose-response relationship of tumor incidences and mortality (including tumor-related mortality). From the statistical point of view, the review concludes that AZD6244 had no effect on survival. The tumor analysis showed no statistically significant dose-response relationship or pairwise increase in tumor incidence for either sex in rats or mice.
L2835: **Rat Study:** Rats (50/sex/dose) were dosed by oral gavage with AZD6244 daily for up to 104 weeks. The respective AZD6244 dose in the water control (WC), vehicle control (VC), low (LD), mid (MD), and high-dose (HD) groups were 0, 0, 0.25, 0.8, or 2.5 mg/kg/day in male and 0, 0, 0.1, 0.3, or 1 mg/kg/day in females. The treatments for all rats were terminated at Week 104.
L2836: The survival analyses didn't show any statistically significant dose response relationship or pairwise difference in mortality in males and females. The respective survival rates in the WC, VC, LD, MD, or HD groups at the time they were terminated were 64%, 60%, 70%, 66%, or 70% in males and 76%, 72%, 78%, 76%, or 72% in females.
L2837: The tumor analysis showed that there were no statistically significant positive dose-response relationships or statistically significant pairwise increase against the vehicle control or water control for any tumor types tested.
L2838: **Mouse Study:** Mice (25/sex/dose) were dosed by oral gavage with AZD6244 daily for up to 26 weeks. The respective AZD6244 doses in the vehicle control (VC), low (LD), mid (MD), and high-dose (HD) groups were 0, 6, 16, or 50 mg/kg/day in males and females. The 50 mg/kg/day level was reduced to 30 mg/kg/day beginning on Week 19 due to excessive mortality. Also, a positive control group consisting of 15 males and 15 females was dosed intraperitoneally with 7.5 mg/mL N-Nitrosomethylurea (NMU) in citrate-buffered saline on Day 1 only. The treatments for male mice and female mice were terminated at Weeks 27, respectively.
L2839: The survival analyses showed a statistically significant dose response relationship in male mice only. Mortalities in male HD group and positive control groups for male and female were significantly higher than that of the vehicle or water control group. The respective survival rates in the WC, VC, LD, MD, HD, or PC groups at the time they were terminated were 100%, 100%, 100%, 100%, 88%, or 13% in males and 100%, 100%, 100%, 96%, 100%, or 20% in females.
L2840: The tumor analysis showed that no AZD6244-related effects on the incidence of neoplastic findings were noted for male and female mice. The incidence rates of tumor types of malignant lymphoma in hemolymphoreticular tissue, papilloma in skin and stomach in male and female mice were statistically significant higher in the positive control than those in the vehicle control.
L2841: ## 2 Background
L2842: Selumetinib (AZD6244) is a potent, selective, oral, inhibitor of mitogen-activated protein kinase kinases 1 and 2 (MEK1/2) that is not competitive with respect to adenosine triphosphate (ATP). This application is for the use of selumetinib as an oral treatment for paediatric patients aged 3 years and above, with neurofibromatosis type 1 (NF1) and symptomatic, inoperable plexiform neurofibromas (PN). EstraZeneca (the sponsor) submitted the nonclinical studies 501094-3855cr: "A 104-week oral carcinogenicity study in Wistar rats" and 20086561-0994cm: "A 26-week oral carcinogenicity study in mice" via submission NDA 213756/SN-0001. The electronic tumor.xpt files were submitted at the same time.
L2843: The phrase "dose response relationship" refers to the linear component of the effect of treatment, and not necessarily to a strictly increasing or decreasing mortality or tumor incidence rate as dose increases. The dose unit "mg/kg/day" will be referred to "mkd" in the tumor tables. Results of this review have been discussed with the nonclinical team.
L2844: ## 3 Rat Study- 501094-3855cr
L2845: **Study Report:** 501094-3855cr-report.pdf (statistical report on page 3919)
L2846: **SAS data:** tumor.xpt
L2847: This study was conducted to evaluate the carcinogenic potential of the test article, AZD6244, after once daily oral gavage administration to male and female Han Wistar CRL: WI(HAN) rats for up to 104 consecutive weeks. The test material was administered at doses of 0.25, 0.8, or 2.5 mg/kg/day for males and 0.1, 0.3, or 1 mg/kg/day for females. This review refers these dose groups as the low (LD), mid (MD), or high (HD) dose groups, respectively. Two groups of the same size were used as controls. One control group was dosed with Milli-Q water and the other was dosed with the vehicle, 10% ethanol, 30% PEG 400 and 60% Phosal 50. This review refers these two group as vehicle control (VC) and water control (WC). There were 50 rats/sex/dose in the main study. All rats were terminated at Week 104.
L2848: Pairwise comparisons were performed against Vehicle Control. The following pairwise comparisons were performed: VC group vs WC group and VC group vs dosed groups.
L2849: Assessment of toxicity was based on dose analysis, morbidity, mortality, injury, body weight, food consumption, clinical observations and masses, ophthalmology, clinical pathology, toxicokinetics, macroscopic observations, and microscopic evaluations.
L2850: ## 3.1 Sponsor's Analyses
L2851: ## 3.1.1 *Survival Analysis*
L2852: Intercurrent mortality data were analyzed using the Kaplan-Meier product-limit method. An overall test comparing all groups was conducted using a log-rank test12. Any animal with accidental injury that caused death or unscheduled sacrifice was censored in the estimation. In addition, all animals still alive at the end of the experimental period were censored at the following day. If this overall test was significant (p &lt; 0.05) and there were more than two
L2853: File Name: NDA213756Carcin.doc
L2854: groups, then a follow up analysis was done where each treatment group was compared to the control group using a log-rank test. Results of all pair-wise comparisons are reported at the 0.05 and 0.01 significance levels. All endpoints were analyzed using two-tailed tests.
L2855: **Sponsor's concluded results:** There were no statistically significant differences (Kaplan-Meier) in mortality between any of each of the water control group and the treated groups and the vehicle control for males or females.
L2856: ## 3.1.2 *Tumor Data Analysis*
L2857: Neoplastic findings classified as fatal and incidental were processed using the Peto death rate method and the Peto prevalence method, respectively. The processing of incidental tumors was done by creating a single separate interval for the time following the experimental period (terminal sacrifice period) and by dividing the experimental period into the following fixed intervals [FDA's draft Guidance for industry. 2001]: weeks 1-52, weeks 53-78, weeks 79-92, and over week 92, and over week 92. Using the derived outcomes from the processing of both fatal and incidental tumors, a test statistic was built to perform a global survival-adjusted trend test on tumor data observed in a "mortality dependent" context [Peto et al, 1980].
L2858: All p-values are reported using upper-tailed test, unless otherwise indicated. Evaluation criteria (levels of significance) were applied differently for rare tumors (background rate of 1% or less) and common tumors (background rate greater than 1%) The evaluation criteria from the FDA are given in Table F (FDA).
L2859: Table F. Evaluation Criteria for Common and Rare Tumors
Test for Positive Trends	Control-High Pair-wise Comparisons
Common and rare tumors will be tested at 0.005 and 0.025 significance levels, respectively	Common and rare tumors will be tested at 0.01 and 0.05 significance levels, respectively
L2860: **Sponsor's concluded results:** There were no neoplastic or proliferative findings attributable to administration of AZD6244.
L2861: ## 3.2 *Reviewer's Analyses*
L2862: To verify the sponsor's analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing pharmacologist, this review analyzed the SAS data sets of these studies received on 7/26/2019 via NDA 213756/SN0001.
L2863: ## 3.2.1 *Survival Analysis*
L2864: The survival distributions of rats in all treatment groups were estimated using the Kaplan-Meier product limit method. For control, low, medium, and high dose groups, the dose response
L2865: relationship was tested using the likelihood ratio test and the homogeneity of survival distributions were tested using the log-rank test. The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates are given in Figures 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. The intercurrent mortality data are given in Tables 1A and 1B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively. Results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals, are given in Tables 3A and 3B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L2866: **Reviewer's findings:** This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers (percents) of death that occurred prior to termination of the group were 20 (40%), 15 (30%), 12 (34%), 15 (30%), or 18 (36%) in male rats and 14 (28%), 11 (22%), 12 (24%), 14 (28%), or 12 (24%) in female rats in the VC, LD, MD, HD, or WC groups, respectively. The survival analyses didn't show any statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality in males and females.
L2867: ## 3.2.2 *Tumor Data Analysis*
L2868: The tumor data were analyzed for dose response relationships and pairwise comparisons of control group with each of the treated groups. Both the dose response relationship tests and pairwise comparisons were performed using the Poly-k method described in the papers of Bailar and Portier [2] and Bieler and Williams [3]. In this method an animal that lives the full study period (w_{\max}) or dies before the terminal sacrifice but develops the tumor type being tested gets a score of s_h = 1. An animal that dies at week w_h without developing the tumor before the end of the study gets a score of s_h = \left(\frac{w_h}{w_{\max}}\right)^k &lt; 1. The adjusted group size is defined as \sum s_h. As an interpretation, an animal with score s_h = 1 can be considered as a whole animal while an animal with score s_h &lt; 1 can be considered as a partial animal. The adjusted group size \sum s_h is equal to N (the original group size) if all animals live up to the end of the study or if each animal that dies before the terminal sacrifice develops at least one tumor of the tumor type being tested, otherwise the adjusted group size is less than N. These adjusted group sizes are then used for the dose response relationship (or the pairwise) tests using the Cochran-Armitage test. One critical point for Poly-k test is the choice of the appropriate value of k, which depends on the tumor incidence pattern with the increased dose. For long term 104-week standard rat and mouse studies, a value of k=3 is suggested in the literature. Hence, this reviewer used k=3 for the analysis of this data. For the calculation of p-values the exact permutation method was used.
L2869: The adjusted levels of significance for testing a positive dose response in the 2-year rat study are 0.005 and 0.025 for a common tumor and a rare tumor, respectively. The adjusted levels of significance for the pairwise comparison in the 2-year rat study are 0.01 and 0.05 for a common tumor and a rare tumor, respectively. A rare tumor is defined as one in which the tumor rate is less than 1% in the vehicle control group.
L2870: The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 5A and 5B in the appendix for male and female rats, respectively.
L2871: **Reviewer's findings:** There were no AZD6244-related effects on the incidence of neoplastic findings were noted.
L2872: ## 4 Mouse Study- 20086561-0994cm
L2873: **Study Report:** 20086561-0994cm-report.pdf (statistical report on page 1420)
L2874: **SAS data:** tumor.xpt
L2875: This study was conducted to evaluate the carcinogenic potential of the test article, AZD6244, after twice daily oral gavage administration to male and female CByB6F1/TgrasH2 transgenic mice for up to 26 consecutive weeks. The test material was administered at doses of 6, 16, or 50/30 mg/kg/day for male and female mice. This review refers these dose groups as the low (LD), mid (MD), and high (HD) dose groups, respectively. The 50 mg/kg/day level was reduced to 30 mg/kg/day beginning on Week 19 due to excessive mortality. There were 25 mice/sex/dose in the main study. Similarly constituted control groups were treated with reverse osmosis deionized (RODI) water or the control article alone (10% ethanol, 30% PEG 400, and 60% Phosal 50 PG). Also, a positive control group consisting of 15 males and 15 females was dosed intraperitoneally with 7.5 mg/mL N-Nitrosomethylurea (NMU) in citrate-buffered saline on Day 1 only. This review refers these controls as water control (WC), vehicle control (VC) or positive control (PC).

L2886: ## 4.2 *Reviewer's Analyses*
L2887: To verify the sponsor's analyses and to perform additional analyses suggested by the reviewing pharmacologist, this review analyzed the SAS data sets of these studies received on 7/26/2019 via NDA 213756/SN0001.
L2888: ## 4.2.1 *Survival Analysis*
L2889: The Kaplan-Meier curves for survival rates of all treatment groups are given in Figures 2A and 2B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively. The intercurrent mortality data of all treatment groups are given in Tables 2A and 2B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively. Results of the tests for dose response relationship and homogeneity of survivals for control, low, medium, and high dose groups are given in Tables 4A and 4B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively.
L2890: **Reviewer's findings:** This reviewer's analysis showed the numbers (percent) of death that occurred prior to termination of the group were 0, 0, 0, 0, 3 (12%) or 13 (87%) in male mice and 0, 0, 0, 1 (4%), 0, or 12 (80%) in female mice in the WC, VC, LD, MD, HD, or PC groups, respectively. The survival analyses show a statistically significant dose response relationship in mortality in males only. Mortalities in male HD group and positive control for male and female group were significantly higher than that of the vehicle or water control group.
L2891: ## 4.2.2 *Tumor Data Analysis*
L2892: The tumor data were analyzed for dose response relationships and pairwise comparisons of the water control group and the vehicle control group separately with each of the treated groups, the water control group, and the positive control group using the same method that was used for the rat study. The tumor rates and the p-values of the tested tumor types are listed in Tables 6A, and 6B in the appendix for male and female mice, respectively.
L2893: **Reviewer's findings:** Following table displays the tumor types showing p-values less than or equal to 0.05 for pairwise comparisons of vehicle control and positive control.

L2953: Graph showing MEK enzyme activity (Percent of Control) versus Concentration (nM) of Selumetinib. The activity decreases sigmoidally as the concentration increases, indicating inhibition.
L2954: Figure 2 displays two bar charts comparing Proximal Nerve Volume and Tumor Number between Vehicle and AZD6244 treated mice.
The left chart shows Proximal Nerve Volume (mm^3). Vehicle treated mice show a volume of approximately 0.9 mm^3. AZD6244 10mg/kg BID treated mice show a volume of approximately 0.6 mm^3. The difference is marked with an asterisk (*).
The right chart shows Tumor Number / mouse. Vehicle treated mice show a number of approximately 17 tumors/mouse. AZD6244 10mg/kg BID treated mice show a number of approximately 4 tumors/mouse. The difference is marked with double asterisks (**).
L2955: Figure 3 shows the inhibition of ERK phosphorylation by AZD6244 in Neurofibroma Tumors.
a. Western blot analysis comparing p-Erk1/2 and \beta-actin levels in Vehicle and AZD6244 10mg/kg treated mice. The blot shows a clear reduction in p-Erk1/2 signal in the AZD6244 group compared to the Vehicle group.
b. Quantification of the Western blot results, showing the ratio of p-Erk1/2 (ADU) to \beta-actin. Vehicle treated mice show a ratio of approximately 1.0. AZD6244 10mg/kg treated mice show a ratio of approximately 0.4. The difference is marked with an asterisk (*).
L2956: Box plot showing simulated steady state AUC0-12h (ng * h/mL) across various Body Surface Area (BSA) bands. The Y-axis ranges from 0 to 5000. The X-axis shows BSA bands: 0.55-0.69 m^2, 0.70-0.89 m^2, 0.90-1.09 m^2, 1.1-1.29 m^2, 1.3-1.49 m^2, 1.5-1.69 m^2, 1.7-1.89 m^2, and 1.9-2.2 m^2. The AUC0-12h values generally increase with increasing BSA, with the lowest values observed in the 0.55-0.69 m^2 band.

The X-axis shows Target PN Status at Baseline: Progressive (hatched), Non-Progressive (diagonal hatching), and Unknown (green hatching). The plot shows a distribution of percentage changes, generally centered around -20% to -30%.

L2959: Kaplan-Meier Plot of Progression-Free Survival (PFS) comparing Placebo and Selumetinib 25 mg/m2 BID arms in SPRINT Phase II Stratum 1 (Progressive PN).
The X-axis represents Time from study treatment or placebo initiation (years), ranging from 0 to 4. The Y-axis represents Probability of Progression-Free Survival, ranging from 0 to 100.
The Placebo arm (solid red line) shows a rapid decline in PFS, reaching approximately 20% survival at 3 years. The Selumetinib arm (dashed purple line) maintains a high probability of PFS, remaining near 100% survival throughout the observed period (up to 4 years).
Censored observations are marked by open circles (O).

L2961: Kaplan-Meier Plot of Duration of Response (DoR) for Selumetinib 25 mg/m^2 BID. The Y-axis is Probability of remaining in response (0 to 100). It then remains stable until cycle 32, where it drops to about 85%. The legend indicates 'Selumetinib 25 mg/m^2 BID' and 'CENSORED' observations. L2975: Figure 22 displays two sets of plots comparing NCI central analysis (Left) and Independent central review (Right) results for binomial logistic regression on AUCss of selumetinib. Each set includes a top plot showing Probability of response versus AUCss of selumetinib (nM.h), and a bottom plot showing Dose (mg/m2) versus AUCss of selumetinib (nM.h). The top plots show a sigmoid curve fitted to the data points, indicating a positive trend for efficacy (ORR) association with increasing AUCss. The bottom plots show box plots of Dose versus AUCss, illustrating the relationship between exposure and dose.
L2976: Figure 24 displays observed (dots or circles) and simulated (lines) selumetinib plasma concentration-time profiles. The figure is organized into three rows of plots. The top row shows single dose administration (25 mg) in Study 83, comparing administration without and with itraconazole and fluconazole. The middle row shows single dose administration (75 mg) in Study 85, comparing administration without and with rifampicin, and a single intravenous infusion (0.08 mg, 0.25 hr) in Study 80. The bottom row shows multiple dose administration (25, 50, and 75 mg, bid) in Study 05, illustrating steady-state concentrations over time.
L2977: Figure 1A: Kaplan-Meier Survival Functions for Male Rats
This figure displays the Kaplan-Meier survival curves for male rats across five dose groups. The X-axis represents Survival time (week), ranging from 0 to 100. The Y-axis represents Survival Probability, ranging from 0.0 to 1.0. The legend indicates the dose groups:
0 | Vehicle Control (Blue line)
0.25 mg/kg/day | Low Dose (Red line)
0.8 mg/kg/day | Mid Dose (Green line)
2.5 mg/kg/day | High Dose (Brown line)
0 | Water Control (Purple line)
The survival curves for all groups start at a survival probability of 1.0. The Vehicle Control and Water Control groups maintain the highest survival probabilities throughout the study period, with the Water Control group showing slightly lower survival than the Vehicle Control group after approximately 60 weeks. The survival curves for the three dose groups (0.25 mg/kg/day, 0.8 mg/kg/day, and 2.5 mg/kg/day) are very close together and show a gradual decline in survival probability, indicating no significant dose-dependent effect on survival in male rats.
L2978: Figure 1B: Kaplan-Meier Survival Functions for Female Rats
This figure displays the Kaplan-Meier survival curves for female rats across five dose groups. The X-axis represents Survival time (week), ranging from 0 to 100. The Y-axis represents Survival Probability, ranging from 0.0 to 1.0. The legend indicates the dose groups:
0 | Vehicle Control (Blue line)
0.1 mg/kg/day | Low Dose (Red line)
0.3 mg/kg/day | Mid Dose (Green line)
1 mg/kg/day | High Dose (Brown line)
0 | Water Control (Purple line)
The survival curves for all groups start at a survival probability of 1.0. The Vehicle Control and Water Control groups maintain the highest survival probabilities throughout the study period. The survival curves for the three dose groups (0.1 mg/kg/day, 0.3 mg/kg/day, and 1 mg/kg/day) are very close together and show a gradual decline in survival probability, indicating no significant dose-dependent effect on survival in female rats.
